- REQ-95 THE daemon SHALL give each file its path, `old_path` for a rename, status, counts of added and deleted lines, `hash` and hunks of lines prefixed `+`, `-` or a space; a binary file SHALL have `binary` and no hunks, and a file the daemon did not read SHALL have `unreadable` (the reason) and no hunks.
- REQ-96 IF the diff of a scope passes 2 MiB, THEN the node SHALL have `too_large`, list at most 200 files by path and status with no hunks and no `seen`, and give the number of files left out in `omitted`; `set_viewed` on it SHALL be `unsupported`.
- REQ-97 WHILE a client has an `uncommitted` node open, the daemon SHALL read it again every 2 seconds and patch it when it changed; THE daemon SHALL NOT run git for a node nobody has open, beyond reading it once when it is made.
- REQ-98 WHEN `refresh_diff` names a `diff` or `commits` node, the daemon SHALL read it again at once; an unknown node SHALL be `not_found`.
- REQ-99 WHEN `open_commits` names a workspace in a git repository, the daemon SHALL reply with the id of a `commits` node listing at most 200 commits of `origin/<base>..HEAD` (of `HEAD` when there is no base), newest first, each with its id, short id, subject and commit time, and `more` when older ones exist.
- REQ-100 WHEN `set_viewed {node, path, viewed}` names a file of a `diff` node, the daemon SHALL record the file's current `hash` for that workspace and scope (or forget it) and patch every client that has the node open; a file with a recorded `hash` equal to its own SHALL carry `seen` `yes`, one with a different `hash` SHALL carry `seen` `changed`, and one never marked SHALL carry none.
- REQ-101 THE recorded marks SHALL be kept in `session.json` across a restart; the daemon SHALL drop those of a removed workspace, and those of a file that is no longer in an `uncommitted` or `general` diff.
- REQ-102 THE daemon SHALL remove a `diff` or `commits` node that no client opened within 60 seconds of making it, or that no client has had open for 60 seconds, and SHALL send `gone` for those of a removed workspace.
- REQ-103 THE daemon SHALL run `git` for these reads without a pager, colour, external diff or text conversion, and without git's optional locks, so a read never blocks the user's own git.
- REQ-104 WHEN `d` is pressed on the highlighted workspace, THE terminal client SHALL open the diff viewer over that workspace's panes (the sidebar stays), ask the daemon for its `uncommitted` node and watch it; WHEN `esc` or `q` is pressed, or the sidebar is clicked, it SHALL close the viewer and stop watching the nodes it opened. (Amended by N167: `d` opens the diff as a panel of the layout, REQ-728 to REQ-731.)
- REQ-105 THE viewer SHALL have the views Sin commitear, Commits and General (keys `1 2 3`, or a click on its tab), ask the daemon for each node once, and show how many files or commits each holds once it is known.
- REQ-106 WHILE the Commits view has no commit open, THE viewer SHALL list the commits (subject, short id, age) in the left column and `↵` SHALL open one; WHILE one is open, `( )` SHALL step to the previous or next commit and `h` SHALL go back to the list.
- REQ-107 THE viewer SHALL show the changed files as a tree on the left (a folder with a single folder in it as one row, `+ −` totals, a status letter M A D R ? and a mark when seen) and every file of the view in one scroll on the right, in the tree's order, with the current file's header pinned on top; a file that is seen SHALL be folded to its header.
- REQ-108 WHERE the body is 100 columns or wider, THE viewer SHALL draw changes side by side (a file that is new, untracked or deleted in one column), and below that in one column; `s` SHALL force one column, two, or go back to the width; WHERE the area is under 84 columns the tree SHALL be hidden and `tab` SHALL show it over the body.
- REQ-109 WHEN `v` is pressed, THE viewer SHALL send `set_viewed` for the current file (viewed unless it is already seen, then not) and, once the daemon has patched the node, go to the next file; `j`/`k` SHALL move between files, `]`/`[` between hunks, space and `b` by a page, `g`/`G` to the ends, and the wheel scroll the body.
- REQ-110 WHILE a view has no node yet THE viewer SHALL say it is reading; IF the daemon refuses or fails, THEN it SHALL show the daemon's message with the other views still reachable and `r` SHALL ask again; WHEN there is no base, no changes, or a diff over the limit, it SHALL say so (a diff over the limit lists names without counts).
- REQ-111 WHILE the `uncommitted` view is open THE viewer SHALL show `● en vivo`, and for 2.5 seconds after the daemon patches the node for a reason other than its own mark, `● actualizado`.
- REQ-112 THE viewer SHALL NOT put any control character of a diff, a path, a commit subject or an error message on the terminal: a tab becomes spaces and any other control character a dot.
- REQ-113 THE phone SHALL open the changes of a workspace from the `±` of its screen, on Sin commitear; a drop-down at the top SHALL change the workspace, and Back SHALL close the file, then the commit, then return to the workspace (REQ-218).
- REQ-114 THE phone SHALL show the views Sin commitear, Commits and General as tabs, ask the PC for each view's node once when first shown, and show the number of files or commits each holds once it is known (`!` when it failed).
- REQ-115 THE phone SHALL list the changed files as a tree (a folder with a single folder in it as one row, folding by tap, `+ −` totals, a status letter, a box at the right); tapping a file SHALL open it and tapping its box SHALL send `set_viewed` for it, unmarked when it is already seen.
- REQ-116 THE phone SHALL show a file as unified lines numbered from its hunks' starts, additions and deletions tinted and signed; a binary, unreadable or renamed file with no lines SHALL say so, and a file whose `seen` is `changed` SHALL carry the notice that it changed since it was looked at and a ring in place of the tick.
- REQ-117 WHILE the Commits view has no commit open THE phone SHALL list the commits (subject, short id, age); tapping one SHALL open its files with `‹ commits` and `◂ ▸` to the neighbouring commits.
- REQ-118 WHILE Sin commitear is open THE phone SHALL say `● en vivo` and how many files are seen, and for 2.5 seconds after the PC patches the node for a reason other than the phone's own mark it SHALL say `● actualizado`.
- REQ-119 WHILE a view has no node yet THE phone SHALL say it is reading; IF the PC refuses, fails or the link drops before it answers, THEN it SHALL show the message with `reintentar` and the other views reachable; WHEN there is no base, no changes or the diff is over the limit, it SHALL say so (names only, no counts, with the omitted number).
- REQ-120 WHEN the phone leaves the changes screen, it SHALL stop watching the nodes it opened (`close`), and a node it opened SHALL be opened again after the link comes back.
- REQ-121 THE phone SHALL tell the PC at `hello` that it reads `diff` and `commits` nodes; WHEN it has no PC (the fixtures), it SHALL serve the diff fixtures read-only.
- REQ-122 WHERE a pane's shell has no integration (an unsupported shell), the pane node SHALL carry no `blocks` and clients SHALL draw its screen.
- REQ-123 THE `blocks` node of a pane SHALL keep its id for as long as the pane lives.
- REQ-124 WHEN a pane is closed, or its workspace removed, the daemon SHALL send `gone` for its `blocks` node.
- REQ-125 THE daemon SHALL keep at most 4 MiB of block output per pane, by dropping the oldest blocks.
- REQ-126 WHERE a pane's shell is not zsh, bash or fish, the daemon SHALL start it unchanged.
- REQ-127 WHEN a client's editor stops a running program, it SHALL send `write` of `\u0003`.
- REQ-128 WHEN a pull request is opened or `main` is pushed, CI SHALL run `just fmt-check` (Linux), `just clippy` and `just test` on Linux and macOS with zsh and fish installed, and `just android-check` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`).
- REQ-129 WHEN the daemon starts an agent (`claude`, by `new_pane` or by resuming it), it SHALL pass it `--settings <state>/claude-hooks.json`, a file it writes at start whose hooks run `<jwn> hook <event>`; an agent started with `--settings` of its own SHALL keep them and get no second set.
- REQ-130 WHEN `agent_event` arrives for a pane, the daemon SHALL raise on it: `permission` for a `notification` of `permission_prompt`; `question` for a `notification` of `elicitation_dialog` or `agent_needs_input`, and for a `pre_tool_use` of `AskUserQuestion`; `plan` for a `pre_tool_use` of `ExitPlanMode`; `agent_done` for `stop`; and nothing for any other notification or tool; an unknown pane SHALL be `not_found`.
- REQ-131 WHEN `agent_event` of any kind but `notification` arrives, the daemon SHALL first end the pane's `permission`, `question`, `plan` and `agent_done` attentions.
- REQ-132 THE `jwn hook <event>` command SHALL send `agent_event` for the pane named by `JWN_PANE_ID` with the `notification_type` and `tool_name` of the JSON on its stdin, and SHALL exit 0 without a word whatever fails (no pane, no daemon, unreadable input, an event it does not know).
- REQ-133 WHEN an agent's program ends, the daemon SHALL end the `permission`, `question`, `plan` and `agent_done` attentions of its pane.
- REQ-134 WHILE a `plan` or a `question` is pending on a pane, a `notification` of `permission_prompt` SHALL NOT raise `permission` on it.
- REQ-135 WHERE `gh` is found (on the path, or named by `JWN_GH`), THE daemon SHALL look every 60 seconds at the newest pull request of the branch of each worktree workspace that has a pane, with `gh pr list --head <branch> --state all --limit 1 --json number,state,statusCheckRollup` run in the worktree, outside the hub's lock and waiting at most 10 seconds.
- REQ-136 WHEN that pull request is merged, the daemon SHALL raise `merged` on the workspace; WHEN it is open and a check ended in failure, timeout or start-up failure, or a commit status in failure or error, it SHALL raise `ci_failed`.
- REQ-137 THE daemon SHALL raise an attention from a pull request only when what it sees differs from the last time (the pull request, merged, failing), ending the `merged` and `ci_failed` it had raised before, so that one a person has seen does not return while nothing changes; WHEN the branch has no pull request, it SHALL end them.
- REQ-138 IF `gh` is missing, fails or does not answer in time, THEN the daemon SHALL keep what it had and say it once in its log.
- REQ-139 WHEN the person presses `x` at the panel level of the terminal client on a pane whose shell is idle (no program running in it and no block running), THE client SHALL send `close_pane` for it at once.
- REQ-140 WHEN the person presses `x` on a pane where a program or a block is running, THE client SHALL ask first, naming it, and send `close_pane` only on `y`; any other key SHALL cancel.
- REQ-141 WHEN a pane is closed, THE workspace SHALL stay, even with no panes left, and the terminal client SHALL move its focus to a neighbouring pane when there is one.
- REQ-142 WHEN the person presses `x` at the workspace level, THE terminal client SHALL ask, and on `y` send `remove_workspace` without `remove_worktree`.
- REQ-143 WHERE the workspace is a worktree, THE question of closing a workspace SHALL also offer `d`, to remove the worktree as well.
- REQ-144 WHEN the person chooses `d`, THE terminal client SHALL read the workspace's `uncommitted` changes afresh (the daemon returns a node it still holds from an earlier look without reading it again); WHERE they list any file, it SHALL show those files (path and status), and go on as REQ-284 says (N71; before it the worktree could not be removed and only `c` closed the workspace).
- REQ-145 WHERE the `uncommitted` changes list no file, THE terminal client SHALL ask for `y` and then send `remove_workspace` with `remove_worktree`.
- REQ-146 IF `git worktree remove` refuses a removal (changes that appeared since the client looked, for one), THEN the job SHALL fail with git's message (REQ-649), THE terminal client SHALL say `✗ no se pudo quitar <nombre>` in its bar and show the message in the `trabajos` tab, and the workspace and its worktree SHALL stay as they are; IF the daemon answers the request itself with an error (`conflict`, `bad_request`), THEN the client SHALL show it in a box (amended by N159: it was the reply of the removal).
- REQ-147 WHEN a worktree is removed, THE daemon SHALL leave its branch in place.
- REQ-148 WHEN the person chooses «cerrar el panel» in the sheet of a long press on a pane's tab (REQ-182), THE phone SHALL open a sheet naming the pane and saying what closing does, and send `close_pane` only on «cerrar el panel»; it SHALL ask whatever runs in the pane.
- REQ-149 THE phone SHALL NOT offer to close a workspace or remove a worktree, and the sheet SHALL say that is done on the PC.
- REQ-150 IF the PC answers `close_pane` with an error or the link drops before it answers, THEN THE phone SHALL show the message and keep the pane's tab.
- REQ-151 THE terminal client SHALL read `mode`, `dark` and `light` from `settings.json`; WHERE the file is missing, unreadable, or holds a value it does not know or a theme of the other kind, it SHALL use `system`, `one-dark` and `one-light` for that value and not fail.
- REQ-152 WHEN the person presses `,` in the terminal client, it SHALL show settings with the mode and only the themes of the kind in force (the dark ones while the mode is dark, or `system` on a dark machine; the light ones otherwise); choosing one SHALL set the choice of that kind, apply it at once and write `settings.json` whole and atomically.
- REQ-153 WHILE the terminal client runs, it SHALL read `settings.json` again at most every 2 seconds and repaint when the mode or a theme changed.
- REQ-154 WHILE the mode is `system`, THE terminal client SHALL follow the machine's dark or light appearance (macOS `AppleInterfaceStyle`), and SHALL be dark where it cannot tell (no `defaults` to ask, or not macOS); a machine that answers that the key is not there is in light mode.
- REQ-155 THE terminal client SHALL draw every colour it chooses itself (backgrounds, borders, selection, marks, diff tints) from the theme in force.
- REQ-156 THE ten palettes SHALL be defined once in `contract/themes.json`, each saying whether it is `dark` or `light`, and each client SHALL have a test that its own table equals it.
- REQ-157 THE phone SHALL have an `ajustes` screen, opened from the gear of Home (REQ-216), with the mode and only the themes of the kind in force, keep a choice for each kind, apply it at once, keep them on the phone across launches, and send nothing to the PC for it.
- REQ-158 WHILE the phone's mode is `system`, THE phone SHALL follow Android's dark or light setting.
- REQ-159 WHEN the person presses `m` in the terminal client, it SHALL show the phone box and, WHERE the link is off, ask the daemon to turn it on from a thread of its own, so that the client keeps working while the tunnel opens.
- REQ-160 WHEN the link is on, THE box SHALL show the QR of the link (dark modules on a light ground with a quiet zone), the link, the local address, and the tunnel's address or that there is no tunnel; WHERE the area is too small for the QR, it SHALL show the link without it and say so.
- REQ-161 WHEN `esc` is pressed in the box, THE client SHALL close it and leave the link on; WHEN `s` is pressed it SHALL send `mobile_stop` and close it; WHEN `c` is pressed it SHALL copy the link.
- REQ-162 WHEN a phone asks to pair, THE client SHALL open the box with the question (the phone's name and the code), unless another question is open, in which case it SHALL wait for that one to be answered and say in the status bar that a phone is asking.
- REQ-163 WHILE the pairing question is shown, THE client SHALL answer yes only to `y` pressed at least 0.8 seconds after the question appeared, and no to any other key.
- REQ-164 WHEN the question is answered, THE box SHALL go back to the link, say whether the phone was paired or refused, and show how many phones are paired.
- REQ-165 IF the link cannot be turned on, THEN THE box SHALL show the daemon's reason and any key SHALL close it; WHEN `m` is pressed again it SHALL try again.
- REQ-166 WHILE the link is on, THE sidebar SHALL say `● teléfono · m`.
- REQ-167 THE phone's field to type a command SHALL rise with the keyboard, so that it stays above it.
- REQ-168 WHILE a pane's document, or the `blocks` node it names, has not arrived, THE clients SHALL draw grey bars where its lines will be, and SHALL NOT draw its screen, which would show for a moment and give way to the blocks.
- REQ-169 THE terminal client SHALL work at one of three levels, the workspaces of the sidebar, the panels of the open workspace, or typing in a panel; WHILE at each the status bar SHALL say WORKSPACE, PANEL or TERMINAL with the keys of that level, and the border of the area where the person is SHALL be blue (the sidebar's, or the focused panel's).
- REQ-170 WHEN `↵` is pressed on a workspace, or its row is clicked, THE client SHALL open it at the panel level, with the panel last focused; WHEN `↵` is pressed at the panel level, or a panel is clicked, it SHALL start typing in the focused panel.
- REQ-171 WHILE the client is at the terminal level, every key SHALL go to the program, `esc` and the `ctrl` keys included, except `^␣`, which SHALL take the client to the panel level.
- REQ-172 WHEN `esc` is pressed at the panel level, THE client SHALL go to the workspace level and show the panels as they were; at the workspace level `esc` SHALL do nothing.
- REQ-173 WHILE at the workspace level, `j` and `k` and the wheel over the sidebar SHALL move the highlight; WHILE at the panel level, `h`, `j`, `k`, `l`, the arrows and `tab` SHALL focus another panel (REQ-291).
- REQ-174 WHEN `x` is pressed, THE client SHALL close what the level is about: the workspace at the workspace level (REQ-142), the panel at the panel level (REQ-139).
- REQ-175 WHILE at the panel level, `n` SHALL ask the daemon for a new panel; `f` SHALL show the focused panel over the whole area, hiding the others, and a second `f` or `esc` SHALL show them again.
- REQ-176 WHEN `?` is pressed at the workspace or the panel level, THE client SHALL show a box with the keys of that level and any key SHALL close it; at the terminal level `?` SHALL go to the program.
- REQ-177 THE contract SHALL have the commands `rename_workspace {workspace, name}` and `rename_pane {pane, name}`, with fixtures and in the Kotlin types.
- REQ-178 WHEN `rename_workspace` arrives for a worktree, THE daemon SHALL give the worktree that name, trimmed, of at most 80 characters and with no control character, and keep it across a restart; an empty name SHALL bring back the one it had (`repo: branch`); a worktree's folder and branch SHALL NOT be touched; IF the workspace is a folder or the root of a repository (a directory on disk), THEN the daemon SHALL refuse with `bad_request` and change nothing; an unknown workspace SHALL be `not_found` and a name that is not allowed `bad_request`.
- REQ-179 WHEN `rename_pane` arrives, THE daemon SHALL give the pane that name as its title, which SHALL stay whatever program runs in it and after a restart; an empty name SHALL bring back what runs in it, else its shell; the same refusals as REQ-178.
- REQ-180 WHEN `r` is pressed at the workspace level of the terminal client on a worktree, it SHALL ask for its name (on a folder or a repository's root it SHALL do nothing), and at the panel level for that of the focused panel, with what is shown as the text to edit; `↵` SHALL send it, `esc` SHALL change nothing, and a `ctrl` chord SHALL be no letter of the name; at the terminal level `r` goes to the program.
- REQ-181 WHERE a worktree has the name it got when it was made (`repo: branch`), THE terminal client SHALL show its branch; WHERE it has another, it SHALL show that.
- REQ-182 WHEN a pane's tab is long-pressed, THE phone SHALL open a sheet with «nombrar el panel» and «cerrar el panel»; tapping outside the sheet or Back SHALL close it, and the tab SHALL carry no icon.
- REQ-183 WHEN a workspace row of Home, or the title of the workspace's screen, is long-pressed, THE phone SHALL open the sheet of that kind of workspace (REQ-227).
- REQ-184 WHEN «renombrar» or «nombrar» is chosen, THE phone SHALL show a field with the name as it is shown; «guardar» SHALL send `rename_workspace` or `rename_pane` with what is typed (empty: the automatic name); IF the PC refuses or the link drops, THEN the phone SHALL show the message and keep the sheet.
- REQ-185 WHERE a worktree has the name it got when it was made (`repo: branch`), THE phone SHALL show `↳` and its branch in the list, the header and the avisos; WHERE it has another, it SHALL show that name.
- REQ-186 WHEN `jwn` is run with no arguments, or as `jwn tui`, and no daemon answers on the socket, THE client SHALL start one in the background, with its output in `<state>/daemon.log`, wait up to 5 seconds for it, and say where the log is when it does not come up; WHEN one answers, it SHALL start nothing.
- REQ-187 WHEN the client ends, or the terminal it ran in closes, THE daemon it started SHALL keep running, with every pane; `jwn stop` SHALL end it.
- REQ-188 WHEN `mobile_start` arrives and the PC has a private address, THE daemon SHALL answer at once with the link over that address and the pairing token, bring the tunnel up in the background, and say `pending` until it is up or has failed; WHERE it has no private address, it SHALL wait for the tunnel (up to 90 seconds) as before.
- REQ-189 WHEN the tunnel comes up, THE daemon SHALL tell the connected phones its host (`endpoints`, REQ-82), and `mobile_link` SHALL answer with the link that includes it and the same pairing token, a new one only when that token is spent or has expired.
- REQ-190 THE daemon SHALL keep the tunnel it started until the link is turned off (`mobile_stop`, or the daemon ending), however many times `mobile_start` is asked, and whichever clients come and go.
- REQ-191 WHEN the terminal client's box shows a link whose tunnel is still opening, it SHALL say so and keep the link over the network working; WHEN the tunnel is up it SHALL show its address and a QR of the link that includes it, without losing a pairing question that was open.
- REQ-192 THE `create_worktree` command SHALL take an optional `folder` that names the worktree's folder inside `<repo>-wt/`, and SHALL refuse with `bad_request` one that is empty, `.`, `..`, over 80 characters, or has a `/`, a `\` or a control character.
- REQ-193 WHEN `o` is pressed at the workspace or the panel level of the terminal client, it SHALL open a folder browser that starts in the parent folder of the project of the highlighted workspace (home when there is none), lists that folder's visible subfolders sorted without regard to case, marks those already open and those that are git repositories, and shows the highlighted one's path, whether it is a repository and what it holds.
- REQ-194 WHILE the browser is open, typing SHALL filter its folders by letters in order; `↑` `↓` `ctrl-n` `ctrl-p` SHALL move; `→` or `tab` SHALL go into the highlighted folder; `←`, or `⌫` with nothing typed, SHALL go up; `~` with nothing typed SHALL go home; `ctrl-u` SHALL clear what was typed; `esc` SHALL close it.
- REQ-195 WHERE what was typed is a name a folder may have and no folder there has it, THE browser SHALL offer a row that makes the folder.
- REQ-196 WHEN `↵` is pressed on a row, THE client SHALL open that folder as a workspace with `open_folder` and give it a shell with `new_pane`, making the folder first when it is the row that makes one, and open the workspace at the panel level once the session shows it; WHERE the folder is already open, it SHALL go to that workspace and ask the daemon nothing.
- REQ-197 WHEN `w` is pressed at the workspace or the panel level, THE client SHALL open a box with a name (the first free `ws-N`, replaced by the first key typed) and what it will make: the branch `feat/<name>`, the branch it starts from (the highlighted workspace's) and the folder `<repo>-wt/<name>`; WHERE the highlighted workspace is not in a git repository it SHALL say so and open nothing.
- REQ-198 IF the name is not lowercase letters, digits and dashes, or the worktree is already open, or its folder is there, or its branch exists, THEN THE box SHALL say which instead of what it will make, and `↵` SHALL do nothing.
- REQ-199 WHEN `↵` is pressed with a name that can be used, THE client SHALL send `create_worktree` with the branch, `base` and the folder, give the workspace it makes a shell with `new_pane`, and open it at the panel level once the session shows it; IF the daemon refuses, THEN it SHALL show the message.
- REQ-200 WHILE the browser or the box is open, every key and click SHALL be theirs; at the terminal level `o` and `w` SHALL go to the program.
- REQ-201 WHEN the title or the legend of Home's workspace list is long-pressed, or its `+` is tapped, THE phone SHALL open a sheet «workspace nuevo» offering to open a folder of the PC, and SHALL NOT offer a worktree there.
- REQ-202 THE `list_dir` command SHALL reply with the canonical `path`, its `parent` (none at `/`), the daemon's `home`, the subfolders as `dirs` sorted without regard to case, each with whether it holds a `.git`, and their `total`.
- REQ-203 THE `list_dir` result SHALL hold at most 500 `dirs`, and `total` SHALL count all of the folder's subfolders.
- REQ-204 THE `list_dir` command SHALL leave out the subfolders whose names start with a dot unless `hidden` is true.
- REQ-205 IF the path of `list_dir` is relative, is not a folder, is missing or cannot be read, THEN THE daemon SHALL reply `bad_request`, `bad_request`, `not_found` or `io` respectively.
- REQ-206 THE workspace summaries of the `session` node SHALL carry `project` whenever the workspace node does.
- REQ-207 WHEN the phone's folder browser comes to a folder, it SHALL list its subfolders marking those with a workspace open in the session and those that are repositories, offer the crumbs from `~` (or `/`), `..` up and a toggle for hidden folders, and say how many of how many when the PC cut the list.
- REQ-208 WHEN the dock of the folder browser is pressed on a folder with no workspace, THE phone SHALL send `open_folder` and then `new_pane`, and open the workspace as soon as the session shows it; WHERE a workspace is already there, it SHALL open that one and send nothing.
- REQ-209 IF the PC refuses to open a folder or to give it a shell, THEN THE browser SHALL show the PC's message and stay where it is.
- REQ-210 WHEN the phone's worktree sheet opens, it SHALL name the first free `ws-N` (not a folder of the repository's worktree folder, read with `list_dir`, nor a workspace open there; from N79 that folder is the `root` of the repository's settings, REQ-350) and show the branch, the branch it starts from (the one the repository is on) and the folder as REQ-349 says.
- REQ-211 IF the name is not lowercase letters, digits and dashes (at most 40, no leading dash), or is taken, THEN THE sheet SHALL say which instead of what it will make, and creating SHALL do nothing.
- REQ-212 WHEN `crear y abrir` is pressed with a name that can be used, THE phone SHALL send `create_worktree` with the branch, the `base` and the folder, then (from N79) `start_workspace` and not `new_pane` (REQ-351), and open the workspace as soon as the session shows it; IF the PC refuses, THEN THE sheet SHALL show its message and stay.
- REQ-213 WHERE the session has more than one repository, THE worktree sheet SHALL let the person choose it; the sheet of a repository's root SHALL offer a worktree of it (REQ-227).
- REQ-214 THE phone SHALL open on Home and SHALL have no bar at the bottom of its screens; Home's header SHALL carry `+` (REQ-201), a bell and a gear.
- REQ-215 THE bell SHALL carry the number of attentions waiting for the person that this phone has not seen, WHERE there are any; WHEN it is tapped, THE phone SHALL open Avisos, and Back or the arrow of its header SHALL return to Home.
- REQ-216 WHEN the gear is tapped, THE phone SHALL open Ajustes, and Back or the arrow of its header SHALL return to Home.
- REQ-217 WHERE the phone is connected to a PC it paired with, THE `ajustes` screen SHALL show that PC above the mode, with how many PCs the phone has paired, and tapping it SHALL open the list of PCs; WHEN Back is pressed in that list with a PC connected, THE phone SHALL return to Ajustes.
- REQ-218 THE phone's screen of changes SHALL have an arrow in its header that returns to the workspace the changes are of.
- REQ-219 THE `session` node SHALL list its workspaces with each repository's workspaces together, its root first and then its worktrees in the order they were opened, the repositories in the order their first workspace was opened, and the folders that are not repositories last.
- REQ-220 WHERE a worktree is not preceded by its repository's root or by another worktree of it, THE clients SHALL draw the repository's name as a heading above it.
- REQ-221 WHERE a folder that is not a repository is the first of them and something comes before it, THE clients SHALL draw `carpetas` as a heading above it.
- REQ-222 THE daemon SHALL give a folder workspace that is the root of a git repository `project` equal to its own path, in its summary and in its node, and SHALL leave `project` out of any other folder.
- REQ-223 THE terminal client SHALL draw a worktree's row four columns in, with `↳` before its dot, and the headings between groups as rows that are not highlighted, not moved onto by `j` and `k`, not numbered, and a click on which does nothing.
- REQ-224 THE phone SHALL draw each workspace's row as `docs/prototypes/mobile.html` does: its number, `●` (a root or a folder) or `↳` (a worktree, drawn further in than its root) lit when it has panes, the name over its detail, the tag `raíz` or `carpeta` (none on a worktree), the mark of its most urgent unseen attention at the right and, as a third line of its text under the detail, that attention (REQ-247); and SHALL mark the workspace opened last with a blue bar, and count in `workspaces · N` the workspaces and not the headings.
- REQ-225 (withdrawn) The disabled «worktree nuevo» of a folder that is not a repository: its sheet no longer offers a worktree at all (REQ-227).
- REQ-226 WHERE a folder has no `project` and a worktree names its path as its repository, THE clients SHALL treat the folder as that repository's root, so a daemon that does not say it still gets the list grouped.
- REQ-227 THE sheet of a long press on a workspace row of Home, or on the title of the workspace's screen, SHALL be one for each kind and be titled by it: a repository's root offers «worktree nuevo» and «worktrees existentes» (WHERE its repository is known, REQ-539) and «quitar el workspace»; a folder that is not a repository offers «quitar la carpeta» (neither is renamed: it is a directory on disk, N68); a worktree offers «renombrar el worktree», «worktrees existentes» (WHERE its repository is known), «cerrar el workspace» and «quitar el worktree».
- REQ-228 WHEN a removal is chosen in that sheet, THE phone SHALL open a sheet that names the workspace, says how many panes close and what stays, and SHALL send `remove_workspace` only on its button, with `remove_worktree` only for «quitar el worktree»; the sheet SHALL close when its button is pressed (REQ-661, amended by N159).
- REQ-229 IF the PC refuses a removal (git, for a worktree with changes), THEN the removal SHALL be a job that failed (REQ-649): THE phone SHALL say it in a notice, mark the row with `✗` until the job is dismissed and show the PC's message in the sheet of the job (REQ-659), and nothing SHALL be removed; an error the PC answers at once (`conflict`) SHALL be a notice (amended by N159: the sheet used to wait for the reply and show it).
- REQ-230 WHEN a removal succeeds from the screen of the workspace, THE phone SHALL go back to Home.
- REQ-231 THE phone's Home header SHALL carry a search icon that opens a sheet «workspaces por último uso» with a field and, for each workspace of the session, its dot (lit when it has panes), its name (`repo/branch` for a worktree), its path, its kind and the mark of its most urgent unseen attention.
- REQ-232 THE search SHALL list first the workspaces opened on this phone, the latest first, then the rest in the order of Home's list; WHILE a text that is not blank is typed it SHALL keep those whose name, branch, repository or path contain it without regard to case, and say «sin resultados» when none does.
- REQ-233 WHEN a row of the search is tapped, THE phone SHALL close the sheet and open that workspace.
- REQ-234 THE phone's pane tabs SHALL end with a fixed `+`; WHEN it is tapped, THE phone SHALL send `new_pane` for the workspace with no `program`, so the pane is a shell in the workspace's folder, and SHALL ignore taps on it while the answer or the pane is awaited.
- REQ-235 WHEN the PC has made the pane and the workspace's document lists it, THE phone SHALL select it; IF the PC refuses, THEN THE phone SHALL show its message under the tabs and let the person try again.
- REQ-236 WHILE the keyboard is up, WHEN the person taps anywhere on the phone's screen that is not a text field (or the composer, whose send goes with its field), THE phone SHALL take the focus from the field and put the keyboard away; a scroll or a drag SHALL NOT.
- REQ-237 THE phone's content SHALL rise with the keyboard on every screen, with no gap left where the navigation bar is, so a field and what is above it stay in view.
- REQ-238 THE phone's editor SHALL take the keyboard's enter key as a new line of what is typed, growing to five lines before it scrolls, and SHALL send the text, new lines included, followed by `\r` only from its send button (REQ-60).
- REQ-239 WHEN `N` is pressed at the panel level (or after `^␣` from the terminal level), THE terminal client SHALL open the menu «panel nuevo» over the panels with the shell first, then the listed programs, and the shell row highlighted; `n` SHALL still ask for a shell at once, with no menu.
- REQ-240 WHILE the menu is open, `j` `k` and the arrows SHALL move the highlight, `↵` or a click on a row SHALL start that row, a program's own letter SHALL start that program and `n` the shell, and `esc` or a click outside the menu SHALL close it; every other key, a `ctrl` chord and the wheel SHALL do nothing, and no key SHALL reach a pane.
- REQ-241 WHEN the menu starts a program, THE terminal client SHALL send `new_pane` for the highlighted workspace with the entry's `command` as `program`, and WHEN it starts the shell, with no `program`; IF the daemon refuses, THEN the client SHALL show its message.
- REQ-242 THE terminal client SHALL take the menu's programs from the `[[program]]` tables of `config.toml`, in the file's order, each with its `name`, its `command` and an optional `key`, and SHALL never write the file.
- REQ-243 WHERE `config.toml` is missing or has no `[[program]]`, THE terminal client SHALL offer `claude` (`command = ["claude"]`, key `c`); WHERE it has entries, it SHALL offer those and no others.
- REQ-244 THE terminal client SHALL give an entry no key when its `key` is not one lowercase letter, is `j`, `k` or `n`, or is already an earlier entry's; the entry SHALL stay in the menu.
- REQ-245 IF `config.toml` cannot be read, is not TOML, or has an entry with no `name` or an empty or missing `command`, THEN THE terminal client SHALL keep the list it had (the built-in one before any good reading) and SHALL show in the menu the reason with the line of the error or of the entry's `[[program]]`; WHEN a later reading has no fault, THE message SHALL go.
- REQ-246 WHILE the terminal client runs, it SHALL read `config.toml` again at most every 2 seconds, so an edit shows in the menu without a restart.
- REQ-247 THE phone SHALL draw the line that says an attention, in a Home row and in Avisos, in the colour of what it asks: yellow for a decision (permission, question, plan), red for a failure (CI failed, a command that exited with an error) and cyan for something to look at (agent done, a command that ended well, merged, waiting on the screen); the text alone, with no background, bar or border.
- REQ-248 THE phone SHALL NOT draw a go control («ir ›») in Home or Avisos: a tap on the line of attention in a Home row, or on an Avisos item, SHALL open the node it names (REQ-34); a tap on the rest of a Home row SHALL open the workspace; a long press on the line SHALL give the row's menu as on the rest of the row.
- REQ-249 THE legend under the Home list SHALL name the three colours of REQ-247 («te espera», «falló», «por revisar»), each in its own colour.
- REQ-250 WHEN the shell reports a command that names a program that takes the screen (claude, nvim, htop, … `procs::takes_screen`), THE daemon SHALL put the pane's `blocks` node in mode `full` in the same patch that adds the block, without waiting for the look at the foreground; a program that an alias or a script started SHALL still be found by that look.
- REQ-251 WHEN the shell reports that the running command ended, THE daemon SHALL put the node back in mode `blocks` in the same patch that marks the block ended, even while the foreground still names the program; WHILE no command is reported running, the foreground SHALL NOT make the mode `full`.
- REQ-844 WHEN a pane is started with a program (the shell runs it with `-c`, REQ-371 and `launch.rs`), THE daemon SHALL NOT give the pane a `blocks` node from the start: the pane is its screen (no `blocks` node) until the shell reports its first prompt or command, which is when the program ends and the shell that takes over reaches its prompt, and from then it SHALL have the `blocks` node as any pane of an integrated shell; WHEN a pane is started with no program, THE daemon SHALL give it the `blocks` node from the start, as before.
- REQ-845 THE rule of REQ-844 SHALL NOT change what a client draws for a pane with a `conversation` (the conversation is drawn whatever its `blocks` node), for a shell that has no integration (never a `blocks` node), or for a pane that comes back after a restart with its saved blocks (REQ-298 to REQ-300), which has them from the start.
- REQ-252 WHEN the shell reports the end of its prompt (OSC 133;B), THE daemon SHALL note the column the cursor is in on the pane's screen, and WHILE it holds that column, THE pane node SHALL carry `input` (`text`, `cursor`): the text from that column of the first row of the wrapped line the cursor is in, with the rows a wrap split joined and the cursor's place in it in characters.
- REQ-253 WHEN a prompt is about to be drawn (OSC 133;A) or a command starts or ends, THE daemon SHALL drop the noted column, so that `input` is absent while a command runs and until the next prompt ends, and for a shell that does not report its prompt.
- REQ-254 WHILE a shell pane is drawn as blocks, THE terminal client SHALL draw under the list a band of its own colour with room around it (a free row, the chips, a free row, the line, a free row), the chips as pills (the folder and, when it has one, `⎇ branch`; while a command runs, `◌` and the command in yellow), and the line as a green `❯`, the text of `input` and a blue `➤` at its right.
- REQ-255 WHILE no text is typed and no command runs, THE field SHALL say «escribe un comando»; WHILE a command runs, it SHALL say «va al programa» and a red `■ ^C` SHALL stand beside the `➤`.
- REQ-256 WHEN the text is longer than a row of the field, THE terminal client SHALL wrap it inside the band, which SHALL grow up to the limit of REQ-703 and keep in view the rows that hold the cursor (REQ-704), and the list of blocks SHALL give those rows up.
- REQ-257 WHEN `■ ^C` is clicked, THE terminal client SHALL write `\u0003` to the pane; WHEN `➤` is clicked, it SHALL write `\r`.
- REQ-258 WHERE the pane carries no `input` and no command runs, THE terminal client SHALL show in the field the line of the pane's screen the cursor is on.
- REQ-259 WHILE a pane's `blocks` node is in mode `full`, its last block runs, that block is not `full` (it does not use the alternate screen, as claude does not) and its `out` has nothing but blanks, THE client SHALL draw the pane as starting, not as its screen: a spinner and «iniciando <program>…» (the file name of the command's first word), with `^C cancela` in the terminal and `toca ■ para cancelar` on the phone.
- REQ-260 WHEN a row of that block's `out` has more than blanks in it, THE client SHALL draw the pane's screen as it is, and SHALL NOT draw the pane as starting again for that command, even if its `out` is blank again.
- REQ-261 WHEN a program that has the screen has painted nothing 20 seconds after its block started, THE client SHALL draw the pane's screen as it is.
- REQ-262 WHILE a command runs in a pane whose node is in mode `full`, THE phone's composer SHALL show the stop mark `■` that writes `\u0003`, as it does in mode `blocks` (REQ-67).
- REQ-263 WHILE the program in a pane's terminal is on the alternate screen, THE pane's `screen` SHALL carry `alt: true`; otherwise `alt` SHALL be absent.
- REQ-264 WHILE a pane's `blocks` node is in mode `full`, its last block runs and is `full` (the command used the alternate screen) and the pane's `screen` has no `alt` (the program left it), THE terminal client SHALL draw the pane as closing, not as its screen: the spinner and «cerrando <program>…» with «esperando a que termine», for any program, the name being the file name of the command's first word.
- REQ-265 WHEN a program has stayed off the alternate screen for 3 seconds since this client saw it leave, and its command still runs, THE terminal client SHALL draw the pane's screen as it is; WHEN it is back on the alternate screen the count SHALL start again from the next time it leaves.
- REQ-266 WHEN a program writes a query to its terminal in a pane's output, THE daemon SHALL answer it on the pane's input as a terminal does: the primary and secondary device attributes (`CSI c`, `CSI > c`), the device status (`CSI 5 n`) and the cursor's place (`CSI 6 n`, with the cursor as it was where the query stood in the output), the terminal's name and version (`CSI > q`), the keyboard enhancements that are on (`CSI ? u`, none), the state of a mode (`CSI Ps $ p`, `CSI ? Ps $ p`, always «not recognised») and the size of the text area (`CSI 18 t`).
- REQ-267 THE daemon SHALL NOT answer the colour queries (OSC 4, 10, 11, 12), nor read a query inside a string (OSC, DCS, APC); a query split between two reads of the output SHALL be answered once.
- REQ-268 THE daemon SHALL write its answers on a thread of its own, so that reading a pane's output never waits for its input.
- REQ-269 THE terminal client SHALL read a node's document again only when its epoch or its revision is not the one it read last, and SHALL drop what the client no longer holds.
- REQ-270 WHEN a batch of output events holds several for one pane, THE daemon SHALL read that pane's screen and blocks once, at the last of them.
- REQ-271 WHEN a list in a node loses items at its front and gains items at its end, THE patch of the daemon SHALL say it as the removals of the first items and the additions at the end (up to 8 items dropped at once), with whatever else changed said as usual; applying the patch SHALL give the new document whatever the lists hold.
- REQ-272 THE terminal client SHALL build the lines of only those blocks that reach the rows it shows, and SHALL take the height of the list from counting, so that drawing a pane costs what it shows and not its history.
- REQ-273 WHEN a command whose text is `clear`, alone, ends with status 0, THE daemon SHALL drop every block of the pane, that one included, and tell its clients; a `clear` with arguments or that fails SHALL be a block like any other; the ids that follow SHALL go on and none SHALL be reused.
- REQ-274 THE contract SHALL have the command `sync_workspace {workspace}`, with a fixture and in the Kotlin types; its result SHALL be `{branch, pushed, new_branch}`.
- REQ-275 WHEN `sync_workspace` arrives for a worktree that has a branch, THE daemon SHALL push that branch, without force and without merging anything, with `git push` when the branch has an upstream and with `git push --set-upstream origin <branch>` when it has none; `pushed` SHALL be the commits that went up (those ahead of the upstream, or those no remote has) and `new_branch` SHALL say whether the branch had no upstream.
- REQ-276 WHEN the branch has an upstream and no commit ahead of it, THE daemon SHALL push nothing and answer `pushed` 0.
- REQ-277 IF git refuses the push, THEN THE daemon SHALL answer `git_failed` with git's own message and change nothing; IF the workspace is a folder, a repository's root or a worktree with no branch, THEN it SHALL answer `bad_request`; IF it is unknown, `not_found`; AND it SHALL NOT hold the hub's lock while git runs.
- REQ-278 WHEN `s` is pressed at the workspace or the panel level of the terminal client on a worktree, THE client SHALL send `sync_workspace` over a connection of its own and show the box «subir el worktree» with the command and a spinner until the daemon answers, with every key waiting; it SHALL then say «subido <rama> · N commits[ · rama nueva en origin| a origin]», or «<rama> · al día con origin, nada que subir» when nothing went up.
- REQ-279 IF the daemon refuses the push, THEN THE terminal client SHALL show a box «No se pudo subir <rama>» with the daemon's message, which any key closes and does nothing else.
- REQ-280 WHEN `s` is pressed on a folder or a repository's root, THE terminal client SHALL say that it is a folder and that `s` pushes a worktree, and send nothing.
- REQ-281 THE contract SHALL give `remove_workspace` an optional `force` (false when absent) and have the command `check_workspace {workspace}` whose result is `{ahead}`, with fixtures and in the Kotlin types.
- REQ-282 WHEN `remove_workspace` arrives with `remove_worktree` and `force`, THE daemon SHALL run `git worktree remove --force`, which takes the folder with the changes that were not committed, and leave the branch in place (REQ-147); IF `force` comes without `remove_worktree`, THEN it SHALL answer `bad_request` and change nothing.
- REQ-283 WHEN `check_workspace` arrives for a worktree that has a branch, THE daemon SHALL answer `ahead`, the commits of the branch that no remote has (REQ-275); a folder or a worktree with no branch SHALL be `bad_request` and an unknown workspace `not_found`.
- REQ-284 WHERE the `uncommitted` changes list any file, THE terminal client SHALL show a box that lists them, says how many commits are not pushed and stay in the branch (when there are any) and asks for the branch's name (the workspace's name when it has no branch) in a field; every key SHALL be a letter of that name, `↵` SHALL send `remove_workspace` with `remove_worktree` and `force` only when what is typed is exactly that name, and `esc` SHALL go back to the question of closing.
- REQ-285 WHERE the `uncommitted` changes list no file, THE terminal client SHALL say how many commits are not pushed and stay in the branch (when there are any) and keep REQ-145: `y` removes without `force`.
- REQ-286 THE contract SHALL give the `workspace` node an optional `layout` and the command `new_pane` an optional `beside` and `dir`, as the Interfaces say, with a fixture of each and in the Kotlin types.
- REQ-287 THE daemon SHALL keep for each workspace a layout whose leaves are exactly its panes, none while it has no panes, save it in `session.json`, and after every change bring it to agree with the panes: a pane that has no place SHALL be split off the last one (beside it when it is alone, below it when it is not), a pane that is gone SHALL give its room to its neighbour, a pane the tree has twice or does not know SHALL be dropped, and a ratio out of range SHALL be brought in range.
- REQ-288 WHERE a workspace has no saved layout (one made before layouts), THE daemon SHALL give it the first pane on the left and the rest stacked on the right, each with an even share.
- REQ-289 WHEN `new_pane` arrives with `beside`, THE daemon SHALL split that pane along `dir` (a row when absent), each half getting 500 thousandths; IF `beside` is not a pane of the workspace, THEN it SHALL answer `bad_request` and start nothing; WHEN it arrives without `beside`, the pane SHALL go where REQ-287 puts a pane that has no place.
- REQ-290 THE terminal client SHALL draw the panels at the rectangles of the daemon's layout, the first side of a split getting its share of the width or height rounded down and each side at least one cell when there are two; WHERE the workspace has no layout, it SHALL arrange them as before (REQ-288).
- REQ-291 WHEN `h`, `j`, `k`, `l` or an arrow is pressed at the panel level, THE terminal client SHALL focus the panel nearest on that side among those that touch the focused one's side and overlap it across, and nothing when there is none; `tab` SHALL focus the next in the order the panels were made.
- REQ-292 WHEN `|`, `-` or `n` is pressed at the panel level, or a program is chosen in the menu of `N`, THE terminal client SHALL send `new_pane` with `beside` the focused panel and `dir` `row` for `|`, `col` for `-`, and along the focused panel's longer side (`row` when it is at least twice as wide as high) for `n` and the menu; the new panel SHALL take the focus when it appears.
- REQ-293 THE contract SHALL have the commands `swap_panes {a, b}` and `grow_pane {pane, dir, by}` (`by` from -800 to 800 thousandths), with fixtures and in the Kotlin types.
- REQ-294 WHEN `swap_panes` arrives for two different panes of one workspace, THE daemon SHALL exchange their places in the layout, keeping its shape and its shares and the order of the workspace's `panes`; IF the panes are the same, or `b` is not in the workspace of `a`, THEN it SHALL answer `bad_request`, and IF `a` is unknown, `not_found`. (Amended by N167: a view is accepted where a pane is, REQ-724.)
- REQ-295 WHEN `grow_pane` arrives, THE daemon SHALL move the nearest split around the pane whose direction is `dir` so that the pane has `by` thousandths more of the split (less when negative), the share kept from 100 to 900; IF no split of that direction is around the pane, or `by` is out of range, THEN it SHALL answer `bad_request` and change nothing, and IF the pane is unknown, `not_found`.
- REQ-296 WHEN `H`, `J`, `K` or `L` is pressed at the panel level, THE terminal client SHALL send `swap_panes` with the focused panel and the one on that side (REQ-291), or say that there is no panel there and send nothing; WHEN `<`, `>`, `{` or `}` is pressed it SHALL send `grow_pane` for the focused panel, `row` for `<` `>` and `col` for `{` `}`, by -50 for `<` `{` and 50 for `>` `}`, and WHEN the daemon refuses it SHALL say there is no split that way for that panel.
- REQ-297 THE daemon SHALL keep the blocks of each shell pane on disk in `<state>/history/<pane id>.json`, written whole and atomically, in a folder only the person can enter and a file only the person can read (0700 and 0600), at once when a command starts or ends or the blocks are emptied, at most every 2 seconds for the output of one that runs, and all that changed when the daemon is stopped from inside; the file of a closed pane SHALL be removed, and so SHALL those of panes the session does not have when the daemon starts.
- REQ-298 WHEN the daemon starts and a restored pane has a saved history, THE pane's `blocks` node SHALL have those blocks from the start, without waiting for a prompt and in the order they had, ahead of what the new shell runs, the ids going on after the last one.
- REQ-299 WHERE a block was running when it was saved, THE daemon SHALL bring it back as `interrupted`, not running, with no `code` and with `ms` what it had run until the save.
- REQ-300 WHEN a history is restored, THE daemon SHALL add after it a block with `restart` (no command, `started` the time of the start), unless the last block already is one, and SHALL add nothing when no block was saved; the limits of REQ-57 and of 4 MiB SHALL hold for what is restored.
- REQ-301 THE terminal client and the phone SHALL draw a block with `restart` as one line across the list that says «el daemon se reinició» and how long ago, which cannot be selected, and a block with `interrupted` as «interrumpido», with the time it had run when that is a second or more, apart from the colours of a command that ended (the terminal client's bar grey, the phone's label yellow).
- REQ-302 IF a history file cannot be read, THEN THE daemon SHALL move it aside to `<pane id>.json.bad`, say so on its standard error and start the pane with no history.
- REQ-303 WHEN `clear` empties a pane's blocks (REQ-273), THE saved history SHALL be empty too.
- REQ-304 WHEN the left button is pressed on a command or an output line of a pane's list of blocks in the terminal client, it SHALL begin a drag in that pane and let go of what was selected before; a press anywhere else SHALL be the click it always was and let go of it too.
- REQ-305 WHILE a drag is held, THE terminal client SHALL select from where it began to the line and column under the pointer, over the command and output lines only, the first line from the column it began on, the last up to and including the column it is on and the lines between whole; WHEN the pointer is above or below the list it SHALL select to its first or last line shown and scroll it a line every 60 milliseconds while it stays there; the selection SHALL stay in the pane it began in.
- REQ-306 WHEN the button is let go after the pointer moved, THE terminal client SHALL copy what is selected through OSC 52, the command lines without their prompt mark, the folder and time lines, the notes and the gaps left out and the lines joined by line breaks, and say «copiado · N líneas · M caracteres»; IF nothing that can be selected was covered, THEN it SHALL copy nothing; WHEN the pointer never moved, the press SHALL be the click it was.
- REQ-307 THE selection SHALL stay lit until the next press or key (`esc` included) and the wheel SHALL not let go of it.
- REQ-308 THE terminal client SHALL light a selection with the colour, among the six accents of the theme in force, that shows best against the theme's ground, and write on it in the ground colour or the text colour, whichever has the greater contrast with it; on each of the ten themes both SHALL have a contrast of at least 3 to 1.
- REQ-309 THE contract SHALL have the commands `list_files {workspace, path?}` and `find_files {workspace, query}`, with fixtures and in the Kotlin types; the result of the first SHALL be `{path, entries: [{name, kind (dir, file or link), size?, git?, ignored?}], total}` and that of the second `{paths, cut?}`.
- REQ-310 WHEN `list_files` arrives, THE daemon SHALL answer the entries of that folder of the workspace (`path` relative to the workspace's folder, the folder itself when absent), folders first and then the rest, each by name whatever the case, leaving out `.git`, at most 2000 of them with `total` saying how many there are, a link as `link` and not followed, and the size of a file.
- REQ-311 WHERE the folder is in a git repository, THE daemon SHALL give each entry what git says of it (`conflict`, `modified`, `deleted`, `added` or `untracked`, a folder's being the most serious of what is under it) and whether git ignores it; WHERE it is not, no entry SHALL have either.
- REQ-312 IF the path is absolute, has `..` or goes outside the workspace through a link, THEN THE daemon SHALL answer `bad_request`; IF it does not exist, `not_found`; IF it is a file, `bad_request`; IF the workspace is unknown, `not_found`.
- REQ-313 WHEN `find_files` arrives, THE daemon SHALL answer the paths of the workspace whose letters hold those of `query` in order, whatever the case, from the files git tracks and those it does not track or ignore (from a walk of bounded size that leaves out `.git`, `node_modules`, `target` and folders that begin with a dot when it is not a repository), the 50 best, those whose name holds the query whole first and then the shorter, with `cut` when there were more; an empty query SHALL answer none and one of more than 200 characters `bad_request`.
- REQ-314 WHEN `t` is pressed at the workspace or the panel level of the terminal client, it SHALL show the file tree in a column of 28 cells at the right of the panels, which share the rest, give it the keys and ask for the workspace's folder; WHEN it is shown and the keys are elsewhere, `t` SHALL give it the keys, and from inside it SHALL close it; the status bar SHALL say ARCHIVOS while the keys are in it.
- REQ-315 THE file tree SHALL show the open folders from the top, folders first, without what begins with a dot unless `.` shows it, each entry with what git says of it at the right and what git ignores, or is under a folder it ignores, dim, with a note row for a folder being read, one that could not be read (and why) and the entries that are not shown; `j` and `k` SHALL move, `↵` or `l` SHALL open or close a folder asking the daemon for it once, and `h` SHALL close an open folder or go to the one that holds the entry.
- REQ-316 (removed by N151) NO key of the terminal client SHALL open `$VISUAL`, `$EDITOR` or `nvim` for a file of the tree or of a viewer: `e` does nothing there. It had opened a new panel where REQ-292 puts one; the panel no longer draws a program such as `nvim` well (the user, 2026-10-20), and `↵`, `l` and a click open the viewer (REQ-615). A `/memory` row opens its file in the viewer (REQ-641).
- REQ-317 WHEN `/` is pressed in the file tree, THE client SHALL ask `find_files` for what is typed after each letter, show the paths of the answer to what is typed now and no other, open the one the keys are on on `↵` and end the search on `esc`; `y` SHALL copy the path through OSC 52 and say it, `.` SHALL show or hide the hidden entries and `r` SHALL ask again for every open folder.
- REQ-318 WHEN a row of the file tree is clicked, THE client SHALL give it the keys and open or close the folder or open the file; WHEN a panel is clicked, it SHALL take the keys back; THE wheel over the tree SHALL scroll it.
- REQ-323 THE phone SHALL have a screen of the files of a workspace, opened from the folder icon of the header of the workspace's screen, which reads the workspace's folder from the PC one folder at a time with `list_files`, starting at the workspace's own.
- REQ-324 THE walk SHALL go no higher than the workspace's folder: the first crumb SHALL be the workspace's name and go to it, the title SHALL say `/` or `/` and the path under it, there SHALL be no row to go up at the workspace's folder, and the system's back SHALL go up a folder and, at the workspace's folder, leave the screen for the workspace.
- REQ-325 WHEN a folder is tapped, THE screen SHALL go into it; a file or a link SHALL be listed, with its size (a link saying «enlace»), and do nothing when tapped.
- REQ-326 THE rows SHALL be in the order the PC sent them, folders first, without those whose name begins with a dot unless `.ocultos` is on, each with what git says of it at the right (`M`, `?`, `A`, `D` or `!`) and what git ignores dim and marked `◌`.
- REQ-327 WHILE a folder is being read, THE screen SHALL say «leyendo…» and an answer to an earlier tap SHALL NOT replace the folder asked for last; IF the PC refuses or the link fails, THEN it SHALL say the message and offer «leer otra vez»; an empty folder SHALL say so, and a listing the PC cut SHALL say how many of how many it shows.
- REQ-328 THE Kotlin types of `FileKind`, `GitMark`, `FileEntry` and `FileListing` SHALL read the result of the fixture `reply-ok-list-files.json` that the Rust types write.
- REQ-329 THE contract SHALL have the commands `project_config {repo}` and `start_workspace {workspace}`, with fixtures and in the schema; `create_worktree` and `new_pane` SHALL not change.
- REQ-330 WHEN the daemon needs the settings of a repository, THE daemon SHALL read `.jw.toml` at the root of the repository (the top level git reports for the path or for the workspace's project) afresh, and SHALL use for what the file does not say, or when there is no file, `root` `<repo>-wt` beside the repository, `branch` `feat/{name}`, no setup, one shell and the agent `claude`.
- REQ-331 THE daemon SHALL ignore every key of `.jw.toml` it does not use, those the old jw wrote (`env`, `dev`, `workspace`, `match`, `sync`, `tui`) among them, and SHALL read `root`, `branch`, `setup`, `copy`, `ports`, `[agent]` and `[layout]` as the old jw wrote them, so that the `.jw.toml` of the old jw's repositories works as it is.
- REQ-332 THE daemon SHALL read `root` with `~` as the home and a relative path from the repository's root, and `ratio` as the share of `a` kept between 0.1 and 0.9.
- REQ-333 IF `.jw.toml` is not valid TOML, or has a value of another type, or a marker other than `{name}` in `branch` or other than `{name}` and `{branch}` in a `setup` line, or a layout node that is both or neither a leaf and a split, or a `split` other than `right` or `down`, or a command line with a quote left open, or more than eight panes, THEN `project_config`, `create_worktree` and `start_workspace` SHALL answer `bad_request` with a message that names `.jw.toml` and the line of the key, or of the table that holds it, and SHALL make nothing.
- REQ-334 WHEN `project_config` arrives, THE daemon SHALL answer `{source?, root, branch, setup, panes}` as the interfaces say, and `git_failed` when the path is not in a repository.
- REQ-335 WHEN `create_worktree` arrives, THE daemon SHALL put the worktree at `<root>/<folder>` with the `root` of the repository's settings (REQ-330), the folder as before (REQ-192).
- REQ-336 WHEN `start_workspace` arrives for a workspace with no panes, THE daemon SHALL start the panes of its layout and reply their ids in its order; they are those of the settings that apply to it (a worktree's, its repository's; a folder's, REQ-500 and REQ-501), and one shell when they have no layout; IF the workspace has a pane, THEN it SHALL answer `conflict`, and `not_found` for an unknown one.
- REQ-337 THE daemon SHALL start a leaf `run = "agent"` with the `start` of the agent `[agent] default` names (that name itself when it has none), `editor` with `[layout] editor`, else `$VISUAL`, else `$EDITOR`, else `nvim`, `shell` as a plain shell and any other text as the command line it is, as a program in the pane (the wrapper of REQ-50: the shell stays when it ends), and SHALL arrange them as the tree says, `right` as a row and `down` as a column; WHERE `[layout]` says nothing it SHALL start one shell.
- REQ-338 WHERE a leaf starts the agent `claude`, THE daemon SHALL start it as `new_pane` does (its own conversation id and its hooks).
- REQ-339 WHERE `setup` has lines, THE daemon SHALL add a pane named `setup` below the whole layout, in a split whose first part keeps 68%, which runs the lines in `/bin/sh` in the worktree's folder, one after the other, writing `❯ ` and the line before each one and stopping at the first that fails, with `{name}` and `{branch}` replaced by their values quoted for the shell, and which stays a shell with all it wrote on its screen when they end.
- REQ-340 THE panes of the layout SHALL start at once and SHALL NOT wait for the setup.
- REQ-341 WHEN the lines of a setup end, THE `setup` pane SHALL raise `shell_done` with the exit status of the line that failed, or 0, by reporting it the way a shell integration does (the script ends with OSC 133;D).
- REQ-342 WHEN `w` opens the box of a worktree in the terminal client, THE client SHALL ask `project_config` for the repository, say that it is reading until it answers, and `↵` SHALL do nothing while it waits or after a refusal.
- REQ-343 THE box SHALL show, for a name that can be used, the branch (the template with `{name}` replaced) and the folder (`<root>/<name>`), one line for each setup command, the programs of the panes (and `setup` when there are lines) and whether they come from the repository's `.jw.toml` or there is none; IF `project_config` was refused, THEN it SHALL show the message in red instead.
- REQ-344 THE checks of the name in the box (a name that is valid, a worktree already open there, the folder or the branch already existing) SHALL be made on the branch and the folder of REQ-343.
- REQ-345 WHEN `↵` is pressed in the box with a name that can be used, THE client SHALL send `create_worktree` with that branch, the base and the folder and then `start_workspace` for the workspace it made in place of `new_pane`, and open it as before; IF `start_workspace` is refused, THEN it SHALL show the message and open one shell.
- REQ-346 WHERE the file is the one of design-studio (`setup` with one line, `[agent] default = "claude"`, `[layout]` with `editor = "nvim"` and `run = "agent"`), THE daemon SHALL start one `claude` pane and the setup pane.
- REQ-347 WHEN the phone's worktree sheet opens, and again when the person chooses another repository, THE phone SHALL ask `project_config` for the repository and say «leyendo .jw.toml…» until the PC answers, and `crear y abrir` SHALL do nothing while it waits or after a refusal.
- REQ-348 IF the PC refuses `project_config` (a `.jw.toml` it cannot use), THEN THE sheet SHALL show the PC's message in place of what it will make.
- REQ-349 THE sheet SHALL show, for a name that can be used, the branch (the settings' template with `{name}` replaced), the branch it starts from, the folder (the last folder of the settings' `root` and the name), one line for each setup command, the programs of the panes (and `setup` when there are lines) and whether they come from the repository's `.jw.toml` or there is none.
- REQ-350 THE names a worktree cannot take SHALL be the folders of the settings' `root`, read with `list_dir`, and the workspaces open in it.
- REQ-351 WHEN a worktree made by the phone has been created, THE phone SHALL ask `start_workspace` for it and SHALL go on sending `new_pane` for a folder it opens (REQ-208).
- REQ-352 IF `start_workspace` is refused, THEN THE phone SHALL send `new_pane` so that the worktree has one shell, and THE sheet SHALL say that it was opened with one shell and give the PC's message, and stay.
- REQ-353 THE binary SHALL have the subcommands `ls`, `pane ls`, `pane open`, `pane close`, `read`, `prompt`, `worktree` and `skill`, which reach the daemon on `identity::socket_path()` and use only nodes and commands that exist.
- REQ-354 IF a command fails, THEN it SHALL write `<bin> <command>: <message>` to stderr and exit 1, a use it does not accept SHALL write its usage to stderr and exit 2, and an agent that waits for an answer (REQ-454) exit 3; IF no daemon answers, THEN every command but `skill` SHALL say so and exit 1.
- REQ-355 THE word that names a workspace SHALL be taken as its id, else as exactly its name, its branch or its folder's name, else as the start of the name or the branch of one workspace; IF several workspaces fit or none does, THEN the command SHALL say so, list the several, and do nothing.
- REQ-356 WHEN `ls` runs, THE binary SHALL print one row for each workspace of the session (name, kind, branch, how many panes, the kind of its most urgent attention), or with `--json` an array of `{id, name, kind, path, project?, branch?, panes, attention}`, `panes` being their number and `attention` the list of the kinds.
- REQ-357 WHEN `pane ls` runs, THE binary SHALL print one row for each pane of the workspace (id, title, the program running in it or `-`, and `claude` or `codex` when that is the agent in it), or with `--json` an array of `{id, title, running?, agent?}`.
- REQ-358 THE agent of a pane SHALL be the program `claude` or `codex` when it is the pane's foreground program, whoever started it; a script an interpreter runs counts by the script's name, as the pane's title does.
- REQ-359 WHEN `read` runs, THE binary SHALL print the last `--lines` (40 by default) lines of the screen of the pane, trailing blank lines dropped; the pane SHALL be the one `--pane` names, else the first with an agent in it, else the first of the workspace.
- REQ-360 WHEN `read --blocks` runs, THE binary SHALL print the last `--lines` commands of a shell pane, each as `❯ <command>`, its output as text and `[exit <code> · <ms> ms]` (or `[running]`); IF the pane has no commands to read, THEN it SHALL say so and exit 1.
- REQ-361 WHEN `prompt` runs, THE target SHALL be the pane `--pane` names, else the first with an agent in it; IF there is none, THEN the command SHALL say that no agent runs in the workspace and write nothing.
- REQ-362 IF the target is the pane the command itself runs in, THEN it SHALL refuse and write nothing.
- REQ-363 WHEN `prompt` has a target, THE binary SHALL, unless `--now`, wait up to 40 seconds for the pane's screen to hold some text and be unchanged for one second, then write the text as a bracketed paste (`ESC [ 200 ~`, the text, `ESC [ 201 ~`) and, in a write of its own 200 ms later, `\r`; IF the screen does not settle, THEN it SHALL say so, suggest `--now` and write nothing; WITH `--now` it SHALL write at once.
- REQ-364 WITH `--tag`, THE text SHALL be preceded by `[<bin>: <workspace>, pane <id>] ` naming the pane and the workspace the command runs in; IF it runs in no pane, THEN `--tag` SHALL be refused.
- REQ-365 WHEN `worktree` runs, THE binary SHALL take the repository from `--in` (a path, or a workspace as REQ-355 names it), else from the project of the workspace of the pane it runs in, else from the git top level of the current folder; read its settings (`project_config`); make the branch `--branch` or else the template with the name; send `create_worktree` (the name as the folder, `--from` as the base) and then `start_workspace`; and print the workspace's id and path.
- REQ-366 IF `start_workspace` is refused after the worktree was made, THEN THE binary SHALL open one shell in it, and say that and the PC's message, and exit 1.
- REQ-367 WITH `--task`, WHEN the worktree has started, THE binary SHALL wait up to 40 seconds for an agent to run in it and then send it the text as `prompt` does; IF none does, THEN it SHALL say that the worktree was made and no agent took the task, and exit 1.
- REQ-368 WHEN `pane open` runs, THE binary SHALL send `new_pane` with the words after `--` as the program (a shell when there are none), `--of` as the pane to split (the last of the workspace when `--split` is given without it) and `--split` as the direction, and print the new pane's id.
- REQ-369 WHEN `pane close` runs, THE binary SHALL send `close_pane`; IF the pane is the one the command runs in, or the last of its workspace, THEN it SHALL refuse unless `--force`.
- REQ-370 WHEN `skill` runs, THE binary SHALL print the skill it carries, which names the binary and its environment variable from the product's identity; WHEN `skill install` runs, it SHALL write it, whole and through a rename, to `skills/<name>/SKILL.md` under `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` or else under `~/.claude`, and print the path.
- REQ-371 THE shell integration of zsh, bash and fish SHALL define `claude` as a function of the interactive shell that runs `<bin> claude "$@"`; `command claude`, a path to the program and a script SHALL run the original.
- REQ-372 WHEN `<bin> claude` runs in a pane of the daemon with only arguments the engine can serve (none, or `-c`, `--continue`, `-r`, `--resume [id]`, `--session-id`, `-n`, `--name`, `--model`, `--effort`, `--permission-mode`, `--add-dir`, `--settings`, `--chrome`, `--no-chrome`, `--prompt-suggestions`, and a first message), THE binary SHALL send `open_conversation` for that pane and stay in the foreground until the conversation ends.
- REQ-373 IF `<bin> claude` runs outside a pane of the daemon, OR is given `-p`, `--print`, `-h`, `--help`, `-v`, `--version`, an input or output format, or a subcommand (`mcp`, `config`, `update`, `doctor`, `install`, `auth`, `plugin`), THEN it SHALL run the original `claude` with the same arguments and end with its status.
- REQ-374 WHEN the daemon starts a `claude` in a pane whose shell has the integration, it SHALL NOT add its hooks and SHALL give it a session id as it does for any agent.
- REQ-375 WHEN `open_conversation` arrives, THE daemon SHALL reply `{node, session}` and, for a new conversation, start the engine in the folder `claude` was typed in (`PWD` of `env`, else the pane's) with the command of `docs/claude-engine.md`, a session id of its own choosing (or `--session-id`), the conversation's name, model, effort and mode and the environment `env` carries (the daemon's own when it carries none); for a conversation that is resumed (`resume`, `-r`, `-c`) it SHALL start the engine when the next message comes (REQ-391, REQ-393).
- REQ-376 WHILE a conversation is open, THE pane's node SHALL name it in `conversation` and its `running` SHALL be the program `claude`, so `jwn ls`, the titles and the rows of Home do not change.
- REQ-377 THE daemon SHALL keep the engine for as long as the conversation is open, whether or not a client is connected, and SHALL end it (closing its input) when the conversation closes, when the binary's `claude` ends or when the pane closes.
- REQ-378 WHILE a turn streams, THE daemon SHALL grow the `text` item with what arrives and send the patch at most every 50 ms, plus another 50 ms for each 4 KiB of the text that has arrived, and at most every second, and SHALL send the whole text once the message ends.
- REQ-379 WHEN the engine uses a tool, THE daemon SHALL add a `tool` item with its name and its argument (the path or the command, cut to 200 characters), set its state from the result, keep up to 40 lines of its output and, for an edit, its diff.
- REQ-380 WHEN a turn ends, THE daemon SHALL add a `result` item with its time, the tools it used and its cost, set `status` to `idle` and raise `agent_done`.
- REQ-381 WHEN the engine starts a turn nobody asked for (a monitor's line, a background task that ended, a scheduled run), THE daemon SHALL add a `note` that says why and treat the turn as any other.
- REQ-382 THE messages sent while a turn runs SHALL wait in `queue` and be sent one by one when a turn ends, and an interrupt SHALL NOT empty it.
- REQ-383 WHEN `conversation_interrupt` arrives while a turn runs, THE daemon SHALL send the engine's `interrupt` and add a note «Interrumpido por ti» to the turn.
- REQ-384 WHEN the engine asks to use a tool, THE daemon SHALL set `ask` (the tool, what it is about to do, the diff of an edit, the options), set `status` to `asking` and raise `permission`.
- REQ-385 WHEN `conversation_answer` arrives for the `ask` that is set, THE daemon SHALL answer the engine, clear `ask` for every client and set `status` to `tool`; an answer for any other `ask` SHALL be refused.
- REQ-386 WHEN the answer allows, THE daemon SHALL reply `allow` with the tool's input; WHEN it denies, `deny` with a message that says the person refused; WHEN it allows for the session, `allow`, and it SHALL answer `allow` without asking to the later requests of the same tool (for a command, the same first words) until the conversation closes.
- REQ-387 THE daemon SHALL offer an answer for the session only when the engine suggests one, and SHALL offer no mode and no tool that bypasses permissions.
- REQ-388 THE conversation's `name` SHALL start as the first 28 characters of the first message (the first 7 of the session's id until then) and its `accent` as `blue`; `conversation_set` SHALL change them, the daemon SHALL give the name to the engine (`--name`, and `/rename` once it runs) and both SHALL be saved with the conversation.
- REQ-389 WHEN `conversation_set` changes the model or the effort, THE daemon SHALL send the engine `/model <m>` or `/effort <e>`; WHEN it changes the mode, THE daemon SHALL ask the engine that runs to change it (`set_permission_mode`) at once, also while a turn runs (the user, 2026-10-19: «cuando estemos en un modo corriendo debemos permitir el cambio de modo»; checked against claude 2.1.303, which answers and says the new mode in the middle of a tool); the node SHALL show the new value only when the engine says it runs with it.
- REQ-390 IF `conversation_set` names a mode that is not `manual`, `acceptEdits`, `plan` or `auto`, THEN THE daemon SHALL refuse it.
- REQ-391 IF the engine's process ends while the conversation is open, THEN THE daemon SHALL set `status` to `dead`, add a note with the reason it can read and start it again with `--resume` when the next message comes (`status` `resuming` until it runs).
- REQ-392 THE node SHALL keep the last 300 items (`first` counts the ones dropped), and THE daemon SHALL save them with the name, the accent, the model, the effort, the mode and the session in `<state>/conversations/`, at most 2 MiB for each conversation.
- REQ-393 WHEN the daemon restarts, THE pane SHALL be brought back by what ran in it (`claude --resume <session>`) and the conversation SHALL come back with its items, a `divider` that says the daemon restarted and `status` `dead` until the next message.
- REQ-394 WHEN `conversation_shell` arrives, THE daemon SHALL run the command with the person's shell in the pane's folder, without a terminal, for at most 60 seconds and keeping up to 64 KiB of its output, add a `shell` item and send the output to the engine as text with the person's next message.
- REQ-395 WHILE the binary's `claude` runs in a terminal the client does not draw, IT SHALL print the items as lines (the question as a numbered prompt) and send what is typed with `conversation_send`, with no box and no colour the terminal lacks.
- REQ-396 THE schema, its fixtures and the conformance tests of Rust and Kotlin SHALL cover the node, its items and the commands, and `default_accepts` and the phone's `hello` SHALL list `conversation` v1.
- REQ-397 WHILE a pane has a `conversation`, THE terminal client SHALL open its node, draw it in place of the pane's screen and blocks, and send what is typed to the conversation and not to the pane.
- REQ-398 THE terminal client SHALL draw the person's message on a band with `❯` in the conversation's accent and Claude's with `●` and its code in cyan, and each tool as `● name(argument)` with the dot yellow while it runs, green when it worked and red when it was denied or failed, and under it `⎿` its summary, an edit's diff with the added and removed lines tinted from the theme.
- REQ-399 WHEN `^O` is pressed or a tool's line is clicked, THE terminal client SHALL open the output of the tool or the steps of the subagent, and WHEN pressed again close them.
- REQ-400 WHILE `status` is not `idle`, THE terminal client SHALL draw over the field a line with what Claude is doing, for how long and `esc interrumpe`; WHEN a turn ends it SHALL draw its `result` item.
- REQ-401 THE terminal client's field SHALL sit at the foot with a row of chips (the folder, the branch and how many messages wait) and the line; `↵` SHALL send, or queue while Claude works, `esc` SHALL interrupt, and a text of many lines pasted SHALL go in as one paste.
- REQ-402 THE terminal client's footer SHALL show at its left the mode and, while something runs in the background, `⚙ n`, and at its right the model, and nothing else; a click on the mode or the model SHALL open its list; the mode SHALL be dim for `manual`, magenta for accept edits, cyan for `plan` and yellow for `auto`.
- REQ-403 WHEN `⇧tab` is pressed, THE terminal client SHALL change the mode in the order manual, accept edits, plan, auto, and `/mode`, `/model` and `/effort` SHALL open a list or take the value typed after them.
