- REQ-622 THE clients SHALL draw a class with the theme's accent for it (kw magenta, str green, num yellow, ty and attr cyan, fn blue, cm dim and italic) and a run with no class, or one they do not know, as plain text.
- REQ-623 THE daemon SHALL colour at most the first 64 KiB of a file and SHALL stop 300 ms after its first line (the first use of a language compiles its grammar, up to 480 ms, once), at a line too long to be kept (REQ-614) or at a line the grammar cannot parse; the lines after that SHALL be sent plain.
- REQ-624 WHERE a `.md` is shown as source, THE daemon SHALL colour it as Markdown.
- REQ-625 THE shell integration of zsh, bash and fish SHALL report the folder the shell is in, as `OSC 633;P;Pwd=<base64>`, each time it draws a prompt, and THE daemon SHALL keep the last one that is an absolute folder that exists.
- REQ-626 WHEN the folder reported is not the pane's `cwd`, THE daemon SHALL publish it as the pane's `cwd` within a second, so a `cd` shows in the chip and the status line of both clients.
- REQ-627 IF a pane's shell has reported no folder (a shell without the integration, or a program that has not given the prompt back), THEN THE pane's `cwd` SHALL stay as it was.
- REQ-628 WHEN a pane restarts, THE daemon SHALL start its shell in the pane's `cwd` if that folder exists, and in the workspace folder if not.
- REQ-629 WHEN a pane is made beside another, THE daemon SHALL start its shell in that pane's `cwd` if that folder exists, and in the workspace folder if not.
- REQ-630 WHEN a conversation is opened from a pane with no environment, THE engine SHALL start in the pane's `cwd`; with the shell's environment (`jwn claude`), in its `PWD`, as now; and THE conversation node SHALL carry that folder as `cwd`.
- REQ-631 WHERE a conversation has `cwd`, THE `@` picker of both clients SHALL write the reference as the path from that folder, with `../` where it climbs, and the phone's list SHALL start in that folder when it is inside the workspace and at the workspace root when it is not.
- REQ-632 THE file tree, THE file viewer and THE phone's Archivos SHALL keep the workspace folder as their root, whatever the panel's `cwd`.
- REQ-633 WHILE the panel of a shell is shown, its chip SHALL say its folder and that a Claude opens there; and THE phone SHALL have a button of at least 48 dp that opens a Claude from the panel, saying the folder it opens in.
- REQ-634 WHERE `config.toml` does not say `chrome = false`, THE engine of a conversation SHALL be started with `--chrome` (the tools of Claude in Chrome); a `--chrome` or `--no-chrome` typed in `jwn claude` SHALL win over the config, and a conversation opened with no arguments (a resume, the phone's button) SHALL get the config's.
- REQ-635 THE conversation SHALL serve `--prompt-suggestions <value>` and pass it to the engine, so that `suggestions = false` in `config.toml` (REQ-572) keeps the person in the conversation instead of running the original `claude`.
- REQ-636 WHEN a workspace is selected in the terminal client or opened on the phone, THE client SHALL ask the daemon for the workspace's root folder (and the terminal's open folders) before the file tree or the files screen is shown, once, and keep the answer; WHEN the tree is shown again or a folder of the phone's files screen is entered, THE client SHALL show what it kept at once and ask again, replacing the rows when the answer comes; a failed second answer SHALL leave the rows, and the phone SHALL show the error only when it has nothing to show.
- REQ-637 WHEN the left button is pressed twice on the same cell within 400 ms in the terminal client, THE client SHALL select the word under it and, for the list of a conversation, of a terminal's blocks and of a Markdown viewer, copy it as a drag over it does (REQ-463, REQ-306), and in the field of a conversation select it, so that what is typed next takes its place; a word is letters and digits of any alphabet and `_ - . / ~`, a full stop or a dash that ends it is left out, and a space or any other mark is no word and the press is the click it was.
- REQ-638 WHEN the daemon stops, THE daemon SHALL close the input of every engine, wait up to 3 s for all of them together and end the process group of each that has not ended, before the process exits; WHILE an engine runs, THE daemon SHALL keep a note of its group in `conversations/<pane>.<pgid>.pgid`, removed when it ends; WHEN the daemon starts, it SHALL end the group of each noted number that is still a `claude` with `--input-format stream-json` (any other process is left alone), remove the notes, and only then bring the conversations back (REQ-575 would otherwise refuse them); a group number of 0 or 1, the daemon's own or one that a signed pid cannot hold SHALL never be signalled; WHEN an engine cannot be handed to its readers at its start, THE daemon SHALL end its group and remove its note.
- REQ-639 WHILE a text file is open, THE viewer SHALL have a cursor, a block on a character of a line (its tabs being spaces), that `h` `j` `k` `l` and the arrows move one character or one line, going back to the column it wanted when a line is long enough and never past the first or last line or the end of the line; THE view SHALL follow it with two rows of margin, and sideways when wrapping is off; `␣` `PgDn` `PgUp` SHALL move it a page, `e` to the end of the word it is on or of the next one and `b` to the start of the word it is on or of the one before, as Neovim's do (a word is letters, digits and `_`, or a run of other marks, and a blank or a line break is the space between them; they cross lines, and with a selection open they grow it), `g` and `G` to the first and the last line, a search to the match and a reread SHALL keep it (inside the new file); THE wheel SHALL scroll without moving it; THE bar SHALL say `L<line>:<column>` with the percentage.
- REQ-639a WHEN `v` is pressed, THE viewer SHALL open a selection at the cursor that covers what lies between it and the cursor, lit with the theme's selection colours (REQ-308), and its status SHALL say `SELECCIÓN`, the lines and the keys; `v` again SHALL let go of it; `esc` SHALL let go of it before it closes anything (then the search, then the tab); `y` SHALL copy the characters it covers, line by line, and let go, and with none open copy the whole file as before.
- REQ-639b WHEN `↵` is pressed, THE client SHALL write `@<path>#L<from>-<to> ` (`#L<n> ` for one line) in the field of the agent of the workspace, for the lines selected or, with none, the line of the cursor, with the path the file was asked of the daemon by (from the workspace's folder) written as the `@` picker writes a path (REQ-631), and give the keyboard to that field; THE client SHALL send nothing, the viewer SHALL stay open and the status SHALL say so; WHERE the workspace has no agent, the reference SHALL be copied and the status say it.
- REQ-640 THE viewer of text SHALL keep up to 8 files open, one tab each; THE tab that shows SHALL have the keys and each tab SHALL keep its own cursor, selection and place; WHERE there are two or more, a bar of their names SHALL be above the file, and `Tab` and `⇧Tab` SHALL go round the tabs and a click on a name go to it; WHEN a file that is open is opened again its tab SHALL show as it was, and WHEN it is read again (`r`) it SHALL take the new reading where the cursor was; `esc` or `q` SHALL close the tab that shows and the last one the viewer; THE ninth file SHALL close the oldest tab that is not the one showing.
- REQ-640a WHEN `t` is pressed in the viewer of text, THE tree SHALL be shown beside it with the keys, and `↵` on a file of it SHALL open it in a tab and give the keys back to the viewer; `esc` SHALL give them back and close nothing; WHEN a Markdown view is opened from the viewer it SHALL be over the tabs, which SHALL come back when it closes, and a text file read while it is open SHALL join them.
- REQ-640b WHEN the button goes down on the text of the viewer, THE cursor SHALL go to that character; WHEN the pointer is dragged, the selection SHALL cover from where the press began, and WHEN the button is let go what it covers SHALL be copied and the selection SHALL stay, so that `↵`, `y` or `esc` follow it; a press that did not move SHALL copy nothing; WHEN the button is pressed twice on one cell within 400 ms (REQ-637), THE word under it SHALL be selected, and copied when the button is let go.
- REQ-641 WHEN a row of `/memory` is chosen, THE client SHALL open its file in the viewer if it is inside the workspace (a `.md` as Markdown, any other as text), and WHERE it is outside, which the daemon does not read (OPEN-17), copy its path and say so; NO key or row of the client SHALL open an editor program.
- REQ-642 THE phone's viewer of a text file SHALL NOT scroll sideways: a line that is long SHALL go on in the next rows under its own number, and a word with no space SHALL break at the edge; THE `⏎` button that wrapped the lines SHALL NOT exist.
- REQ-643 WHEN a number of a line is tapped in the phone's viewer, THE phone SHALL pick that line; WHEN another number is tapped, it SHALL pick every line between the two; WHEN the only line picked is tapped, it SHALL let go; WHILE lines are picked, a bar of touch targets of at least 48 dp SHALL say `L12–15` and how many lines, and offer `@ al agente`, `copiar` and `×`.
- REQ-643a WHEN `@ al agente` is chosen, THE phone SHALL close the viewer, open the workspace's screen at its first pane that runs a `claude` and write `@<path>#L<from>-<to> ` in that pane's field (REQ-639b's reference, written from the folder the engine runs in) once, sending nothing; WHERE no pane of the workspace runs one, THE phone SHALL copy the reference and say so; WHERE another `claude` holds the session, it SHALL say that nothing was written; `copiar` SHALL copy the reference with the path as the PC was asked for the file and let go of the lines.
- REQ-644 THE phone SHALL NOT scroll sideways the lines of a diff or the output of a tool inside a conversation, nor the code blocks of a Markdown document: a line that is long SHALL go on in the next row; WHAT keeps scrolling sideways SHALL be only the columns of a Markdown table, the screen of a terminal (it has its own columns) and the strips of controls (chips, tabs, breadcrumbs).
- REQ-645 WHEN `cd @` and nothing else is typed in the shell of a pane (zsh, bash or fish), THE shell integration SHALL take the shell to the folder of the pane's workspace, which the daemon gives every shell in `JWN_WORKSPACE_DIR` (at its start and when it is brought back); any other `cd` SHALL be the shell's own; THE integration SHALL NOT define `cd` in zsh or bash where the person has a function or an alias of that name (in fish it runs before the person's config, so theirs wins); and the pane's folder SHALL follow as after any `cd` (REQ-625), so that a Claude opened next starts there.
- REQ-646 THE name of the program (binary, state and config directories, socket, env prefix, the scheme of the pairing link, the peer names on the wire and the names the daemon prints) SHALL come from `identity.toml`; WHERE a name has to be spelt by Cargo or by a script that no program renders (the binary's `[[bin]] name`, the env names the shell integration reads, the file `fish/jw.fish`), a test SHALL fail when it differs from the identity.
- REQ-647 THE `session` node SHALL carry `jobs`, a list absent when empty (additive: `v` stays 1), newest first, of `{id, kind: remove_workspace|sync_workspace, workspace, name, state: running|done|failed, started_at (ms since the epoch), duration_ms (once it ended), detail (the error, when it failed), cancellable}`; THE `workspace` node and its summary in the session SHALL carry `removing`, absent when false. THE contract SHALL have the commands `dismiss_job {job}` and `dismiss_jobs {}`, with schema, fixtures and the Kotlin types in the same part (N159a).
- REQ-648 WHEN `remove_workspace` arrives with `remove_worktree`, THE daemon SHALL check it as before (`bad_request` for a folder, `not_found` for an unknown workspace), refuse with `conflict` («ya se está quitando») a workspace that is already being removed, and otherwise, in one step under the lock, mark it `removing` and begin a job of kind `remove_workspace`, answer `{job}` at once, and run `git worktree remove` (with `--force` when asked) in a thread of its own without the lock. WHEN `remove_worktree` is absent, THE daemon SHALL close the workspace at once and answer ok with no job (it does no I/O).
- REQ-649 WHEN `git worktree remove` ends well, THE daemon SHALL, in one revision of the session, remove the workspace and end its job `done` with `duration_ms`; IF git refuses (changes that were not committed without `force`, a folder that cannot go), THEN it SHALL unmark the workspace, leave it and its folder as they were, and end the job `failed` with git's whole message in `detail`; IF the thread ends without saying, THEN the job SHALL be `failed` and the workspace unmarked. The branch stays in every case (REQ-147).
- REQ-650 THE daemon SHALL hold its lock for the bookkeeping of a job only (its beginning, its end), never while git runs; a job that begins and a job that ends are each one `refresh`.
- REQ-651 WHILE a workspace is `removing`, THE daemon SHALL answer `conflict` («ya se está quitando») to every command that looks the workspace up (a new panel, `start_workspace`, `sync_workspace`, a second `remove_workspace`, `review_pr`).
- REQ-652 WHEN `sync_workspace` begins to push (after it found there is something to push), THE daemon SHALL begin a job of kind `sync_workspace` named by the branch, `cancellable`, and end it when the push ends: `done`, or `failed` with the error as `detail` (`cancelado` when `cancel` stopped it). The reply of `sync_workspace` SHALL stay what REQ-276 says (it waits for the push), so a client that waits for it and `cancel` (REQ-604) work as they did.
- REQ-653 THE daemon SHALL keep the 20 newest jobs that ended and every one that runs, and SHALL NOT save them: a restart loses them and the `removing` mark. `dismiss_job {job}` SHALL forget a job that ended (`not_found` when there is none, `conflict` when it runs); `dismiss_jobs {}` SHALL forget every one that ended and answer ok when there are none. Dismissing is for every client; what a client has seen of a job is its own.
- REQ-654 WHERE `check_workspace` and the other reads that a question of the terminal client waits for are slow, THE terminal client SHALL make them in a thread of its own (REQ-658); the daemon SHALL NOT turn them into jobs.
- REQ-655 THE terminal client's «avisos» (`^␣ a`) SHALL have two tabs, `alertas` (what it showed, unchanged) and `trabajos`, and `Tab` SHALL change between them. THE `trabajos` tab SHALL list first the jobs that run, each with a spinner, what it is (`quitar <nombre>`, `subir <rama>`) and its time running, live; and below, the ones that ended (`✓` green, `✗` red) with how long each took, newest first; and a line under the list SHALL say the result or the error of the one selected. `↵` SHALL go to the job's workspace when it is still there (and say so when it is not), `x` SHALL dismiss the selected job that ended, `X` every one that ended (`dismiss_job`, `dismiss_jobs`), `c` SHALL stop the selected job that is `cancellable` (`cancel`, REQ-604) and say that a removal is not stopped, `esc` SHALL close; a click on a tab SHALL change to it. WHEN `^␣ a` is pressed with no alert waiting and the jobs' mark showing, THE box SHALL open on the `trabajos` tab, as a click on the mark does.
- REQ-656 THE status bar SHALL show `avisos N` counting only the alerts, as before, and to its left the jobs' own mark: `⠋ N trabajos` while any runs; when none runs, `✓ N` in green and `✗ N` in red for the jobs that ended that this client has not seen, which opening the `trabajos` tab ends. A job that failed SHALL NOT count in `avisos N` (RAT-80).
- REQ-657 WHILE a workspace is `removing`, THE terminal client SHALL draw its row with a blue spinner at its end and the name dim, and SHALL answer any key that would act on it (`d`, `x`, `s`, `n`, `r` to rename, `↵`, a split) with the line `ya se está quitando`; WHEN its removal failed and the job is not dismissed, the row SHALL be as usual with a red `✗` at its end.
- REQ-658 THE terminal client SHALL NOT wait for the daemon in the thread that draws for a removal, for the question `d` (`check_workspace`, the `uncommitted` diff) or for a push: each runs in a worker with its own connection and its answer comes through a channel read at each tick, as `spawn_sync` does (RAT-81). WHEN a job begins by this client, the bar SHALL say `quitando <nombre>…`; WHEN a job it saw run ends, it SHALL say `quitado <nombre> · 4,2 s` (green) or `✗ no se pudo quitar <nombre>` (red).
- REQ-659 THE phone's bell SHALL open «avisos» with two tabs, `alertas` (what it showed) and `trabajos`: under `corriendo` the jobs that run, with a spinner and their time live, and under `terminados` the ones that ended (`✓`, `✗`), with how long they took and how long ago, each a row of at least 56 dp; a tap on a job SHALL open a sheet with its result or its whole error and what fits it: `cancelar` (only a job that is `cancellable`), `ir al workspace` (when it is there, and for a removal only when it failed), `descartar` (one that ended), `cerrar`; `limpiar` in the header of the tab SHALL dismiss every one that ended. The bell's badge SHALL count only alerts and the jobs SHALL have a mark of their own on the bell: a spinner while any runs, then a green or red dot for what ended and was not shown, which showing the tab ends (the record of what was seen is the phone's own, per daemon epoch); the bell SHALL open on the tab that has something (alerts first). There SHALL be no strip under Home's title and no notification of the system (the phone is a quick connection; the PC is where the state is).
- REQ-660 WHILE a workspace is `removing`, THE phone's row of it SHALL show a blue spinner and `quitando…` with the name dim and a tap SHALL say `ya se está quitando` and open nothing; WHEN its removal failed and the job is not dismissed, the row SHALL show a red `✗`.
- REQ-661 WHEN the person confirms the sheet of removing a workspace, THE phone SHALL close it at once, send `remove_workspace`, and leave the outcome to the jobs SHALL say `quitando <nombre>… míralo en avisos › trabajos` as a notice over Home for a few seconds, and WHEN a removal ends SHALL say `✓ quitado <nombre> · 4,2 s` or `✗ no se pudo quitar <nombre>` the same way (an answer that is an error, such as `conflict`, is said as a notice too); the sheet SHALL NOT wait for git.
- REQ-662 WHEN the terminal client opens a workspace, it SHALL record that workspace (its id and its path) in `<state>/tui-last.json`, written whole to a temporary file and renamed, only when what is to be recorded differs from what it recorded last (not at each tick); IF the write fails, THEN the client SHALL go on without saying so. It SHALL NOT record that no workspace is open (`esc`, moving the highlight, a new epoch): the file says the last workspace that was opened.
- REQ-663 WHEN the terminal client starts and the file names a workspace, it SHALL keep the highlight on the first workspace and open nothing until the session node arrives; WHEN it arrives, the client SHALL select and open the workspace whose id is the recorded one or, when none has it, the one whose path is the recorded one, with the first panel focused.
- REQ-664 IF the file is missing, empty or not understood, or the session has no such workspace, THEN the terminal client SHALL start as before (the first workspace highlighted, none opened) without an error or a message, and a recorded workspace SHALL NOT be looked for again once the session has arrived. A workspace the client asked to be made (`goto`) SHALL win over the recorded one.
- REQ-670 WHEN the person presses option-backspace (or option and ctrl, or ctrl alone) in a text field of the terminal client, THE client SHALL erase the word before the cursor, whichever of the encodings below the terminal sends: `esc DEL` and `CSI 127;3u`, `127;5u`, `127;7u` (backspace with alt, with ctrl or with both), `esc BS` (`h` with ctrl and alt) and `CSI 8;3u`, `8;5u`, `8;7u` (the character `\x08` with alt, ctrl or both); `BS` alone and `DEL` alone SHALL stay a backspace of one character, and a plain ctrl-h SHALL stay what it was (it is not a word): ctrl-backspace erases a word only where the terminal tells it from ctrl-h, which is with the keyboard protocol (`CSI 127;5u`).
- REQ-671 THE terminal client SHALL say that in one place, before any field reads the key (the loop of the terminal client and the one of `jw claude`): every encoding of REQ-670 that erases a word becomes backspace with alt and nothing else, which is what the composer already took and what `esc DEL` is; THE rule of what a word is SHALL be one function, the composer's own (spaces bound it, the spaces after it go with it, by characters), used by the composer and by the fields that are a `String`.
- REQ-672 WHEN the keyboard is in the shell of a pane, THE client SHALL send `esc DEL` for each encoding of REQ-670 that erases a word, and `DEL` for `BS` alone.
- REQ-673 THE fields that were a `pop()` SHALL erase a word as the composer does: the name of a rename and the branch typed to confirm a removal (`r`, `d`), the name of a new workspace (a name picked from the list goes whole, as with backspace), the search of the folder browser (with nothing typed it goes up a folder, as backspace does), the line of the project screen being typed, the filter of the lists of the composer (the leader's) and of the file tree, the search of the pull requests, of the Markdown viewer and of the file viewer, and the line of the plain terminal's `claude` (`jw claude`).
- REQ-680 WHEN shift+↑ or shift+↓ is pressed in the composer's field, THE client SHALL move the cursor one visible row up or down and select what it crosses (the selection begins where the cursor was, or keeps its start), as shift+← and shift+→ do by character and alt+shift+← and alt+shift+→ by word. A visible row is what the field draws (`field::wrap`, REQ-254): it breaks at `\n` and every `text_width` characters, so ↑ and ↓ follow the rows that are seen and not only the line breaks.
- REQ-681 THE cursor SHALL keep its column as far as the row it goes to reaches (there is no column that is remembered); WHEN it is on the first row shift+↑ SHALL select to the start of the text, and on the last row shift+↓ to its end; shift+↑ and shift+↓ SHALL NOT scroll the list.
- REQ-682 WHEN ↑ or ↓ is pressed with no shift while a selection is active, THE field SHALL clear it and move the cursor one visible row from where the cursor is; ↑ on the first row SHALL clear it and recall the previous message and ↓ on the last row, while one is recalled, the next (REQ-714); ↑ on the first row with nothing to recall and ↓ on the last row with nothing recalled SHALL clear it and scroll the list one line, as they did (changed by N166).
- REQ-683 WHEN shift+Home or shift+End is pressed, THE field SHALL select to the start or the end of the line (as far as the `\n`), the selection that shift+← gives; a key with no shift SHALL clear it.
- REQ-684 THE `session` node SHALL carry `groups`, a list absent when empty (additive: `v` stays 1), in the order they are shown, of `{id, name}`; THE summary of a workspace in the session SHALL carry `group` (a group's id), absent when it has none; THE contract SHALL have the commands `create_group`, `rename_group`, `delete_group`, `move_to_group` and `move_group`, with schema, fixtures (one for each command, a session with groups, and invalid cases of shape: `move_group` without `delta`, a `group` that is a number) and the Kotlin types in the same part (N164a); the rules of a name are the daemon's (REQ-686), not the schema's (a name is trimmed first), and `move_repo` comes with its schema, fixture and Kotlin type in N164e (REQ-702).
- REQ-685 THE daemon SHALL keep the group of a workspace under its key, which is its `project` when it has one (REQ-222) and its own path when it has not, in `session.json`, as a field that an older file lacks (it loads as no groups; `v` of the file stays 1); WHEN a workspace is closed THE entry SHALL stay, and WHEN a workspace with that key is opened THE daemon SHALL put it in that group IF the group still exists.
- REQ-686 WHEN `create_group` arrives, THE daemon SHALL trim the name, refuse with `bad_request` one that is empty or over 40 characters and with `conflict` one that is another group's without regard to case, and otherwise add the group last, answer `{group}` and refresh once; THE same SHALL hold for `rename_group`, which refuses an unknown group with `not_found`.
- REQ-687 WHEN `delete_group` arrives, THE daemon SHALL remove the group and every entry that names it and refresh once; THE workspaces of the group SHALL stay open, with all their panes, and SHALL be in no group; an unknown group is `not_found`.
- REQ-688 WHEN `move_to_group` arrives, THE daemon SHALL set the group of the workspace's key (every workspace that has it moves: a repository's root and its worktrees), or remove it WHEN `group` is absent, and refresh once; an unknown workspace or group is `not_found`; the workspace that is moved SHALL keep its panes, layout and attention.
- REQ-689 WHEN `move_group` arrives with `delta` -1 or 1, THE daemon SHALL swap the group with its neighbour and refresh once; AT either end it SHALL change nothing and answer ok; any other `delta` is `bad_request`.
- REQ-690 THE `session` node SHALL list the workspaces of each group together, the groups in their order, inside each group as REQ-219 says (each repository's workspaces together, root first, repositories in the order of their first workspace), and the workspaces that are in no group last, as REQ-219 says; WHERE no group exists THE list SHALL be what it was before N164a.
- REQ-691 THE terminal client SHALL draw, WHERE a group exists, a row for each group (`▾` open or `▸` folded, its name, and when open the number of its workspaces at the right) and under an open group its workspaces and headings two columns further in than REQ-223 says; a group that is empty SHALL show `vacío`; the workspaces that are in no group SHALL come last under the heading `sin grupo`, two columns in, WHERE a group exists and one of them does; WHERE no group exists THE sidebar SHALL be as REQ-219 to REQ-223 say. THE row of a group SHALL be highlighted and moved onto like a workspace's, with no number; a heading SHALL still be none of those.
- REQ-692 WHEN a group is folded, THE terminal client SHALL draw at the right of its row what its workspaces say: a blue spinner and the number of those that work or are being removed, then one mark for each kind of attention they have, the most urgent first, with the number when it is over one, at most three kinds and `+n` for the rest, in the colours of the marks of a workspace row (`!` red, `?` yellow, `◆` magenta, `✗` red) and the name clipped to make room; `avisos N` SHALL count every attention, folded or not.
- REQ-693 THE terminal client SHALL move the cursor over the group rows and the workspaces that are drawn, a folded group's workspaces being skipped; WHEN the cursor is on a workspace of a group that is folded (by `h`, `z` or a click), it SHALL go to the group's row; WHEN a workspace is chosen from «avisos», by `↵` or by a click, THE client SHALL unfold its group; the numbers drawn SHALL follow the workspaces that are drawn and SHALL NOT be keys; WHERE the rows are more than the sidebar has room for, the list SHALL move just far enough to keep the cursor's row in view, as a function of that row alone, and a click SHALL find the row that was drawn at its place; while the cursor is on a group's row, no key SHALL act on a workspace (`selected_id()` is none), and the place of the panels SHALL say what the group's workspaces say.
- REQ-694 THE terminal client SHALL, at the workspace level: unfold or fold the group under the cursor with `↵` (and a click on its row), fold it with `h` (on a workspace, `h` goes to its group) and unfold it with `l`, and fold or unfold all with `z`; ask with `g` for the group of the workspace under the cursor, in a list of the groups with `1` to `9`, `0` for none and `n` for a new one that contains it, and send `move_to_group`; ask with `G` for the name of a new group and send `create_group`; on a group's row, `r` SHALL rename it and `x` SHALL ask before it sends `delete_group`, the question saying how many workspaces become «sin grupo» and that none is closed; `J` and `K` SHALL send `move_group` on a group's row and `move_repo` on a workspace's; a refusal SHALL be said by the daemon's own words in a box (`Manage::Failed`). WHEN a workspace is moved, THE destination group SHALL open in this terminal and the cursor SHALL stay on the workspace; WHEN a group is made, the cursor SHALL go to it as soon as the session lists it (and give up after 8 s); `g` on a group's row SHALL say where to stand and send nothing; `J` and `K` at either end of the groups SHALL say so and send nothing, and on a workspace's row they SHALL send `move_repo` for its repository (REQ-702), after saying and sending nothing when it is first or last of its group (or of those in none) or when the workspace is a folder that is not a repository, the cursor following the workspace when the list changes order; a name typed has at most 40 letters and an empty one waits; WHERE the session has groups and no workspace, the cursor SHALL be on the first group. None of these keys SHALL use Alt.
- REQ-695 THE terminal client SHALL say in «avisos» the group of an alert before the workspace's name, as `grupo › workspace`, WHERE the workspace has one (the verb stays at the right of the row, and two worktrees of one repository still read differently); THE list of keys `?` SHALL list `g`, `G`, `h`, `l`, `z`, `J`, `K` and what `r` and `x` do on a group; WHILE the cursor is on a group's row THE bar SHALL be the chip `GRUPO` with the keys of a group (`↵`, `h` `l`, `z`, `r`, `x`, `J` `K`, `G`), and the bar of a workspace SHALL stay as it was (it has no room for more: `?` has them).
- REQ-696 THE terminal client SHALL keep which groups are folded in its own memory; it SHALL send nothing about it, a daemon that restarts or another client SHALL not change it, and a group the daemon no longer lists SHALL be forgotten.
- REQ-697 THE phone SHALL draw Home with a row for each group (its name, the number of its workspaces or, when folded, what REQ-692 says in its own marks), the workspaces under it, a tap on the row folding or unfolding it, and a long press opening the sheet of the group; WHERE no group exists THE phone SHALL draw Home as before, with one line more at its end, «＋ grupo nuevo», which is how the first group is made; what is under a group's row or under «sin grupo» SHALL be one step further in, a group that is open and empty SHALL say `vacío`, and the number before a workspace's row SHALL be its place among the rows that are drawn (a folded group leaves no gap; the number is a label and no key); THE sheet of the workspace's own screen (a long press on its title) SHALL offer «mover a un grupo…» too.
- REQ-698 THE sheet of a long press on a workspace SHALL have «mover a un grupo…» for every kind (REQ-227), saying where the workspace is and whether the move takes the repository (root and worktrees: how many) or only it; THE sheet it opens SHALL list the groups with a mark on the current one, «sin grupo» and «＋ grupo nuevo…» (a name field and «crear y mover»), and SHALL send `move_to_group` and close once the PC has moved it (the group the workspace is put in SHALL open on the phone when the group is tapped, before the move is sent, and stay open if the move fails; one made with «crear y mover» reads the id of the new group from the reply of `create_group` and sends `move_to_group` after it); THE sheet of a group SHALL rename it (a field and «guardar»), move it up and down (`move_group`, a button disabled at its end) and ask before it deletes it (REQ-687's consequence in words); a refusal SHALL be said in the sheet.
- REQ-699 THE phone's «avisos» SHALL show, over the list of alerts, a filter of chips (`todos`, one for each group with its count, `sin grupo`) and each alert SHALL say its group before the workspace's name (`grupo › workspace`) WHERE it has one, the chips SHALL be there only WHERE the PC has groups, and going to a workspace from an alert SHALL open its group on the phone; THE bell SHALL count every alert whatever the filter.
- REQ-700 THE phone SHALL keep which groups are folded on the phone for each PC it is paired with, apart from the record of what was seen (that one goes with the daemon's epoch, this one does not), send nothing about it, and forget a group only when the PC it is connected to has sent its session and does not list it (the chip of «avisos» that named it goes back to «todos»).
- REQ-701 THE binary SHALL have `group ls`, `group new`, `group rename`, `group rm` and `group move`, which reach the daemon as the other agents' commands do and send the commands of REQ-686 to REQ-688; `ls` SHALL print each workspace's group (the name, or `-`) as a new last column, so the columns that were there keep their place, and, with `--json`, a `group` field (the name) WHERE it has one; `<group>` in the commands SHALL be an id or a name, matched without regard to case (REQ-686 makes names unique so); `skill install` SHALL teach them.
- REQ-702 WHEN `move_repo` arrives with `delta` -1 or 1, THE daemon SHALL swap the repository's block of workspaces with the neighbouring block of the same group (or of «sin grupo»), keep the order inside each block and refresh once; AT either end it SHALL change nothing and answer ok; THE order SHALL survive a restart; THE repositories of a group are then in the order they were moved to, not of their first workspace (this amends REQ-219); a loose folder is no block (REQ-690 keeps them last), so `move_repo` of one is `bad_request` and the terminal client says «una carpeta va al final y no se ordena» without sending it; THE contract of the command (schema, fixture, Kotlin type) comes with this requirement (N164e).
- REQ-703 WHILE the field of a pane (the composer of a conversation, `conversation.rs`, and the line of a shell pane drawn as blocks, REQ-254) holds text, THE terminal client SHALL grow its band with the text up to a limit of rows of text that depends on the pane's inner height `ih`: `min(max(5, ih / 3), ih − 11)`, never below 1. The 11 is the foot row, the four rows of the band that are not text (REQ-254), the line of work and the blank row over it (counted whether or not a turn runs, so the band does not move when one starts or ends) and the four rows the list keeps; 16 rows give 5, 24 give 8, 36 give 12 and 12 give 1. The list SHALL give those rows up as REQ-256 says; the checklist's strip is not counted and yields as it does. A shell pane has no line of work and counts it all the same, so the two fields grow alike.
- REQ-704 WHEN the text wraps to more rows than the limit, THE field SHALL draw a window of that many rows at an offset that the client keeps between frames (the composer's in its `Ui`, a `Cell` beside `text_width`, RAT-87; a shell pane's in the `View`, for each pane): a frame moves the offset the least that puts the cursor's row in the window (up to the cursor's row when it is above, down until it is the last row when it is below) and keeps it within `0 ..= rows − limit`; a text that fits has offset 0. THE window SHALL be a function of the stored offset, the text, the cursor and the pane's size alone, and only the frame's draw SHALL store the offset, so every place that reads the field (the draw, a click, a double click, `index_at`) sees the rows the last frame drew (RISK-85).
- REQ-705 WHEN ↑ or ↓ moves the cursor inside the text (REQ-680, REQ-682), THE window SHALL follow it by REQ-704, a row at a time; ↑ on the first row of the text and ↓ on its last SHALL do what REQ-682 and REQ-714 say (recall, or scroll the list), whatever rows the window shows (the first row is the text's, not the window's). In a shell pane the keys are the shell's and the window follows the cursor that the shell reports in `input`.
- REQ-706 WHEN the text has more rows than the window, THE field SHALL draw, in the last column of the pane's inner box (inside the columns the field keeps free for the marks, so the width the text wraps to does not change), at the right of the window's rows, a bar of dim `│` with a thumb of blue `┃` whose length and place follow the window (`max(1, round(v² / n))` rows long for a window of `v` of `n` rows, at `round(offset / (n − v) × (v − length))`; where the bar has two spare rows or more, the thumb touches the top only at offset 0 and the bottom only at the last offset), and, ending one column before the bar's, on the row of the chips, `↑N ↓M` in yellow (`N` rows of text above the window, `M` below, both always written); THE chips SHALL be cut before that count and not drawn under it (RISK-86); WHEN all the text fits, neither SHALL be drawn.
- REQ-707 WHEN the wheel turns over a row of text of the field's window, the text has more rows than the window and nothing is open over the band (a list, a menu, `ask`), THE terminal client SHALL move the window one row for each notch, within `0 ..= rows − limit`, without moving the cursor, instead of scrolling the list (a peek, held like the offset; the cursor is not drawn while it is out of the window); ANY key that reaches the field (in a shell pane, any key sent to the pane), a click in the field and a change of the text (of `input`, for a shell pane) SHALL end the peek and bring the window back to the cursor by REQ-704, from the offset the last frame stored and not from the peek's. Over the chips' row, the free rows of the band, the list, or when the text fits, the wheel SHALL scroll the list as REQ-411 says, and a shell pane's blocks as REQ-68 says. (The terminal client already receives the wheel with its position: `scroll(down, x, y, …)` in `tui.rs`; today `scroll_pane` and `Ui::scroll_by` look at the pane and not at the row, and N165d gives the composer the same test of the band that a click already has.)
- REQ-708 THE field of a shell pane SHALL have what REQ-703 to REQ-707 give the composer, with what is the shell's: its text is `input`, so the offset and the peek are the client's and kept for each pane (not the daemon's, and not in the node); WHILE a command runs, and WHERE the pane carries no `input` and the field shows the line of the screen (REQ-258), the field is one row and has no window, bar or count.
- REQ-709 WHEN text is pasted into the composer of a conversation (`ConvUi::paste`, bracketed paste, line ends normalised to `\n`) and it has five rows or more (a final `\n` does not count a row) or 300 characters or more, THE composer SHALL put in the line one **fold** in its place and not the text: a label `[Texto pegado #n +N líneas «comienzo…»]` for a text of several rows (`N` its rows), `[Texto pegado #n N caracteres «comienzo…»]` for one row; `comienzo` is its first row that is not blank, trimmed and cut to 16 characters (the `…` only when it was cut); `n` counts the folds of the message from 1 and is not reused inside it. A shorter paste SHALL go in as it does now, character by character. THE fields that are not the composer's (a shell pane's, the name fields, the filters) SHALL NOT fold what is pasted into them.
- REQ-710 A fold SHALL be one character of the composer: ← and → cross it in one step (alt+←, alt+→ and alt+Backspace take it as any character that is not a space), Backspace and Delete erase it whole, a selection takes it whole, a double click on its label selects it and nothing else (REQ-637), and `Home`/`End` and ↑/↓ count it by the width of its label, the column kept in cells (REQ-711). WHEN Tab is pressed with the cursor on a fold or just after it, and no menu, file list, suggestion (REQ-572) or open agents area with room (REQ-601) takes the Tab, THE composer SHALL replace the label by its text, the cursor after it; the other folds keep their numbers.
- REQ-711 THE label SHALL be drawn in yellow (reversed while the cursor is on it), with the width of its text, and SHALL never be split between two rows: one that does not fit what is left of a row starts the next. WHEN the label is wider than `text_width` (a pane that is split), THE composer SHALL drop the beginning, then the count (`[Texto pegado #n]`), then cut it to the width ending in `…]`. THE rows, the cursor, a click (`index_at`) and the window of REQ-704 SHALL be worked out from these widths by one helper that the six places that build `Ui::field` share (RISK-88); the field of a shell pane keeps the width of a character (REQ-708).
- REQ-712 WHEN the message is sent, THE client SHALL send the text of the folds in place of their labels (`text` is what the person pasted, whole), the images the line still names (`[Imagen#n]`) as now, and `folds` with the characters of the sent text that each fold covers; THE numbers of the folds SHALL start again at 1 with the next message, WHEN the field is emptied and WHEN a message is put in it (REQ-714), in the order they have in the line. (The `[Imagen#n]` counter is not reset by a send and the image is not a fold: OPEN-44.) A character of the private-use block (`U+E000` to `U+F8FF`) in what is typed or pasted SHALL be written as `U+FFFD`, because the composer keeps a fold as one of them (RAT-89, RISK-87).
- REQ-713 WHEN the text of a message of the person has more than four rows in the list of a conversation of the terminal client, THE list SHALL draw its first three and, dim, `… +N filas más` (`N` the rows left out); four rows or fewer SHALL be drawn whole (a fold of one row would take the room of the row it hides). The phone SHALL keep drawing the message whole (REQ-718). (`Item::User` is drawn whole today, `conversation.rs`, with `wrap`; there is no reading of the rest: OPEN-45.)
- REQ-714 WHEN ↑ is pressed with no shift, the cursor is on the first row of the text, no menu, file list or picked item takes the key and there is a message to recall, THE composer SHALL keep the draft (its text, its folds and its cursor) if none is kept, put the message before the one shown (the last one, the first time) in the field with its folds as they were sent (REQ-712's `folds`) and the cursor at the start, so that another ↑ goes further back; at the oldest message ↑ SHALL do nothing. WHEN ↓ is pressed with no shift on the last row while a message is recalled, THE composer SHALL put the next one in the field with the cursor at the end and, past the newest, the draft, which ends the recall. Shift+↑ and shift+↓ keep REQ-680. Anything the person edits in a recalled message is dropped when ↑ or ↓ leaves it (OPEN-46); sending a message ends the recall and the sent text becomes the newest message. THE list SHALL keep PageUp and PageDown (a page, `PAGE`) and the wheel (REQ-411, REQ-707) to scroll; ↑ and ↓ scroll it one line only where REQ-682 says.
- REQ-715 WHILE a message is recalled, THE composer SHALL show `↑ i/n` (`i` of `n` messages, 1 the oldest) as one more chip of the chips' row, after those that are there and cut like them before the count `↑N ↓M` of REQ-706, which is at the other end of the row (the prototype drew it at the right end, where the count is, N166c).
- REQ-716 WHEN the daemon takes a `conversation_send` of a conversation whose folder (`cwd`) is in a repository, it SHALL append the message to `sent/<key>.json` of `identity::state_dir()` (`key` the repository's, as `project.rs` makes it, so a worktree and its main folder share one), written whole by a temporary file renamed, mode 0600 in a 0700 directory, keeping the last 100, oldest first, as `{v: 1, entries: [{text, folds?}]}`; it SHALL NOT keep a message that is empty, is `private` (the client trims the line it sends, so only it sees the space or the tab that begins it, and says so; `prompt` sends every message `private`, because an agent wrote it and not the person), is over 20 KB (20 480 bytes of `text`) or is the same as the last one kept; THE client SHALL apply the same rules to the list it keeps in memory (REQ-714), and a file it cannot read SHALL be moved to `<key>.json.bad` and not overwritten (as `session.json` is). A conversation always has a folder (the engine's), and one that is in no repository has the key of its path (`project::key`); the key of a folder is worked out once. `conversation_history {node}` SHALL reply `{messages}`, the entries of the repository of that conversation; THE terminal client SHALL ask it once for each conversation it holds, as soon as it has the conversation's `ConvUi` and not at the first ↑ (the answer is not waited for), and put the answer before what was sent while it was awaited, without repeating a message that the file already had; an answer that comes while a message is recalled SHALL wait for the recall to end, so that the `i/n` does not move; with no answer the list holds only what was sent since the client opened. The images are not kept: a message that named one (`[Imagen#n]`) comes back with the marker as text, as it was sent.
- REQ-717 WHEN ^R is pressed in the composer of a conversation and there is a message to recall, THE terminal client SHALL open over the band the list of the composer's own (`Pop::List`, as `/resume`'s) with the messages, newest first, a row being the message on one line, `⏎` for a break, with `N líneas` at the right when it has several, and its filter: letters typed filter by what the text contains, with no regard to case; ↑ and ↓ move through it as they do in every list (the newest is at the top, so ↓ goes to an older one), Enter closes the list and puts the chosen one in the field **as ↑ would have** (the draft kept, `↑ i/n`, REQ-714, the folds as they were sent), Esc closes it and leaves the field as it was. ^R is a key the composer and the layers over it leave free (it reaches the shell of a pane, whose own search it is, only where the shell holds the focus).
- REQ-718 THE phone SHALL stay as it is: it neither folds, nor recalls, nor lists the messages, and draws a message of the person whole; `folds` and `conversation_history` are in the contract for it to use later (OPEN-48).
- REQ-719 THE `workspace` node SHALL carry `views`, absent when empty, in the order the views were made.
- REQ-720 WHEN `open_view` has `kind: diff`, THE daemon SHALL reply `{view}` of the workspace's diff view, making it only if there is none.
- REQ-721 WHEN `open_view` has `kind: viewer` and a `path`, THE daemon SHALL add the path as a tab of the workspace's viewer (none twice; at most 8, the oldest dropped), making the viewer if there is none.
- REQ-722 WHEN the daemon makes a view, it SHALL place it where a view of that kind was closed last (the same neighbour, side and share) if the neighbour is still in the layout, else beside `beside` on `dir` with half, else at the right edge of the whole layout with half the width.
- REQ-723 WHEN `close_view` names a view, THE daemon SHALL remove it and its leaf (the sibling takes its place) and remember its place; with a `path` it SHALL remove that tab, and the last tab closes the view.
- REQ-724 THE daemon SHALL accept the id of a view wherever `swap_panes` and `grow_pane` take a pane's.
- REQ-725 THE daemon SHALL NOT save views, their leaves or the remembered places; IF it restarts, THEN the layout SHALL agree with the panes alone.
- REQ-726 WHEN `review_pr` makes a review workspace, THE daemon SHALL open its diff view beside the agent once the agent's pane is there (amends REQ-589); the clients start it on the `general` view, which the workspace's `review` says.
- REQ-727 WHEN a workspace is removed, its views and its remembered places SHALL go with it.
- REQ-728 THE terminal client SHALL draw every leaf of the layout as a panel with the same border, focus, zoom, size and swap keys; no overlay or side column SHALL hold the diff or the viewer (amends REQ-104, REQ-471 and REQ-590).
- REQ-729 WHEN `d` is pressed, THE terminal client SHALL open the diff view and focus it, or focus it if it exists, or close it if it has the focus; it SHALL NOT give it the keys.
- REQ-730 WHEN `↵` is pressed on a view, THE terminal client SHALL give it the keys; `^␣` and `esc` SHALL give them back (`esc` no longer closes).
- REQ-731 WHILE the keys are in the diff, `↵` or `o` on a file of the views `1` or `3` SHALL open it as a tab of the viewer, beside the diff on `Row` if the diff is at least 80 columns wide and on `Col` if not, with the focus and the keys; `q` SHALL close the view (closes OPEN-16).
- REQ-732 WHEN a file of the tree `t` is opened, THE terminal client SHALL do the same beside the focused panel.
- REQ-733 WHILE the keys are in a viewer, `Tab` SHALL change the tab and `q` SHALL close it (the last closes the view); `w` stays the wrap.
- REQ-734 WHEN `x` is pressed on a view, THE terminal client SHALL close it without asking.
- REQ-735 WHEN `S` is pressed, THE terminal client SHALL show a digit on each panel in the order of the layout, swap the focused panel with the one of the digit pressed and cancel on `esc`.
- REQ-736 WHEN `f` is pressed, THE focused panel, a view or a pane, SHALL fill the area; `z` SHALL be retired; opening a view or changing workspace SHALL end the zoom.
- REQ-737 IF opening a view or a split would leave a panel under 14 columns or 5 rows, THEN THE terminal client SHALL not do it and SHALL say «no cabe».
- REQ-738 WHILE the terminal is under 64 columns, THE terminal client SHALL show only the focused panel with a strip of titles above it, and `h`, `l` and `Tab` SHALL change the panel.
- REQ-739 WHILE a diff view has no changes, it SHALL say so and stay open and fill when changes arrive; a diff view SHALL never close itself.
- REQ-740 THE phone SHALL show a workspace's panes and then its views as the tabs of `PaneTabs`; a view's tab SHALL have a ✕ that sends `close_view`.
- REQ-741 WHEN a file is tapped in a diff tab, THE phone SHALL send `open_view` and select the new tab; a view the phone opens SHALL appear in the layout of the terminal client.
- REQ-742 THE phone SHALL NOT keep a separate changes screen; the viewer dialogs of the folder screen stay (OPEN-50).
- REQ-743 WHEN the engine asks the person to choose (REQ-435), THE terminal client, THE phone and the plain-terminal `claude` SHALL offer, after the options, a last row «Otra…»; a permission and a plan SHALL NOT have it.
- REQ-744 WHILE a question waits in the terminal client and its field is not focused, `↓` SHALL reach «Otra…», the number after the last option and `↵` or `space` on it SHALL focus the field without answering, and every other key SHALL do what REQ-404 says.
- REQ-745 WHILE the field of «Otra…» is focused, THE terminal client SHALL write every printable key (digits, `y`, `a`, `n` and space included) and the composer's editing keys (`⌥⌫`, `←`, `→`, `Home`, `End`, `Delete`) into it, drawn on the row of «Otra…» (up to three rows, the cursor in view), the options staying drawn.
- REQ-746 WHILE the field is focused, `↵` SHALL answer; IF its text is empty after trimming, THEN nothing SHALL be sent and the row SHALL say «escribe algo, o esc vuelve»; `esc` SHALL go back to the rows keeping the text, and `^C` SHALL interrupt (REQ-603).
- REQ-747 WHERE the question is multi, «Otra…» SHALL have a box, marked while its text is not empty; `↵` on the row of an option SHALL send the marked options with that text, `↵` in the field the same, and with nothing marked and no text nothing SHALL be sent.
- REQ-748 WHEN the next question of the same call arrives, THE clients SHALL start it with the first row lit, nothing marked and no text.
- REQ-749 WHEN the person answers with text, THE client SHALL send `conversation_answer` with `key` the picked indices joined by `,` (empty when none) and `text` the trimmed text.
- REQ-750 WHEN `conversation_answer` for a question has a `text` that is not empty after trimming, THE daemon SHALL take it as the person's own answer, as in the Contract; IF the `key` has an index out of range, or an index together with `text` in a single choice, or `key` and `text` are both empty, THEN it SHALL refuse it as REQ-385 does and keep the question waiting.
- REQ-751 WHEN a question is answered with text, THE tool's line SHALL read `Respondida: …` with the text between «» in the place of a label (`Respondida: Rust, «Zig» · B`), clipped as today, and its source as REQ-405.
- REQ-752 WHEN a question shows in the phone's sheet, THE sheet SHALL end its options with a 48 px row «✎ Otra…» that opens, in place of the options, a card with a field, «Volver» (keeps the text) and «Enviar» (off while the text is empty), the card saying that it goes with what is marked when the question is multi; the answer is REQ-749.
- REQ-753 WHEN `read` or `prompt --wait` print a question, THE binary SHALL print after its options `  N) Otra… (escribe tu respuesta)`; WHILE `claude` runs in a terminal the client does not draw, choosing that number SHALL ask «tu respuesta:» on a line and send it as REQ-749.
- REQ-754 WHEN the engine of a conversation is about to start (a new conversation, a resumed one, one restored after the daemon restarted, or the next message after the engine ended), THE daemon SHALL look, with no lock held, for `<session>.jsonl` under `projects/` of the Claude configuration folder of the environment the engine runs with, and SHALL start the engine with `--resume <session>` when it exists and with `--session-id <session>` only when it does not, whatever `has_transcript` says; a `--session-id` the person typed for a file that exists is `--resume` too (B-28: «Session ID … is already in use»).
- REQ-755 WHEN what that look finds differs from `has_transcript`, THE daemon SHALL set `has_transcript` to what it found, SHALL record `claude --resume <session>` as the pane's resume when it found the transcript (as the end of a turn does), and, IF `has_transcript` said the transcript was there and the file is not, THEN SHALL add a note that says the conversation's transcript was not found and that it starts again with the same id and nothing of before (RAT-108).
- REQ-756 WHEN the person sends `/reload-plugins` or `/reload-skills` (typed, queued, or sent by an agent with `jw prompt`) and the turn that line starts ends with its `result`, THE daemon SHALL send the engine `initialize` again, once, with the id of REQ-458, and WHEN the answer succeeds and has a `commands` list THE conversation's `commands` SHALL become that list as REQ-417 says (and its jwn entries as before), so the skills and commands a plugin brought show in the `/` list without the engine starting again; the request is not sent with the line (the reload has not run yet and the answer would be the old list), a message that waits goes after it, and a turn that is running is never interrupted by it (the engine answers a request of control in the middle of a turn at once and the turn goes on).
- REQ-757 IF the answer to that second `initialize` is an `error`, or a success with no `commands` list, THEN the conversation SHALL keep the list it had, and the client SHALL see no note, no notice and no change (the person only finds the old list, as before).
- REQ-758 WHEN the daemon keeps the list in `commands.json` (REQ-593), it SHALL take it from the conversation whose list the engine said last, not from whichever has an engine first, so that a conversation that has not reloaded does not put the old list back in the file after one that did.
  Nothing a client draws changes: no new field, node, notice or key (the contract is the same); the `/` box shows, after a reload, the commands the engine now has (REQ-416, REQ-596).
- REQ-759 WHEN `artifact_publish` arrives with a `name` of 1 to 64 characters without `/`, a `format` of `html` or `md` and a `text` of up to 512 KiB (524 288 bytes), THE daemon SHALL store it as the next version, `n` from 1, of the artifact of that `name` in that workspace (making the artifact when it is new), keep it unchanged, and reply `{id, n, hash, new: true}`.
- REQ-760 IF the text is over 512 KiB, or the name is empty, longer than 64 characters or has a `/`, or the name belongs to an artifact of the other format, THEN THE daemon SHALL refuse with `bad_request`, the message saying which, and SHALL store nothing; IF the workspace is unknown, THEN with `not_found`.
- REQ-761 WHEN the bytes equal those of the latest version, THE daemon SHALL NOT make a version and SHALL reply that version with `new: false`.
- REQ-762 THE daemon SHALL keep at most 20 versions of an artifact, dropping the oldest and never the latest.
- REQ-763 WHEN a workspace is removed or `artifact_rm` names an artifact, THE daemon SHALL delete its versions from disk, its node entry and, if no pending artifact is left, its attention.
- REQ-764 WHILE a workspace has an artifact whose latest version has no verdict, THE daemon SHALL hold one attention of kind `artifact` on that workspace; WHEN the last such verdict is given or the artifact is removed, THE daemon SHALL end it; WHEN the daemon starts, THE daemon SHALL raise it again for each workspace with a pending artifact, from what is on disk.
- REQ-765 WHEN `open_artifacts` names a workspace, THE daemon SHALL reply the `node` of its `artifacts` (empty when it has none), and WHEN a version is published, a verdict is given or an artifact is removed, THE daemon SHALL patch that node for every client that has it open.
- REQ-766 WHEN `artifact_read` names an artifact and a version, THE daemon SHALL reply `ArtifactDoc` with `html` (a `html` artifact) or `doc` (a `md` one); IF the reply would pass the bridge's 1 MiB message (REQ-79), THEN `bad_request`.
- REQ-767 WHEN `artifact_decide` arrives, THE daemon SHALL record the verdict on version `n` only if `n` is the latest, `hash` is its hash and it has no verdict yet; IF `n` is not the latest or the hash differs, THEN `conflict` («stale»); IF it already has a verdict, THEN `conflict` («decided»); IF the id is unknown, THEN `not_found`.
- REQ-768 IF `type` is `changes` and `text` is empty or has only blanks, THEN THE daemon SHALL refuse with `bad_request`; IF `text` is over 4 KiB, THEN with `bad_request`; an `approved` verdict SHALL take no text, and with one it is `bad_request`.
- REQ-769 WHEN a verdict is recorded and the pane that published still runs a conversation, THE daemon SHALL send it, as `jw prompt` sends to a conversation and as a private message (not kept in the composer's history, REQ-716), one message, `[jw] artefacto «<title>» v<n> (<hash6>): APROBADO desde el teléfono.` or `[jw] artefacto «<title>» v<n> (<hash6>): CAMBIOS pedidos desde el teléfono:` and the person's text on the next lines, and record `delivered: agent`.
- REQ-770 IF the pane that published no longer exists, or has no conversation, or the conversation refuses the message, THEN THE daemon SHALL keep the verdict with `delivered: none` and SHALL NOT type it into that pane or send it to any other.
- REQ-771 THE command `jw artifact publish <file> [--name N] [--title T] [--kind prototype|document|report|spec]` SHALL send the file with the workspace of its own pane (`own_pane`) and that pane, take `format` from the extension (`.html`, `.htm`, `.md`; any other is a usage error), `name` from the file's name without extension in lower case, `title` from `<title>` or the first heading or the name, and `kind` `document` by default, print `<id> v<n> <hash6>`, and exit 1 with the daemon's code when it refuses.
- REQ-772 WHEN `jw artifact publish --wait` or `jw artifact wait <id>` runs, THE command SHALL wait for the verdict of the latest version, up to `--timeout` seconds (600 by default, as `jw prompt`), and exit 0 printing `approved`, or exit 4 printing `changes` and the person's text, or exit 1 on the timeout; exit 3 stays «a question is waiting».
- REQ-773 THE commands `jw artifact ls [--json]` and `jw artifact rm <id>` SHALL list, per workspace, the id, name, title, kind, version and status of each artifact, and delete one.
- REQ-774 IF a phone sends `artifact_publish` or `artifact_rm`, THEN THE bridge SHALL answer `unsupported`, as it does for `mobile_*`, and forward every other command as before.
- REQ-775 THE phone SHALL show a `html` artifact only in a WebView with no JavaScript interface, no access to files or to content, network loads blocked, every navigation refused, and a request interceptor that answers every request empty.
- REQ-776 THE page that WebView loads SHALL be the app's own and not the artifact's: first `<meta http-equiv="Content-Security-Policy" content="default-src 'none'; script-src 'unsafe-inline'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; img-src data:; font-src data:; media-src data:; connect-src 'none'; form-action 'none'; base-uri 'none'; object-src 'none'">`, then the artifact as the `srcdoc` of an `<iframe sandbox="allow-scripts">`, written as an attribute value with `&` and `"` escaped, so nothing in the artifact's text can come before or change the policy.
- REQ-777 THE phone SHALL show a `md` artifact with the Markdown viewer (`MarkdownDoc`) and not in a WebView.
- REQ-778 THE phone's verdict bar SHALL be drawn by the app outside the WebView, with the version and the first six characters of the hash it sends in `artifact_decide`; THE button «Aprobar» SHALL ask once more («¿aprobar vN?») before it sends; «Pedir cambios» SHALL open a sheet with a field and send only when it has text.
- REQ-779 WHILE the version shown is not the latest, THE phone SHALL offer only «ver vN» in place of the verdict bar; WHEN `artifact_decide` answers `stale` or `decided`, THE phone SHALL say so and show the node as it is.
- REQ-780 THE workspace screen's header SHALL have, in place of its files and changes icons, one `⋮` button that opens a menu of four rows, «cambiar workspace», «archivos», «cambios» and «artefactos»; WHILE the workspace has a pending artifact, `⋮` SHALL carry a badge with their number and «artefactos» that number, read from the workspace's `artifacts` node, which the phone opens when it shows the workspace.
- REQ-781 THE «artefactos» row SHALL open that workspace's list, pending first and the decided ones folded under «ya decididos», each row with its title, version, kind, size, age and status; a row SHALL open the artifact.
- REQ-782 WHEN the attention `artifact` is tapped (the workspace row of the list, the bell's «avisos», REQ-659), THE phone SHALL open the list of that workspace; the bell's number counts it as any attention.
- REQ-783 WHILE the link is off (REQ-190), THE phone SHALL show the list and the artifacts it has opened before from its own copy, read only, with a notice that says so and the verdict buttons disabled, and SHALL NOT queue a verdict to send later.
- REQ-784 WHEN the device is revoked or the phone unpairs, THE app SHALL delete its copies of artifacts.
- REQ-785 THE phone SHALL NOT offer to publish or delete an artifact.
- REQ-786 WHERE the person is on the terminal client, THE artifacts SHALL NOT be shown there in this part; the attention `artifact` SHALL have its mark and its place in `URGENCY` (OPEN-57).
- REQ-787 THE skill that `jw skill install` writes SHALL say what `jw artifact` does, that a verdict arrives as a message in the pane that published, and that `--wait` returns it.
- REQ-788 THE `artifacts` node, the `ArtifactDoc` and the five commands SHALL have a fixture each and pass the conformance of Rust and Kotlin (`crates/contract/tests/conformance.rs:163`); THE `artifact` kind SHALL be in the schema's attention list and the two counts that check it; THE `artifacts` type SHALL be in `SUPPORTED`, `wire::default_accepts` and the phone's `hello`.
- REQ-789 THE phone's page shell (REQ-776) SHALL be tested with an artifact that starts with `<!-- <head>`, one that carries its own `Content-Security-Policy` meta, and one that has `"` and `&`: in each the policy comes first and the artifact comes back unchanged.
- REQ-790 THE bytes of an artifact SHALL NOT reach the daemon's log, the attention, or a message to a pane other than REQ-769's; only the title, version and hash do.
- REQ-791 WHILE an attention `artifact` is held, `mark_seen` SHALL NOT end it, and THE phone SHALL NOT take it as seen because the person opened the workspace or the list; only REQ-764's end SHALL.
- REQ-792 WHEN the daemon classifies a command the engine lists (REQ-417), THE command SHALL be `claude` when `builtin` is true, else `plugin` with `group` the name before the first `:` when the name has one, else `skill` when its description ends in `(user)`, `project` when it ends in `(project)`, and `plugin` with `group` `claude.ai` when it ends in `(claude.ai sync)`; and THE daemon SHALL keep the description without that ending, and without the `(<group>) ` a plugin's begins with.
- REQ-793 IF a command has no `:`, is not `builtin` and its description ends in none of those three marks, THEN the daemon SHALL tag it as REQ-417 did before N172 (a name `init` does not list as a skill is `project`, any other is `skill`) and SHALL keep its description as it is.
- REQ-794 THE `group` of a command SHALL be absent unless its source is `plugin`, and a client that does not know it SHALL draw the plugin as before (additive: the Contract's rule that unknown fields are ignored).
- REQ-795 WHEN the list of REQ-416 and REQ-446 opens, THE terminal client and the phone SHALL draw its commands in sections, each under a title with its count, in this order: the product's name (`identity.toml`'s `name`: the terminal reads it from the `identity` crate, the phone's build puts it in `BuildConfig.PRODUCT`), Personales (`skill`), Proyecto (`project`), one for each plugin by its `group` (alphabetical, case aside), `claude.ai` (the group of that name, after the other plugins), Claude; inside a section the names that begin with the letters come first, then by name (the source rank of REQ-416 goes away).
- REQ-796 WHEN letters are typed, THE list SHALL keep the commands whose name holds them (REQ-416) in every section, and a section with none, or that has no command at all, SHALL not be drawn.
- REQ-797 THE counter `n de m` SHALL count commands only, and `↑` and `↓` SHALL move from command to command across sections, never resting on a title, and stop at the first and the last.
- REQ-798 THE terminal client's box SHALL show at most 8 lines, titles included (a title takes a line), and its height SHALL follow the lines there are up to 8; WHEN the first command seen is not the first of its section, THE top line SHALL be its section's title marked `↑`, and the selected command SHALL always be under it.
- REQ-799 WHILE the list is open, `RePág` and `AvPág` SHALL move to the first command of the next section, and to the first of this one (the previous one when already there); in the last section `AvPág` SHALL move to its last command; and `tab` SHALL still complete (REQ-416).
- REQ-800 THE rows SHALL show the name in the colour of its section (dim for a command that only works on Claude's own screen, as before) and the description, and no word of origin; THE titles SHALL be coloured jwn blue, Personales yellow, Proyecto green, plugins cyan, `claude.ai` red and Claude magenta, and THE first word of the line (REQ-459, REQ-596) SHALL use the same colours.
- REQ-801 WHILE the terminal is under 64 columns (REQ-738), THE box SHALL keep its titles and counts, clipped to the width, and clip each description as it does now.
- REQ-802 THE phone's list (REQ-446) SHALL have the same sections, order and filter, a title that stays at the top of the list while its commands scroll, with its count, no word of origin on the rows and the same height as now; a tap on a command SHALL run it and a tap on a title SHALL do nothing.
- REQ-803 THE `/skills` list of both clients (REQ-424, REQ-425) SHALL have the same sections for the commands that are neither jwn's nor Claude's (Personales, Proyecto, plugins, `claude.ai`), under the rules of REQ-796 (the terminal's list is filtered by what is typed in it; the phone's sheet has no filter and keeps all its rows), and no word of origin per row.
- REQ-804 THE list of commands kept in `commands.json` (REQ-593), the history of the composer (REQ-716), the free answer of a question (REQ-743 to REQ-753) and the one-panel-at-a-time rule under 64 columns (REQ-738) SHALL work as they did.
- REQ-805 THE `workspace` node and its summary SHALL carry `chat`, an object `{archived?, active, last?}` (`archived` absent when false; `active` the time, in seconds, of the last item added to its conversation; `last` the text of that item on one line, clipped to 80 characters, absent while there is none), absent from every other workspace, and `kind` SHALL stay `folder`; `active` and `last` SHALL change when a message is sent and when the engine's turn ends, not while a reply streams; THE schema, a fixture of a session with a live and an archived chat, one for each command and the Kotlin types come with N173a.
- REQ-806 THE folder of a chat SHALL be `chats_dir(state)/<id>`, where `chats_dir` is a function of the `identity` crate that takes the daemon's state directory, and no path is written by hand.
- REQ-807 WHEN `chat_new` arrives, THE daemon SHALL make the empty folder, add a workspace with `chat`, no `project`, and the name «conversación nueva», start in it one conversation pane (a new session id, mode `manual`) and reply `{workspace}`; IF the folder cannot be made, THEN the daemon SHALL answer `io` and add nothing.
- REQ-808 WHEN the first message of a chat is sent, THE daemon SHALL name the chat after it, with its whitespace collapsed and cut to 40 characters with `…`, unless the person renamed the chat before.
- REQ-809 WHERE a workspace has `chat`, THE daemon SHALL NOT read its git state, look for its pull request, run a setup or poll its changes, and THE commands `create_worktree` and `open_worktree` (by their `repo`, when it is a chat's folder), `open_diff`, `open_commits`, `sync_workspace`, `start_workspace`, `move_to_group`, `move_repo` and `remove_workspace` SHALL answer `bad_request` for it, and `new_pane` for an archived chat (it has no panel, and `resume_chat` is what opens one) (a chat is closed with `archive_chat` and deleted with `delete_chat`, so that no folder or session id is left behind).
- REQ-810 THE `session` node SHALL list the chats after every other workspace, outside every group and every repository block (amends REQ-219 and REQ-690): the live ones first, the most recently active first, then the archived ones in the same order; THE order of a chat SHALL be its `active`.
- REQ-811 WHEN `archive_chat` arrives for a chat with at least one message, THE daemon SHALL close its pane, keep the folder and the session id, set `archived` and reply ok; IF the chat has no message, THEN THE daemon SHALL remove it as `delete_chat` does and reply `{discarded: true}`; IF the workspace has no `chat`, THEN THE daemon SHALL answer `bad_request`.
- REQ-812 WHEN `resume_chat` arrives for an archived chat, THE daemon SHALL start a conversation pane in its folder with its saved session id, so that the engine starts with `--resume` when the transcript is on disk (REQ-754) and says so when it is not (REQ-755), clear `archived`, make the chat the most recently active and reply `{pane}`.
- REQ-813 WHEN `delete_chat` arrives, THE daemon SHALL close the chat's panes and remove its folder and its workspace and SHALL NOT read, move or delete anything of Claude's own folders; IF the canonical path of the folder is not inside `chats_dir()`, THEN THE daemon SHALL remove nothing and answer `bad_request`.
- REQ-814 THE daemon SHALL keep a chat across a restart as it was (name, `archived`, folder, session id); WHEN it restores a live chat whose transcript is not on disk, THE daemon SHALL open an empty conversation in it and not a plain shell (REQ-52 and REQ-393 restore a pane by `claude --resume` only with a saved transcript).
- REQ-815 WHEN the person renames a chat, THE daemon SHALL keep the new name for good (amends REQ-178, which refuses `rename_workspace` for anything but a worktree: a chat is not a directory of the person's).
- REQ-816 WHEN `promote_chat` arrives for a chat with at least one message and a `to` that is a workspace without `chat`, THE daemon SHALL start a new conversation pane in `to`, leave the chat as it was and reply `{pane, text}`, where `text` is, WHERE `summary` is true, the last six messages of the chat, each cut to 300 characters, for the client to put in the new conversation's field without sending it (what the summary should be is OPEN-62); IF the chat is archived (only a live chat has the messages), has no message or `to` has `chat`, THEN THE daemon SHALL answer `bad_request`.
- REQ-817 THE attention of a chat's pane SHALL be raised, shown and counted in «avisos» as that of any workspace, with no new kind.
- REQ-818 THE terminal client SHALL draw after the workspaces (and «sin grupo») the zone «CONVERSACIONES» with its count of live chats: a row `✦ name` with the mark of its first attention and a spinner while it works, `ninguna · C abre una` when there is none and `archivadas N · A` when some are archived; THE cursor SHALL go through the zone with `j` and `k` as through any row.
- REQ-819 WHEN `C` is pressed on the list, THE terminal client SHALL send `chat_new`, select the chat when the session shows it and, once its panel has its conversation, put the cursor in the field (before that the panel is a terminal that runs `claude` and would take the first keys), asking for no name, folder or branch.
- REQ-820 WHEN `x` is pressed on a chat, THE terminal client SHALL send `archive_chat` and say «archivada · A para reanudar» or, for an empty chat, «vacía: se descartó»; WHEN `r` is pressed on it, THE client SHALL rename it; `↵` SHALL enter it (`l` and `Tab` do not enter a workspace either).
- REQ-821 WHEN `A` is pressed, THE terminal client SHALL list the archived chats, the most recent first: `↵` sends `resume_chat` and enters the chat, `d` asks «y sí · n no» («borra su carpeta; el transcript de Claude queda en ~/.claude/projects/») before `delete_chat`, and `esc` closes the list.
- REQ-822 WHEN `P` is pressed on a chat with at least one message, THE terminal client SHALL list the workspaces and, on `↵`, send `promote_chat` with `summary: true` and open the new conversation, once its conversation is there, with the reply's `text` in its field (and say «el resumen no llegó» when it does not come, never copying it); IF the chat is empty, THEN it SHALL say «nada que llevar: la conversación está vacía» and send nothing.
- REQ-823 WHEN `d`, `g`, `w`, `s`, `p`, `R`, `t`, `J` or `K` is pressed on a chat row, THE terminal client SHALL say «una conversación no tiene diff» (`d`), «…no tiene rama» (`w`, `s`, `R`), «…no va en un grupo» (`g`), «…no tiene proyecto» (`p`) or «…no tiene archivos propios» (`t`), and send nothing.
- REQ-824 WHILE the screen is under 64 columns, THE sidebar SHALL be drawn as at any width, with the zone of the chats in it, and the panes as REQ-738 says (the focused panel under a strip of titles): the chats add nothing to that layout.
- REQ-825 THE conversation of a chat SHALL be the one of a workspace's: the composer that grows and scrolls (REQ-703 to REQ-708), its history (REQ-709 to REQ-718), the free answer «Otra…» (REQ-743 to REQ-753) and the views panel (REQ-719) SHALL work in it as they do there.
- REQ-826 THE phone SHALL draw at the end of Home, after the workspaces, the section «conversaciones · n» with a row for each live chat (its name, its `last` line, the mark of its first attention, a spinner while it works or how long ago since `active`), the row «archivadas · n ›» when some are archived, and `ninguna · el + de arriba abre una` when there is none.
- REQ-827 WHEN the `+` of Home's header is tapped, or its title is long-pressed, THE phone SHALL open the sheet «nuevo» (it was «workspace nuevo», REQ-201) with «abrir una carpeta del PC», «grupo nuevo…» (the sheet of REQ-697) and «conversación nueva».
- REQ-828 WHEN «conversación nueva» is tapped, THE phone SHALL send `chat_new` and, once the session lists the chat as live and its panel has its conversation, open it with the field focused.
- REQ-829 THE phone SHALL NOT draw «＋ grupo nuevo» at the end of Home (amends REQ-697, which gave Home one line more at its end); THE sheet of REQ-698 SHALL keep its «＋ grupo nuevo…».
- REQ-830 THE sheet «⋯» of a chat's header SHALL offer «llevar a un proyecto…» (a list of workspaces, then `promote_chat`) and «archivar»; THE screen «archivadas» SHALL list the archived chats, each with «reanudar» (`resume_chat`) and a delete that asks in a sheet with REQ-821's sentence before `delete_chat`; WHEN `resume_chat` or `promote_chat` answers, THE phone SHALL open the conversation only once the session lists it as live (an archived chat is listed and has no panel), with the field focused and, for a promotion, the reply's `text` in it.
- REQ-831 WHEN a chat row of Home is long-pressed, THE phone SHALL open the chat's own sheet («archivar», «llevar a un proyecto…», «renombrar») and not the workspace's (REQ-227, which offers «mover a un grupo…», REQ-698, for every kind).
- REQ-832 THE phone link SHALL pass `chat_new`, `archive_chat`, `resume_chat`, `delete_chat` and `promote_chat` as it passes the other commands of a workspace.
- REQ-833 THE binary SHALL have `chat new [--json]`, `chat archive <ws>`, `chat resume <ws>`, `chat rm <ws>` and `chat promote <ws> <to> [--no-summary]`, where `<ws>` and `<to>` are resolved as any workspace (id, name, prefix), and `ls` SHALL say `chat` or `chat archivada` where it says the branch and, with `--json`, give the workspace a `chat` object; `chat new` SHALL wait, as `worktree --task` does (REQ-456), for the panel's agent and its conversation before it prints, and an agent that does not come SHALL be no error.
- REQ-834 WHEN `read` or `prompt` names an archived chat, THE binary SHALL answer «archivada: jw chat resume <ws>» and send nothing; THE skill SHALL name `chat new` for an agent that wants another conversation.
- REQ-835 WHEN a panel of a chat is closed (whoever closes it), THE daemon SHALL keep the id of the conversation that panel had in the chat, and WHEN it was the last panel THE daemon SHALL archive the chat as `archive_chat` does (discarding it when nothing was said).
- REQ-836 WHILE the phone shows the workspace screen of a chat, THE header SHALL have `⋯` (the chat's sheet, REQ-830) and no «±», a long press on its title SHALL open the chat's sheet and not the workspace's, and WHEN the chat becomes archived (from this screen, from the terminal, or by closing its last panel) THE phone SHALL leave the screen for Home.
- REQ-837 WHILE the file tree has the keys, WHEN `@` is pressed on a row, THE terminal client SHALL write `@<path> ` (the path, then a space) in the field of the agent of the workspace, which is the conversation of the focused panel, else the first (as REQ-592), and give the keyboard to that field; THE path SHALL be written as the `@` picker writes it (REQ-631: from the folder the conversation runs in, with `../` where it climbs) and a folder's SHALL end in `/`; THE client SHALL send nothing, THE tree SHALL stay shown with its open folders and its row, and THE status line SHALL say that the reference was written and nothing was sent.
- REQ-838 WHERE the workspace has no agent, THE client SHALL copy the text of REQ-837 through OSC 52 and say it in the status line, and THE tree SHALL keep the keys (as REQ-592 and REQ-639b).
