The list folds past four rows and not three because three rows and the line that says «+1» take the room of four. The label says `filas` and not `líneas` as the list's `… N líneas más · ^O` of the tools' output does, because it counts rows as drawn, and has no `^O` because nothing opens a message (OPEN-45).
- RAT-93 **^R, and Enter puts the entry as ↑ would have** (the prototype's list dropped the draft when Enter chose): the key is free in the composer and in the layers over it. Taking the draft and the `i/n` as ↑ does means a mistaken pick is one ↓ away from what was being written.
- RAT-80 **`cd @` goes back to the workspace's folder** (the user, 2026-10-21: «seria mejor volver al worktree que sea un cd @»). The panel's folder follows the shell (REQ-625) and a Claude starts in it (REQ-626), so a person who has `cd`'d into `crates/daemon` and wants a Claude on the whole worktree had to type the path. The shell already holds the answer to `cd`, so the integration, which already has a function for `claude`, has one for `cd`: the daemon gives the folder in an environment variable and the function reads it. `@` is what the `@` picker of the conversation already calls the workspace's files, so `cd @` reads as «to the workspace»; a folder that is really named `@` is `cd ./@`. Not built: the same from the field of a conversation, which could not move a `claude` that runs (its folder is fixed when it starts).
- RAT-47 **The branch is what the person types, with no template** (the user, 2026-10-16: the prefix «should vary with the feature»). A template fixes `feat/` for everything; a `fix/` or a `chore/` would need the settings changed first. Typing the prefix in the name needs no new field in the box, the sheet or the CLI, and `ws-1` with no prefix is a branch of its own; the folder is the last part, so it stays short. Two names with the same last part (`fix/login`, `feat/login`) share a folder, and the check of the folder says so. Fields for the prefix, or chips, were offered and not chosen.
- RAT-46 **A project's settings are the person's before they are the repository's.** The old jw looked in the repository, then in a personal file keyed by the remote; `next/` keeps that order and drops the matching by content (a `match` key) for a name made of the origin, which needs no scan of the folder. Most projects need no setup, and a file in a repository is a thing the team has to agree to, so jwn writes nothing into a repository unless asked (`to: repo`) and asks nothing on the first worktree (the user, 2026-10-16). The personal file's name is made of the origin so that every clone of a repository shares it, and of the path for a repository with no remote.

### Mobile UI (the simple version)
A pane is a zsh (the user's `$SHELL`); claude or nvim run inside it, so the tab shows what runs there and the screen is a terminal. The claude-style conversation view comes when the daemon detects an agent in a pane, not from a role. Design baseline and the thing to approve: `docs/prototypes/mobile.html` (REQ-33 to REQ-38). The mark glyphs are `!` permission, `?` question, `◆` plan, `❯` command done (`✗` on a non-zero exit), `✓` agent done, `…` screen, `⚑` merged, `✗` CI failed; the existing `✻` agent and `⚡` dev stay. Only `shell_done` has a producer in the daemon today.

### Measurements (N5, `jwn bench`)
25 workspaces × 2 panes, real `/bin/sh` shells, release build, Unix socket, one machine (reproduce with `cargo run --release -p jwn -- bench`).

| What | Measured | The brain's model |
|---|---|---|
| level 0 snapshot (session, 25 summaries) | 3.9 KB | about 17 KB |
| level 1 (one workspace) / level 2 (one pane, 24×80) | 0.4 KB / 0.4 KB | |
| every node opened (1 + 25 + 50) | 36 KB | |
| patch: rename, or add a workspace | 0.2 KB / 0.3 KB | about 0.3 KB |
| patch: a command ends with an exit code (3 patches: pane, workspace, session) | 1.3 KB | |
| idle, 76 nodes open | 0 bytes | about 34 KB per 10 min |
| resume check with a current cache | 0.25 KB (request and empty patch) | |
| polling the session snapshot every 10 s for 10 min | 234 KB (resume checks: 15 KB) | about 3 MB |
| command to reply / visible on a second client (p50, p95) | 0.5 ms, 1.6 ms / 0.5 ms, 1.7 ms | |
| keystrokes to the output visible on a second client (p50, p95) | 28 ms, 31 ms (20 ms of it is the coalescing window) | |

Corrections to the model: the level 0 document is smaller than assumed (3.9 KB, because panes are not in it); pushing makes idle cost zero instead of about 34 KB, and a phone that only polls pays 15 KB per 10 minutes with resume checks. The first measurement showed a 4.1 KB patch for adding one workspace because a list that changed length was replaced whole; `diff` now appends and trims (REQ-29, 0.3 KB).
- RAT-94 **Views are not panes.** `ws.panes` drives the PTYs, the blocks' cleanup, `prune_histories` and `pane_count` in `crates/daemon/src/core.rs`, and `Pane.shell` is always the user's shell: a pane with no process would break all of them, and a second list keeps «a pane is a terminal» true.
- RAT-95 **A `views` field, not a new `Layout` variant.** A `t` that a decoder does not know makes serde and kotlinx fail on the whole snapshot (the phone does not read `layout` but it decodes it); a field is additive (nothing is bumped), a variant would make the workspace `v` 2.
- RAT-96 **The daemon places and remembers.** The phone and the terminal client share one layout, and a rule in each client would give two; the client sends only a hint (`beside`, `dir`) because only it knows how many columns it has.
- RAT-97 **The diff at the right edge with half the width.** A diff wants width, and splitting the focused panel of a shell and claude row leaves it about 28 columns (the prototype's selector A); the user's earlier rule that the diff is not full size (REQ-590) stands.
- RAT-98 **One diff, with the views inside; one viewer, with tabs.** The screen is small and the three views are one thing seen three ways; a diff for each view and a panel for each file stay possible with no new contract (`kind` is there).
- RAT-99 **`esc` gives the keys back.** A panel is a panel: `^␣` is the one way out of a shell, and `esc` on a view is the same gesture and no longer a close (`q` and `x` close).
- RAT-100 **The tabs of a viewer are the daemon's, the current tab and the scroll are the client's.** The phone must be able to name `▤ README.md`; which tab is current and how far it is read differ from one screen to another.
- RAT-101 **The client draws the row.** Claude is told that the person can always pick «Other» and that it must not add that option itself (the description of `AskUserQuestion` in Claude Code), and the engine takes any text that is not a label as the answer (`docs/claude-engine.md:65`, tested 2026-10-16): the harness is the one that offers the row, and in jw the harness is the client, so without it the person cannot answer freely. A daemon-made `AskOption` would change the document (`v`) and gain nothing; `ask_lines` adds its own line.
- RAT-102 **`text`, not a new field.** It is already on the command and `key` has no pattern; the old clients keep working for options.
- RAT-103 **`esc` goes back, `^C` interrupts.** The plan's reason already works so (REQ-603, the user 2026-10-19); the hint says it.
- RAT-104 **One rule: «↵ in the field sends».** On the row of «Otra…» `↵` only focuses, so multi keeps «↵ sends what is marked» for the other rows.
- RAT-105 **«texto» between guillemets** tells an own answer from a label on the history line.
- RAT-106 **The field on the row**, not in place of the options as «seguir planeando»: the options and marks stay in sight while writing (the phone does replace them, as the reason's card does).
- RAT-107 **No `jw answer`.** Nothing answers by CLI today (`agent.rs:88-94`) and a question is the person's; a command is a part of its own if wanted.
- RAT-108 **The disk decides the engine's flag, both ways; the in-memory flag is a hint** (the user, 2026-10-22: an agent in the middle of a turn when the daemon restarted answered «continua» with `Error: Session ID b0ada8d0-… is already in use.`, twice, with the pane already at `jw claude --resume <id>` and a 2.5 MB transcript on disk). Claude writes `<id>.jsonl` at the first message, but `has_transcript` was turned on at the end of the first turn (and by `Effect::Resumable`), so a daemon that stopped in between, or an engine that died there, saved `false` for a file that existed, and the next start passed `--session-id` for it. B-28 was closed by design for the restart of a pane (REQ-52, which looks at the disk) and the engine start was never given the same look: the rule was in the spec and not in `launch_of`. The same flag was also false for a `--session-id` the person typed for a session of before (`State::new`). The look is made where the engine starts, not where the conversation is restored, because the file can appear or go in between (a first message, another `claude`) and only the moment of starting is true; it is made with no lock held (a `read_dir` per project folder). When the flag says yes and the file is not there (deleted, or a session id that never had a message, or `claude --resume <id>` typed for an id of nowhere) the choice is `--session-id`, with a note: `--resume` of a file that is not there only makes the engine end with code 1 at every message, and the conversation could never be written to again; starting it under the same id is the one thing that works, and the note says in plain words that what was said before is not in it, which `el motor terminó con código 1` never would. The price is RISK-101: if the look is made in the wrong place the result is «already in use» again, so it uses the folder the engine itself runs with (the environment `claude` was typed in, else the daemon's) and the same function as the restart of a pane (`launch::transcript_exists`), so both agree.
- RAT-109 **A conversation is not told it has a transcript until the disk says so.** Setting the flag from the disk at the start (REQ-755) and not from the engine's first `result` alone keeps the three things that read it in line with the file: the pane's resume (REQ-52), what the node leaves to the transcript when saved (REQ-544) and the argument of the next start.
- RAT-110 **When to ask again: only after the person's `/reload-plugins` or `/reload-skills`.** Tested against the engine (claude 2.1.309, 2026-10-23, in a folder of its own): a skill file made after the engine started is *not* in the answer to `initialize` (122 commands) until the line `/reload-plugins` or `/reload-skills` has run (123, with it); the line runs headless as a turn of `num_turns: 0`, cost 0, one `system/init` and a `result` (the doc said it was terminal-only). Asking when `system/init` differs was rejected: `init` comes at every turn and its `skills` is the engine's own reading, which does not tell that the `commands` changed, so it would ask without need or not ask when it matters (OPEN-56); asking on the line costs one request per reload, which is what the person did to make it change.
- RAT-111 **At the `result` of the reload's turn, not with the line.** The reload happens while the line runs: the same request sent beside it returns the old list. After the `result` nothing is running, so the request meets no turn; and when the person's next message already waits, `initialize` goes first and the message after. Tested the other case too: `initialize` sent three seconds into a turn of 17 seconds was answered in the same instant and the turn ended with its `result`, so the request is harmless in a turn, and asking when the reload ends is only for the answer to be the new one.
- RAT-112 **A failure is silent and keeps the old list.** The list is a help of the box, the person did nothing that asked for it to be shown, and an old list is what they had a minute ago; a note on every failed refresh would be noise (the answer to the first `initialize` already fails silently, REQ-417). A success without `commands` would otherwise leave only jwn's own commands, which is worse than the old list, so it counts as a failure.
- RAT-113 **No port, no page server.** The bridge speaks WebSocket only and refuses any `Origin` (REQ-72); a page server would be a second surface to authenticate, and its authentication would end in a token in the URL, which was tried and thrown out as worse than the channel. Sending the file as text over the authenticated channel keeps REQ-73 and REQ-190 as they are, with the same two paths (REQ-80, 81), and there is nothing to expose while the link is off.
- RAT-114 **A file, copied, versioned.** A folder needs a way to ask the daemon for each path (the WebView can intercept relative paths and ask by the channel, so it is not a port) and many more kinds of file to isolate; it is the next step (OPEN-58). A reference to a file of the worktree would let it change under what the person approved; a copy with its hash makes «approved» mean one set of bytes (REQ-767).
- RAT-115 **WebView, shell and sandbox.** What matters is that the channel and the secret are in Kotlin and the page has no way to them (REQ-775); the sandbox and the policy are a second and third layer for the network and the window. The artifact goes in an attribute of a shell the app writes (REQ-776) because putting the policy inside the artifact's own text fails when the text hides the `<head>` in a comment; a `srcdoc` inherits the policy of the page that holds it. In the prototype, in Chrome, the policy alone also stopped all eight probes; the layers cost almost nothing and do not depend on one mechanism (RISK-113).
- RAT-116 **A menu and an attention, not a screen.** (REQ-791: the other attentions end when the person has seen what raised them; this one is the person's pending task, so only the verdict ends it.) An artifact belongs to a workspace, so its list is in the workspace; that something waits is told as everything else that waits is told (RAT of attention: a line in the row, the bell, «avisos»). A global list was left out because it repeats the bell (OPEN-60). Three header icons plus a fourth would not fit beside the title in 390 px; the menu also takes the other three.
- RAT-117 **The verdict goes to the pane that published.** It is the agent that knows what the artifact was for; sending it elsewhere or to every agent would make one agent act on another's file. When that pane is gone, or is not a conversation, the verdict waits on disk (REQ-770) instead of being pasted into a stranger or typed into a shell.
- RAT-118 **No queue when the link is off.** A verdict made without the link may be about a version that changed; refusing it costs a second tap, a wrong «approved» costs a wrong build.
- RAT-119 **Exit 4.** `jw prompt` already uses 3 for «a question is waiting» (`crates/jw/src/agent.rs:109`); «changes» is not a question and gets its own.
- RAT-120 **Read the origin from the description, not from folders.** Measured, not supposed (`docs/claude-engine.md`): the engine's `initialize` in a private folder listed 124 commands with no origin field, and the marks of REQ-792 cover every one (12 `(user)`, 2 `(project)`, 9 `(claude.ai sync)`, 47 plugin names, 54 `builtin`). A scan of `~/.claude/skills`, `~/.claude/commands` and `<folder>/.claude` would miss what the engine loads from elsewhere (plugins, claude.ai) and make the daemon know Claude's layout, which is not a contract (RISK-101). `group` is a field and not a sixth `source`: both decoders refuse an unknown enum value (no `#[serde(other)]` in Rust, no default in Kotlin), so the whole node would fail on a phone of before.
- RAT-121 **What the user chose with the prototype (`docs/prototypes/command-categories.html`, 2026-10-23), every recommended option:** the order jwn, Personales, Proyecto, plugins, `claude.ai`, Claude (Claude's 50 at the end do not push down what is the person's); an empty category is hidden; fixed categories only for now (OPEN-61); the titles take lines of the 8 (the box keeps its height while moving, REQ-703); `RePág` and `AvPág` to jump, since `tab` completes (REQ-416); `claude.ai` in a section of its own (9 skills, not the person's and not a plugin's); `/skills` with the same sections.
- RAT-122 **A field on a workspace, not a `kind`.** `WorkspaceKind` is closed in Rust and Kotlin, with no `serde(other)`; a `chat` value would fail to read on the phone already installed, on its first chat. An optional `chat` object is ignored by an old client, which draws a folder more under «carpetas» (RISK-118). It also keeps `Pane.workspace` required (`crates/contract/src/lib.rs:936`) and every command of the CLI, which resolves a workspace first, working with no change.
- RAT-123 **An empty folder of its own under the state, not the person's home.** `claude` needs a working directory for its tools, its permissions and where it keeps the session; the home would show the agent every project. `chats_dir()/<id>` is empty, has no git and is the person's to delete.
- RAT-124 **Closing archives.** The engine already starts with `--resume` when the transcript is on disk (N169), so resuming costs nothing and the session id is the only thing to keep; deleting is a choice in the archived list. Deleting the folder does not delete the transcript, which Claude keeps under `~/.claude/projects/`; the sentence says so.
- RAT-125 **Taking to a project does not move the session.** Claude keeps a session by working folder: moving it means copying the transcript to the repository's folder and rewriting its path, and a failure loses the conversation. A new conversation in the project with a summary for the field, which the person edits, loses no thread and risks nothing (the prototype's «mover la sesión» is left out, OPEN-64).
- RAT-126 **A zone, not a group.** Groups are known by the path of their repository (REQ-684); a chat has none, and a group with no path would make «mover a un grupo» half true. The zone is below everything, as the user chose (2026-10-23); with many workspaces it falls below the screen and `C` and `A` work from any row.
- RAT-127 **`manual` permissions and an empty folder** make the agent ask before it touches anything: the mode a conversation already starts in (`conversation.rs:721`); nothing in the start passes another directory or another mode.
- RAT-128 **Creating is one gesture and naming is the first message.** `C` or the `+` creates and focuses; the name «conversación nueva» becomes the first message (the automatic «charla 4» says nothing when the person comes back). «Nuevo» in the phone's `+` joins the folder and the group because those are the three things the person makes from Home; the end-of-Home line for groups would have been the only button there.
- RAT-129 **The tree is where a person already sees the files, so it is where they pick them** (the user, 2026-10-23: «no puedo seleccionar files del sistema con el @ como en Claude Code… podemos hacerlo que se seleccione desde el file tree»). It costs one key because the way a reference reaches the agent's field exists since REQ-592 (the viewer writes `@file#L10-14` there and gives it the keyboard); the tree writes `@path` through the same function and the same rule for the path. What the user chose with the prototype (`docs/prototypes/mention-from-tree.html`, approved 2026-10-23), every recommended option: `@` as the key (the one the field uses); several rows with `space`; the tree stays open and the keyboard goes to the field; folders can be mentioned (`@dir/`, Claude Code does and the agent lists it). One thing the spec settled differently from the prototype's text: the marked rows are written in the order they were marked, not in the tree's order, because a mark under a folder that was closed afterwards has no place in the rows the tree shows.
- RAT-130 **A program started with `-c` is not typed, so the shell marks nothing for it.** The integration reports a command from the prompt's hooks (`preexec` in zsh, the `DEBUG` trap armed by the first prompt in bash, `133;C` in fish), and `-c` has no prompt: no block is opened and what the program prints is thrown away from the blocks (`blocks.rs`, `data`), though it stays on the vt100 screen. With the store integrated from the start (`pty.rs`), the `blocks` node was an empty list under the field «escribe un comando» for as long as the program ran (a `pnpm exec turbo dev` and a `wxt` in jitsubai `feat/dj-migration`; `jw read` showed their screens). The smallest cure is not to integrate at the start when there is a program: the first mark of the shell that takes over (`exec $SHELL -i`, which reaches its prompt when the program ends) integrates the store, as `mark` already does, and until then `view()` is `None`, which a client draws as the screen. `mode_of` is not touched: it reads the foreground only while a block runs, deliberately (REQ-250, REQ-251). The setup of a worktree (`/bin/sh -c` with its own `printf`, only a final `133;D`) and the agent panes started by the daemon (`claude`, which the shell's function turns into the conversation) are also `-c` programs: the first had an empty list too (its `❯` lines are plain text with no `133;C`) and now shows its screen; the second is drawn as its conversation (`tui.rs`, `p.conversation`) before any blocks.
- RAT-131 **Why `jw stop` ends the group of an engine with something pending.** Measured (2.1.309): closing stdin makes Claude answer the pending request itself: the question is rejected («The user doesn't want to proceed…») and, for a plan or a permission, the model retries the same tool until it is killed at the 3 s grace, so the transcript ends in a different place each time and the model spends tokens after the person stopped. Recognising those English texts (option b of the prototype) breaks when Claude rewords them; ending the group leaves the one shape a crash leaves, an unanswered `tool_use`.
- RAT-132 **Why a permission has a record and a question or plan do not.** A question and a plan always wait for a person, so an orphan of that tool is one that waited. A permission and an allowed tool that runs leave identical transcripts, so reading one as the other would ask again for a command that may be running or done. The record is saved forced and removed before the answer is written (REQ-848): a daemon that dies between «Sí» and the next periodic save would otherwise restore a permission for a command that runs.
- RAT-133 **Why the answer is a message and not a `control_response`.** The process that asked is gone; a `control_response` has no one to read it. Measured: after `--resume` the first user message makes Claude settle the orphan itself and take the message (for a question it said it takes the answer from the chat; for a plan it went on with the plan's first step with no second `ExitPlanMode`). The mode comes from the answer because the plan's `Write` asked again when resumed in `plan` (REQ-851).
- RAT-134 **Why one allowance and not «ask twice» or «do not run».** Measured: Claude proposes the same `Bash` again after «sí», with a new `can_use_tool`. The record proves the first call never ran (a command that waited did not run), so allowing the identical call once is what the person already said; any other input is asked as always. The alternatives (a second «Sí»; leaving the tool unexecuted) are in the prototype and were not chosen.
- RAT-135 **Why `lost` is a boolean and not a `ToolState`.** REQ-7 and the contract's rule: additive fields do not bump `v`, and a receiver that knows no `lost` still draws `failed`; a new enum value would break a client that decodes strictly. «sin resultado · pudo terminar» and not «interrumpida»: measured, a `Bash` that ran when the engine was ended finished 20 s later, also after the group kill (its shell is outside the group).

## Risks
- RISK-1 Index-based patch paths break if a client applies a patch to a node at a revision other than `from`. Mitigation: a client that sees a gap drops the node and reopens it for a snapshot.
- RISK-2 Round-trip equality forbids unknown fields inside valid fixtures, so forward-compatibility is tested by one synthetic test (`unknown_fields_are_ignored`), not by fixtures.
- RISK-3 `Attention.rev` assumes `rev` is monotonic per node across restarts; a restart changes `epoch`, so caches and *seen* maps are keyed by `(epoch, id)` and discarded on change.
- RISK-5 Patches are diffed from whole rebuilt documents on every change: simple and always consistent with the snapshot, but O(documents) per change. Fine at 25 workspaces; N5 measures it.
- RISK-6 `jwn stop` ends the daemon with SIGTERM and does not remove the socket or pid file; the next start clears a stale socket, and `stop` only signals a pid while the socket answers.
- RISK-7 `restore` drops a pane whose shell cannot start (say its folder is gone) and saves that, so a transient failure loses the pane; its workspace stays.
- RISK-8 A pane's `cwd` is where its shell started, not where it is now (needs shell integration); `title` is the shell's name until OSC titles are read.
- RISK-9 The loopback TCP listener has no authentication: any local process controls every pane. Development only; the real phone link needs pairing and TLS first.
- RISK-10 The Android live source is a development link (fixed address, no pairing); it has no UI tests, and it was checked by hand on a phone against the terminal client.
- RISK-11 (the mode of a blocks node no longer waits for this look since N62, REQ-250, REQ-251; `running`, the title and `last_program` still do) The program is the foreground process group of the terminal, looked at every 0.7 s with one `ps`; its argv is `ps` output split on spaces (quotes are lost), and a program shorter than the interval can be missed. Unix only.
- RISK-12 Only an agent with a known conversation comes back: one started through `new_pane` (we give it an id) or typed with `--resume <id>`/`--session-id <id>`. A plain `claude` typed at a prompt is shown as running but not resumed. Whether a conversation exists is a look for `<id>.jsonl` under Claude's `projects/` directory, which is Claude's layout, not a contract.
- RISK-17 A pane has one size, and the last client to ask sets it: the terminal client fits the pane to its box when it takes the keyboard, so the phone then draws a screen of that size. Per-client sizes need blocks (the phone draws spans, not the screen) or a screen per client.
- RISK-16 The fish integration (`shell/fish/jwn.fish`) was written on a machine without fish. Its first run (N35, fish 4.0.2 on Linux) passes the shared shell test, with two things found: the test had used bash syntax, and fish 4 marks prompts and command starts itself, with the start before our script can name the command, so the scanner lets a second start mark name the command an empty first one began. A fish older than 4 (no native marks) has not been run; the test skips a shell that is not installed.
- RISK-13 (measured: three blocks, one of 300 lines, are a 9 KB snapshot, and a short command is patches of about 330 and 270 bytes; the cost for a 100-block pane is not measured yet.) The daemon rebuilds and diffs whole documents on each change (RISK-5); a `blocks` node with 100 blocks of 500 rows is large. A command printing continuously changes one block every 20 ms. Measure with the real node (N17) and, if it hurts, diff only the changed block.
- RISK-14 The integration adds hooks to the user's shell (zsh `ZDOTDIR` shim, bash `--rcfile` wrapper, fish `--init-command`) and the user's own rc files must still run. Prompt frameworks (starship, powerlevel10k) also use these hooks; the order may matter.
- RISK-15 A program that redraws in place (progress bars with cursor moves) is rendered by the daemon's vt100, so its rows are the final screen, not every intermediate frame.
- RISK-18 A quick tunnel has no SLA and a new host name each run; a phone that stored the old one reaches the PC only over the private address until it is paired again or `endpoints` arrives. Registered can come about 60 s before DNS resolves (REQ-80 waits for it). Since N134 a tunnel that ends while the daemon runs is replaced (REQ-606), and the connected phones are told its new host; a phone that is away from the private network when the DAEMON restarts has no way to learn the new host and has to be paired again with the QR.
- RISK-19 A lost phone keeps full access until `jwn revoke`: the secret does not expire, and the phone can control every pane (it could read any file the daemon's user can through a shell).
- RISK-20 Anyone on the same network can reach the private-address listener (the pairing token and the person's `y` are what stop them), and the self-signed certificate is trusted only through the QR's `fp`.
- RISK-21 A daemon killed without stopping (SIGKILL, or SIGTERM sent by hand rather than through `jwn stop`) leaves `cloudflared` running until the next `jwn pair` ends it by the pid in `tunnel.pid`; the pid is signalled only while it still looks like a tunnel.
- RISK-22 With a device paired, the daemon listens on the private address whenever it runs (REQ-89), not only while `jwn pair` is open: what stops a stranger on the network is the device secret, the rate limit and the pairing token, as in RISK-20. `jwn pair stop` ends it until the next start. A tunnel host changes at every start, so a phone that is away learns the new one only from `endpoints` while it can still reach the PC.
- RISK-23 A `diff` node is rebuilt whole and diffed on each read (RISK-5), and a file inserted at the start of `files` shifts every index, so one new file can patch as large as the node (up to 2 MiB). Reads happen only while a client watches `uncommitted`, every 2 seconds, and a patch is sent only when the content changed; measured on this repository (N32): General of 203 files is a 1.1 MB snapshot read in 0.2 s, Sin commitear read in 0.09 s; a patch over the tunnel is not measured yet.
- RISK-24 `git diff HEAD` on a huge working tree takes noticeable time every 2 seconds while the node is open; the read runs without the hub lock, so other clients are not held, but the poll can lag behind the interval.
- RISK-25 Untracked files are read from disk by the daemon, not by git: a file being written while it is read can show a partial last line; the next read corrects it.
- RISK-26 The viewer's requests to the daemon (`open_diff` reads git) run in the terminal client's loop and wait up to 20 seconds, so a very large repository freezes the screen, and the live updates of the panes with it, while a view is first read. The node comes back in the next frame; a worker thread would remove the pause.
- RISK-27 `ChangesModel` and the screen decode a `diff` document each time they read it (the node cache keeps documents as JSON), up to three times per redraw for the tabs' counts; a 1 MB diff costs tens of milliseconds per redraw on the phone. A decoded copy kept per revision would remove it.
- RISK-30 `mergedAt` is not read (older `gh` prints a zero time for a pull request never merged); `state` decides. A pull request is looked at with one `gh` call a minute per worktree in use, a use of the person's GitHub quota; many worktrees open make many calls. What was last seen is not saved, so a restarted daemon raises `merged` or `ci_failed` again once for what it finds. A cancelled or skipped run is not a failure, and a check that is still running is not one yet, so a pull request whose only red check was cancelled shows nothing. The shape of `gh`'s JSON was checked against a real open and a real merged pull request of this repository; the commit-status shape (`state`) is from `gh`'s documentation.
- RISK-29 Only an agent the daemon started has hooks: a `claude` typed at a prompt has none and raises no attention (it also has no conversation id for a restart, RISK-12). Of the events, `UserPromptSubmit`, `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse` and `Stop` were seen firing with a real claude (2.1.299) through the daemon, and `agent_done` reached the pane; the `Notification` types and the tool names `AskUserQuestion` and `ExitPlanMode` are taken from Claude's documentation and were not triggered live. `PostToolUse` runs `jwn hook` after every tool, one process each.
- RISK-28 (closed by N39 and N41) The terminal client closes panes (`x`) and so does the phone (the `×` of its tab).
- RISK-31 A worktree seen clean may get changes before `remove_workspace` arrives; the daemon does not force, so git refuses and the client shows its message (REQ-146), which is the only guard. The sixteen named colours a command prints (palette indices 0 to 15 in a block's spans) are the theme's; black and bright white turn into the text colour on a light theme. The diff's tints are the ground with a little green or red in it (`look.rs`), not table entries, so a theme cannot get them wrong. The terminal client asks the machine for its appearance with one command (`defaults read -g AppleInterfaceStyle` on macOS); on Linux it cannot tell and stays dark, and a terminal that is light while the machine is dark is the person's to fix with the mode.
- RISK-32 The QR of the real link is large: with the tunnel and the private address the link is about 170 characters, a code of 57 modules, 33 terminal lines and 61 columns with its quiet zone, and the box needs more beside it. A terminal smaller than that shows the link without the QR (REQ-160), and the prototype's QR is only a drawing. Each `m` that finds the link off makes a new one-use token; the box of a link already on shows the token it had.
- RISK-33 (resolved by N52) The phone offered a worktree of any folder and showed git's refusal for one that is not a repository. `project` now tells which workspaces are repositories (REQ-222, REQ-225); a repository's `.git` is looked for on each rebuild of the documents (one stat per folder workspace), and a folder that gains or loses its `.git` while open changes group when the documents are next built.
- RISK-34 The keyboard's rules (REQ-236, REQ-237) have no test and a text field added without `keepsKeyboard()` loses its own focus on the tap that focuses it (the root takes the focus away at the end of every tap that nothing claimed). A tap in the middle of a drag-and-drop or a long press also counts as a tap that nothing claimed when it ends without moving.
- RISK-35 An entry's `name` labels the menu only: the pane's title is the foreground program the daemon finds by polling `ps` (RISK-11), so `command = ["sh", "-c", "…"]` shows as its first word and a program that ends at once leaves a plain shell. Carrying the name to the pane would add a field to `new_pane`. A `command` that does not exist is not caught: the shell says so and the pane stays a shell (RAT-7).
- RISK-36 The line of attention is a text of about 20 dp high inside a row 50 dp high, so its tap target is smaller than the 48 dp the platform recommends; a tap just beside it opens the workspace instead of the pane. Avisos, where the whole row opens the pane, is the way to be sure.
- RISK-37 The mode follows the command's first word as the shell reports it, so `cd x && claude`, `FOO=1 nvim` or `time less` are found only by the 0.7 s look (RISK-11) and show as a block for up to that long; and a command that the shell does not report (an integration that failed, REQ-55) never gets the mode from the command at all. The pane's `screen` and the node's `mode` still travel in separate messages, so a client may draw one frame of the pane's older screen on a switch; not seen, only inferred from the order of messages.
- RISK-38 `input` is read from the screen, so a right prompt (`RPROMPT`) drawn on the first row of the line shows in it as text after a gap, a buffer with hard line breaks is read from its last row only (its first row's prompt column does not apply), and a screen that was resized after the prompt ended keeps the old column. The text of a shell that never reports its prompt end is the screen's line, with the prompt in it (REQ-258). The field counts characters and not columns, so wide characters (CJK) wrap late. While a command runs what is typed goes to the command and the field cannot show it.
- RISK-39 Once claude has painted, its screen is the pane's whole screen, and claude draws below what was already there, so the shell's history shows above its interface; the loading only hides the screen while nothing is drawn. Starting with a blank screen would hide it, and is left for a later part if the history is in the way. A program whose first output is only blanks or escape sequences is `starting` until its first text. The phone gives up after 20 s on its own timer (its clock is not the PC's), the terminal by the block's start time.
- RISK-40 A program that leaves the alternate screen for a moment and stays (nvim `:!cmd`, a pager asked to run something) shows «cerrando <program>…» for up to 3 s, and a program that is quick to end (nvim) shows it for a frame or not at all; one that never uses the alternate screen (a program on the normal screen) is never «closing» and its old screen cannot be told apart from its own. The phone still shows the shell's screen in that second.
- RISK-41 A program that is not at a terminal that can take input (a shell at its prompt) receives an answer as typed text, as with a real terminal: `printf '\e[c'` at a prompt puts `^[[?62;22c` on the line, and `cat` of a file that holds queries answers them. The reply to `CSI > q` names the terminal as the product (`jwn(0.0.1)`) and a program that chooses features by that name does not know it. A mode is answered «not recognised» for every number, including those the screen tracks (bracketed paste, the mouse): a program that asks first and then does without loses those.
- RISK-42 The daemon still builds, serialises and compares every node of the tree at each refresh (at most once per 20 ms batch), so a pane of large blocks costs its size at each one, and about 180 MB of the daemon's memory is that pane's blocks held three times (the store, the view and the document). A guess at the front drop that is wrong gives a larger patch and never a wrong one; a list that loses more than 8 items at its front at once is patched item by item as before. The terminal client still holds each document twice (the client's JSON and the parsed node).
- RISK-43 A push may wait for something the daemon cannot give: a password, a passphrase, an unknown host. `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` and the daemon having no terminal make git fail instead, but an SSH agent or a credential helper that asks through a window can wait; the client gives up after 120 seconds and says so, and the push may still finish. It is not guarded further.
- RISK-44 The tree has proportions, not sizes, so two terminal clients of different sizes draw the same arrangement at different cell counts, and which panel is on the left of another is the same for both but «the longer side» (`n`) is each client's own. A pane that shrinks under a few cells still draws a box with no room in it; the minimum is one cell of the split, not of what a shell needs.
- RISK-45 The blocks on disk hold whatever the commands printed, secrets included, for as long as the pane lives: the file is for the person alone (REQ-297) but is not encrypted, and a backup of the state folder takes it. Up to 4 MiB of JSON are serialised under the daemon's lock for each pane that changed, every 2 seconds at most, so a very noisy pane costs that much each time (not measured: `/perf-review` before trusting it with many noisy panes); `jwn stop` ends the daemon with a signal and the daemon does not save once more, so the output of a running command is kept up to the last 2 seconds (the command itself, and every command that started or ended, is kept at once); a block running at the crash is kept as it was at the last save.
- RISK-46 The selection is counted in lines from the first of the list: when the oldest blocks are dropped from the front (the 100-block limit) while it is lit, the same line numbers are other text, and it moves. A terminal that does not report the pointer moving with the button down (it needs the button-event mouse mode) gives no drag, only the click. The terminal's own selection (shift-drag in Ghostty) never reaches this client.
- RISK-47 The marks of a folder cost a `git status --ignored` limited to it, which in a very large working tree may take seconds, and its output is read up to 8 MiB; the tree does not watch the files, so what it shows is what was true when the folder was listed until `r` asks again; a search in a folder that is not a repository looks at up to 100 000 files and no deeper than eight folders.
- RISK-48 `setup` runs shell lines written in a file the repository carries, as the person: a repository whose `.jw.toml` was changed by someone else (a clone, a pull) runs their commands in the next worktree the person makes. The old jw did the same; here the box lists every line before `↵` (REQ-343), and so does the phone's sheet since N79 (REQ-349), which shows only the last folder of the `root`, a folder the person can write that may be anywhere; the phone runs nothing itself. `root`, the layout's commands and the agent's `start` are the file's too.
- RISK-49 A command that writes to a pane writes to whatever is there: `prompt --pane` aimed at a plain shell runs the text as a command, and a text that reaches an agent that is waiting for an answer to a question or a permission answers it. The agent is recognised by the program's name (`claude`, `codex`), so one started through a wrapper (`npx`, a script) is not found until `--pane` names it; `codex` has no hooks and raises no attention. «Quiet for a second» can be a model thinking without moving the screen, and the text then reaches an agent that was not asking; and a pane that animates for 40 seconds makes `prompt` fail where `--now` would have written. The pane's id in the environment is trusted: an agent can only be kept from writing to itself because it says who it is.
- RISK-50 What the engine does for these is *docs* or *unverified*, not tested (`docs/claude-engine.md`): stopping a task while it runs (`stop_task`), the choice Claude asks for and the approval of a plan arriving as `can_use_tool`, the rule it takes back for «for this session» (the daemon keeps its own), images as content blocks and `@` in the stream, the checklist's tools (`TaskCreate`; `TodoWrite` is off by default), `ScheduleWakeup` and cron, the page tool, `PushNotification` and workflows. The first task of each part is to try its own with a real Claude, record it in `docs/claude-engine.md` and only then build on it; where it fails the fallback is in the REQ (an error that says so for stopping a task; the text of the question as a normal message; the checklist and the page absent from the node) and the part says so in Corrections. Tested for N81 with the real engine: the turn, the permission, the interrupt, `set_permission_mode`, `/effort`, the resume.
- RISK-51 Claude Code changes what it says between versions (a tool called `Task` became `Agent`): the daemon ignores a message it does not know and keeps going, takes the version from `init`, and `docs/claude-engine.md` says which version each fact was run on. A test runs a fake engine that speaks the recorded messages, and a manual check against the installed `claude` is part of each part.
- RISK-52 The person's own `~/.claude` runs in the engine: its SessionStart hooks print (4 of them in the author's runs), its MCP servers may need a login, and its settings may allow or forbid tools. The daemon hides what is not the conversation and does not try to log anything in.
- RISK-53 `!command` runs as the person in the pane's folder with no terminal: a program that waits for a key hangs until the 60 seconds are over and is killed; one that needs a terminal (an editor) does not work and the client says so. The pid is the daemon's own and nothing else is ever signalled.
- RISK-54 A shell function `claude` of the person's, an alias or a `PATH` entry of theirs may already stand where the integration's goes. The integration defines its function last, after the person's rc, and only when none of theirs is a function; one that is an alias keeps winning and the conversation is not offered. A shell that is not zsh, bash or fish never gets one.
- RISK-55 `auto` and `accept edits` let Claude edit and run without asking, and the phone can change the mode from a pocket. The mode is shown on the pill and in the footer in a colour of its own, a change is a note in the conversation and `bypassPermissions` and `dontAsk` are refused (REQ-390); whether the phone should ask before it leaves `manual` is open (OPEN-11).
- RISK-56 A turn costs what it costs in Claude's own screen and a conversation that streams large outputs makes large patches: a patch of a text that grows replaces the whole string, so at 20 a second a 48 KB answer sent 2.7 MB to a client (56 times the text) and kept its last 256 patches in memory (10 MB for the daemon; 48 MB for 180 KB); REQ-378 spaces the patches by the length of the text, which sent 0.5 MB and 8 MB, 2.6 MB and 9 MB. Measured 2026-10-16 with a release build and a fake engine (`docs/claude-engine.md` has the real one's costs): CPU while it streams is 1.3 % to 1.4 % of a core, idle 0.1 % with a conversation open (3.5 MB and 0.3 % for `jwn claude`'s plain view), 25 other panes add 0.8 %, a node of 300 items is 13 KB and its daemon 8.9 MB and does not grow past that, and saving a turn costs 4.7 ms of CPU with or without 300 items. The daemon bounds what it keeps (300 items, 40 lines of a tool's output, 2 MiB saved).
- RISK-57 A conversation started in Claude's own screen is not served by the daemon, and resuming one of those headless was not tested: `/resume` lists what is on disk and the first message after it says if the engine could not read it; since N100 what it said is read from the transcript and shown (REQ-540), but resuming one of those with the real engine was tried only with a stand-in.
- RISK-58 `/doctor` and `/insights` ran as long model calls in the tests; they are marked as needing Claude's screen so a person does not start one without knowing.
- RISK-59 `list_worktrees` waits for the `git status` of every worktree and `git::read` has no time limit: a worktree on a network folder or with a lock that stays can hold the answer for ever, and the terminal client, which waits 20 s for it, then shows nothing in the box. Reading the status of a worktree with a limit, or leaving `changes` out when it takes long, is not built.
- RISK-60 A conversation made from its transcript depends on Claude keeping it: Claude Code deletes old transcripts after a time it is set to (`cleanupPeriodDays`), and then the chat of that conversation is gone from jwn too, as it is from `claude --resume`; what jwn kept (its notes and costs) stays, with no chat around it. A copy of the chat would avoid it and goes stale (RAT-52); the user chose the transcript.
- RISK-61 A session that jwn only follows shows no working line (the time and the tokens of the turn that runs, which Claude Code shows with its spinner): the transcript says a turn began and says when it ended, but not how many tokens it has used, and a turn that ended by an interruption says nothing. The tools of a turn are listed one by one, where Claude Code folds them («Ran 2 shell commands»); a prototype of how jwn folds them is not made. (N107b: it does show it now, REQ-565, with a turn that is open for at most 15 minutes of silence.)
- RISK-62 A message sent from a conversation that follows a turn of another `claude` starts jwn's engine with `--resume` on a session that the other one is still writing, and the two write the same transcript. This was so before N107b, and the node says it now (`following`); nothing warns or queues. A warning or a wait for the turn to end is a choice for the person, not built. (N111: it is blocked now, REQ-575; what stays is a `claude` that is opened after jwn already writes the session.)
- RISK-63 Two lines that each make an item and have the same millisecond would lose the one that is older at a boundary between pages: items are asked for by the time of the first one held. None were found in 1,217 item lines of a 437 MB transcript. Older pages hold the items of the transcript and those of jwn's own that its memory still has (`kept()` forgets the ones that leave its window, which was so before N108), and the card of a subagent is the tool that began it.
- RISK-64 The daemon reads a file whose path comes from the engine's messages, which are the engine's and not a client's; the path is accepted only if it has the shape the engine writes (REQ-607), so a task that names another file reads nothing. A line printed by a program is shown as text after its escape sequences are removed; one that fakes the engine's closing line `[exited with code n]` loses it only after the task ended. The 2 second look costs one `seek` and at most 16 KB per running shell or monitor; nothing is read for a conversation with none that runs.
- RISK-65 The phone can read a `.env` or a key inside the workspace, over the tunnel: the listing already shows their names and the pairing already trusts the phone, but contents are new (OPEN-13).
- RISK-66 Wrapping 1 MiB of lines at each frame would stall the terminal client: wrap only the visible window and keep it by width.
- RISK-67 The command runs on the connection's own thread, so a first file of a language (up to 480 ms of regex compile, then up to 300 ms of colour) holds the other commands of that connection, the phone's one among them; so does `open_diff` with git. The common languages are warmed in a thread of their own after the real daemon starts (N138); what that thread costs was not measured.
- RISK-68 A new phone against an old daemon: the daemon answers an unknown command with `bad_request` and no `id` (`server.rs`) and the phone drops it (`Data.kt`), so the call waits for the disconnect; REQ-619's 10 s is the guard. The same drop is true of every command and is a bug of its own, not fixed here. The terminal client's `cmd_within` waits for the whole call, not for each message it reads (`crates/wire/src/client.rs`, tested with a daemon that keeps patching and never replies), so live nodes do not stretch REQ-619's 10 s; before it, a patch every 2 s made the wait endless.
- RISK-69 The grammars the pack embeds have their own licences, and the check of crates (all permissive) did not read them: `docs/third-party.md` points at `two-face`'s acknowledgements, and a release must read them.
- RISK-70 A grammar can backtrack badly on odd input (`fancy-regex`): a `parse_line` cannot be interrupted, so the 300 ms of REQ-623 is checked between lines and a line too long to be kept stops the colour first; a minified file and a 200 KiB single line are tests.
- RISK-71 The syntax set adds about 2.8 MB to the daemon (stripped) and loads in about 1 ms.
- RISK-72 Any program that prints `OSC 633;P;Pwd=` (a `cat` of a crafted file) can move the panel's folder. The daemon takes only an absolute folder that exists, so the worst is a wrong chip and a Claude that starts in a folder the person could reach anyway; the jail of the file viewer and of the tree does not move (REQ-632). A mark while a command runs is not refused: a forged `133;D` would get round that, so it is not built.
- RISK-73 The folder is reported only by a shell started with the integration, so a pane that was running when the daemon was updated keeps its old folder until it is restarted, and a shell that is not zsh, bash or fish never reports (REQ-627). A `cd` inside a subshell or a program that never gives the prompt back is not seen until it does.
- RISK-74 A pane's `cwd` is saved and now moves: a restart begins in it, so a folder that was deleted must fall back to the workspace (REQ-628).
- RISK-75 The lock is held for each `refresh`, which rebuilds every document: measured on 31 workspaces and 93 panes (debug build, no disk write) it takes 3.8 ms. A job costs two, one at its beginning and one at its end, so the daemon is no longer held by the removal; `save()` writes `session.json` inside the refresh, and was not measured apart. If a refresh ever has to be shorter, the write goes to a thread of its own that flushes when the daemon stops; not built.
- RISK-76 `started_at` is the PC's clock and the phone counts the time of a running job from it: a phone whose clock is wrong shows a wrong time (the clients clamp it at zero). A finished job has the daemon's own `duration_ms`.
- RISK-79 The entries of `session.json` that name a key outlive its workspaces on purpose (REQ-685) and grow with each repository ever grouped; only `delete_group` removes them. A person with hundreds of folders sees a larger file, not a slower session. A key (the path of a repository) that moves on disk loses its group: it is a new key.
- RISK-80 The terminal client's selection is an index into `session.workspaces` (`tui.rs` `View.selected`); a group's row is selected too, so the cursor needs a second state and every place that reads the index (j and k, the click, «avisos», the number drawn) is touched. A test of each is part of N164c. The sidebar has no scroll: groups add rows, and the cursor row has to stay on the screen (N164c1 decided: the list moves to keep the cursor row in view, with the cursor at the bottom row once it is past the first screenful; REQ-693).
- RISK-84 A group deleted elsewhere (the phone, the CLI) while this terminal's cursor is on its row: the cursor leaves the row and lands on the workspace `selected` last indexed, which is not remapped by id on a group row. It is a different row than the one above or below the group; the next key moves on from it.
- RISK-85 `Ui::field` is built in six places (the draw, a click, a double click, a drag, a release and the geometry of the wheel) and a shell pane's field in three (the draw, the list's geometry and its scroll), and a click maps a cell to a character with `index_at` on the rows the field kept. If one of them worked out another window than the draw's, a click would land on another character. Mitigation: the window is one pure function of the stored offset, the text, the cursor and the pane's size, every place asks it through the same helper (it needs `ih` for the limit, and all of them have it), only the draw stores; a test scrolls the window, clicks a row of text and checks the character.
- RISK-86 The count `↑N ↓M` is drawn on the row of the chips, which can reach the right end (the folder, the branch, «en cola N», «▣ N»), and a pane that is resized or split while the offset is stored changes the limit. Mitigation: the chips are laid out with the count's room taken away (a chip that does not fit is cut as it is now), and the offset is clamped to `rows − limit` on every frame (REQ-704); tests at 16, 24 and 36 rows, with a branch long enough to meet the count, and a window stored at 12 that is drawn at 8. A pane of fewer than 16 rows now has a limit under five (REQ-703): the list keeps four rows where it kept fewer before.
- RISK-87 A fold is one private-use character in `Composer.chars` (RAT-89). A reader of `chars` that is not told would send or show a placeholder: `text()` (what is sent, copied, searched for a `@` or a `/`) and `select::word_at`. Mitigation: `text()` gives the real text and the readers of `chars` go through one `raw()`/`text()` pair; a test sends a line with two folds and a typed character between them and compares the text, the `folds` and the images, one pastes `U+E000` and gets `U+FFFD`, and one takes a word at a fold.
- RISK-88 A label is as wide as 50 characters and `text_width` is as little as 8 (a split pane), and the six places that build `Ui::field` (RISK-85) now need the width of a character to be the fold's label's. If one of them kept counting characters, a click would land on another character or a label would be split. Mitigation: the width is one function of the character and the width of the row (REQ-711), in `field.rs`, that `wrap`, `row_starts` and `index_at` call; tests at the widths 8, 20 and 80, one clicks on a label and checks which one the cursor is before and which after, and the shell field is drawn as it was (REQ-708).
- RISK-89 THE history writes to disk what is typed and pasted, tokens and passwords that were pasted into a message included, in a file that outlives the conversation. Mitigation: 0600 in a 0700 directory, nothing that starts with a space (the shell's convention for a line that is not kept), nothing over 20 KB (a pasted file or a log), nothing for a conversation with no folder, the images are not kept, only the last 100 stay, and `sent/<key>.json` is a plain file the person can delete. There is no switch to turn it off (OPEN-47).
- RISK-90 THE client keeps the messages as it loaded them, once for each conversation, and a worktree shares the key of its repository, so another conversation of it adds to the file what this one does not see. Mitigation: the daemon is the only writer and appends to what it has on disk, not to the client's copy; a message sent in one conversation appears in another of the same repository, here or in another terminal, only when that one is reopened (OPEN-43). A daemon that does not know `folds` or `private` ignores them (the contract ignores unknown fields) and keeps nothing: a newer client works with an older daemon, and the daemon is restarted after an install to have the history.
- RISK-81 `J` and `K` swap panes at the panel level and reorder at the workspace level; `h` and `l` move between panels and fold at the workspace level. A person who learns one may press the other; the help and the bar say which level each is at (REQ-695).
- RISK-82 A client from before N164 and a daemon that has groups: it draws the list in the daemon's order, grouped by repository as ever, with no group rows, no filter and no «mover a un grupo…». Nothing breaks; the person sees the order change and finds the groups in the new clients. Update them with the daemon. The other way round, a daemon from before N164a (main's build, run beside this one) reads a `session.json` that has groups: the daemon has no `deny_unknown_fields`, so it loads it, ignores the groups and, on its next save, drops them; the person's workspaces are safe, the groups are lost.
- RISK-83 The unfolding that REQ-693 does when a workspace is chosen from «avisos» can surprise a person who folded a group to ignore it; the bar says which group it opened.
- RISK-78 A phone with the app of before N159 and a daemon of 0.0.28: its remove sheet gets `{job}` at once, closes as if the removal went, and a refusal of git (a worktree with changes) is invisible to it, for it has no jobs; the workspace just stays. The terminal client of before N159 against the new daemon says «No se pudo quitar» never: it waits for a reply that is ok at once. Update both with the daemon.
- RISK-77 A worktree that is removed while a pane of it runs a program: git removes the folder under the shell (as before N159) and the workspace goes with its panes when it ends; a job that is cancelled by killing the daemon leaves a folder half taken, which git lists again (`git worktree prune`) and the person sees as a worktree that can be opened (N99).
- RISK-4 The Kotlin conformance suite proves the types and the patch rules, not a live connection; the live flows of the phone are checked by hand against a real daemon (RISK-10).
- RISK-91 The draw loop zips `doc.panes` with the rects of the layout and `focus` is an index (`crates/jw/src/tui.rs`, `panes_area`): making it a loop over the leaves touches focus, `Tab`, `h j k l`, swap and zoom. It is the large part (N167b), and the tests of those keys are its guard.
- RISK-92 A phone of before N167 with a daemon of 0.0.29 ignores `views` and does not see the diff or the viewer; the terminal client and the daemon go together.
- RISK-93 Views are lost when the daemon restarts, while the panes come back.
- RISK-94 Two clients on one viewer: the limit of 8 tabs drops the oldest path, which another client may be showing.
- RISK-95 Under 64 columns the rule of one panel at a time changes every workspace, not only the views.
- RISK-96 The phone's tabs and the terminal client's panels share which views exist and the files of a viewer, not what each shows: a viewer the phone opens is a box that names its files in the terminal client until `↵` reads one, and the phone's UI of the views (N167e) was compiled and linted, not seen on a device.
- RISK-97 (closed by N168a, 2026-10-23) **Multi with text** sends `"Rust, Kotlin, mi texto"` as one string, and the engine had been tested with a text alone only. Tested live with `claude` 2.1.304: it takes it and says to Claude `The user answered: "¿Qué lenguajes?"="Rust, Kotlin, mi lenguaje propio"`; capture `crates/daemon/tests/captures/ask-multi-own-2.1.304.jsonl`, `docs/claude-engine.md`. The fallback (the text alone) is not needed.
- RISK-98 REQ-404's «no other key reaches the field» gets an exception; the tests around `conversation.rs:7130-7229` pin the old rule and change with it.
- RISK-99 (closed by N168d) The plain-terminal handler (`claude.rs:252`) had no test and nobody had read it for this. Read: while an `ask` waits it sends `conversation_answer` for a number or `y` `n` `a` and **drops any other line** (it does not go to `conversation_send`, REQ-395 says it does), and it has no way to mark several options. So «Otra…» there is the number after the last option, and the next line is the words alone (`key` empty), even in a choice of several; `reply_to` is the pure part and has tests.
- RISK-100 The phone's card has never been seen on a device; installing needs the user's OK.
- RISK-101 The look for the transcript is Claude's layout and not a contract (RISK-12): it takes any `projects/*/<id>.jsonl` as the session's, while `claude --resume` looks in the folder of the working directory only, so an id whose file is in another project's folder is resumed and the engine says it found no conversation (the note carries what it said); a configuration folder the daemon does not know (a `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` set where the engine starts and not in the environment it was typed in) is looked in wrongly and brings back «already in use» or the note of REQ-755 for a file that exists.
- RISK-102 The pane's resume command is recorded when a turn ends or the engine starts with a transcript found (REQ-755), not when Claude writes the file at the first message: a daemon that stops in the middle of the first turn of a conversation that never started its engine with a transcript brings the pane back as a plain shell, with the conversation lost for the pane (the case of the user had the resume already recorded). Not changed here.
- RISK-103 A conversation opened with a typed `--session-id <id>` for a session that exists resumes it now (REQ-754), but its chat is read from the transcript only when it was opened with `resume` (`Source::open`), so it shows empty until the pane is restored; the model, the effort and the mode the session ended with are not read for it either. Not changed here.
- RISK-104 Other ways the list stays old, not fixed here: a skill file made in the project, or a plugin installed or updated by another `claude`, while the conversation is open and no reload is sent (the engine's answer is old until one is: tested); a conversation that is not the one that reloaded keeps its list until its own reload or its next engine; a `/reload-*` the engine runs by itself (a hook, a plugin) is not seen.
- RISK-105 `commands.json` is shared by all the conversations of a daemon: the one that reloaded writes it (REQ-758) and a conversation opened afterwards starts from it, but an engine that starts later asks for itself and replaces it, so the file is never more than a first guess.
- RISK-106 The reload's `result` is taken by what the daemon sent as a turn; a `result` that belongs to a turn the engine started on its own (a background task's notice) and ends in between could ask early and give the old list. The engine runs one turn at a time and an own turn is told by `inflight` being none, so only the person's turn counts; not seen to fail.
- RISK-107 **The artifact's JavaScript reaches the channel.** The bridge forwards any command except `mobile_*` (`crates/web/src/bridge.rs:392`), `write` to any pane included, so JavaScript that reached the WebSocket or the secret could type in any terminal of the PC. The frontier is REQ-775: both live in Kotlin and the WebView has no bridge to them. N171d starts by measuring it on a device (`addJavascriptInterface` never called, `blockNetworkLoads`, the interceptor, no file access) with the hostile artifact of the prototype.
- RISK-108 **The artifact imitates the bar, or a stale version is approved.** The bar is native and outside (REQ-778) and the verdict carries the version and hash (REQ-767); an artifact can still ask the person in its own words to «approve», which only the title of the bar and what the person reads can stop.
- RISK-109 **Message size.** `MAX_MESSAGE` is 1 MiB (`crates/web/src/bridge.rs:24`) and the code that enforces it reads from the phone (`crates/web/src/bridge.rs:282`); that it holds from the daemon to the phone was not checked, and JSON escaping can double a text full of quotes. 512 KiB is a proposal: N171a measures the largest reply and lowers the cap (REQ-759, 766) if it does not fit; reading by pieces is OPEN-58.
- RISK-110 **The bridge forbids by prefix, not allows by list.** REQ-774 adds two commands to the forbidden; a command added later and not forbidden is open to the phone. A list of what the phone may send is the fix, outside this part.
- RISK-111 **A copy on the phone.** Artifacts opened are kept in the app's private storage (REQ-783) and a stolen unlocked phone shows them as it shows the rest of the app; REQ-784 covers a revoke the phone learns when it connects, and forgetting a PC; a PC the list of PCs shows as revoked keeps its copies until it is forgotten or connected.
- RISK-112 **A plain pane has no «settled».** `jw prompt` waits for the screen to settle before it pastes (`crates/jw/src/agent.rs:704`); the daemon does not, and text pasted into a pane that runs a command is input to that command. N171b chose the second way out: only a pane with a conversation is told, and a plain pane keeps the verdict with `delivered: none` (REQ-770); `artifact publish --wait` and `artifact wait` still return it, since they read the node. An agent that runs in a terminal and not as a conversation must wait, not be told. Telling it needs the wait ported to the daemon: not done.
- RISK-113 **The prototype measured Chrome, not a WebView.** The probes of the hostile artifact were run in a browser (a sandboxed `iframe` inside another), not in Android's WebView (`loadDataWithBaseURL`, its own policy handling); N171d repeats them there and a probe that passes is a defect of REQ-775 or REQ-776.
- RISK-114 The marks are an undocumented convention of Claude Code, in English, measured only in 2.1.309: if a version changes or translates them, every personal and project skill falls to REQ-793's rule (today's, which has guessed wrong), the `claude.ai` ones to Personales and plugin descriptions keep their `(name) `, and nothing says so. The opt-in test path has a stage, `commands` (`scripts/claude-path.py`), that makes a skill in the throwaway folder and fails when the real engine's list does not give it as the project's (without the mark the fallback says `skill`); the marks of the person's own skills (`(user)`) cannot be tried there without writing in the person's `~/.claude`, so they stand on the captured list (N172a).
- RISK-115 (closed by N172b) `RePág` and `AvPág` were not taken by the list of commands: while it was open they went on to scroll the conversation (`conversation.rs`, the general key match). The list takes them now (REQ-799) and `/help` lists them with the keys; the lists of `/skills` and the others keep `RePág` and `AvPág` as a page of rows.
- RISK-116 A phone of before N172 shows the `claude.ai` skills as plugins, in a flat list with a word per row; the daemon and the clients go together (as RISK-92).
- RISK-117 The phone's sticky titles (N172c) were compiled, linted and tested on the JVM (the order, the sections, the filter), but not seen on a device or an emulator: how a sticky title looks over the rows and on a narrow screen is unchecked (as RISK-100); installing needs the user's OK.
- RISK-118 An old client with a new daemon draws each chat as a folder in «carpetas»; nothing breaks. An old daemon reading a `session.json` with chats ignores `chat` and shows folders named after the first message; on its next save it drops the field and the session id: the conversation is then a folder, resumable by hand with `claude --resume`.
- RISK-119 `delete_chat` removes a directory: it checks that the canonical path is inside `chats_dir()` (no `..`, no symlink out) and has a test with a path outside. It never reads Claude's folders.
- RISK-120 Each live chat holds an engine process; there is no cap (RAT-124: archiving frees it). Many chats open at once cost what many conversations cost today.
- RISK-121 The restore of a pane keeps `claude --resume` only with a transcript (`panes.rs:335`); an empty live chat would come back as a plain shell. REQ-814 changes it for chats, with a test.
- RISK-122 If Claude removes old transcripts, an archived chat resumed later starts a new session with the saved id and says so (REQ-755); the person keeps the folder, not the thread.
- RISK-123 `project` of a folder is its own path when it is a git root (`model.rs:114`); a person who runs `git init` in a chat's folder would make it a repository. `chat` wins: the chat is not grouped by repository and has no git read (REQ-809, REQ-810).
- RISK-124 `t` may close a tree that is shown but does not have the keys (`toggle_tree`, `crates/jw/src/tui.rs`, closes it on a second press): REQ-842 needs it to give the keys instead, and N174a has to check what it does after the leader and amend it with a test. `space` is unused in the tree today (checked in `tree_key`), but a mark of a path that is deleted or renamed before `@` writes a path that no longer exists; the agent says so when it reads it, and nothing is checked.
- RISK-125 A program that never ends never lets its shell show a prompt, so its pane stays its screen for good; that is the draw REQ-844 asks for. A fish that reports `133;C` for the `-c` itself would open a block at once and keep the draw as before. Whether the user's rc runs before the `-c` is not changed by this part: `-i` reads it (`launch.rs`), the integration's own script first for zsh and bash. Not verified for fish.
- RISK-126 Claude is not bound to propose the same call again: it did in the one case measured (haiku). If it does not, the allowance stays until the turn's `result` and is dropped; if it proposes another input, REQ-853 asks as always.
- RISK-127 REQ-856 ends the group with `KILL`; the shell of a running tool is outside it (measured), so a tool that ran at the stop may finish with nobody watching. REQ-858's wording is the answer; not fixed.
- RISK-128 A permission of a subagent (`agent_id`) pending at the stop, a restored multi-question call and a restored `multiSelect` were not measured; REQ-848 records only a permission without `agent_id`, so a subagent's is left to Claude to settle («sin resultado»).
- RISK-129 REQ-846's «nothing after it» also restores an orphan that Claude's own terminal left (the person quit there with a question open). Showing it is accepted; its answer is a message as any other.
- RISK-130 The fixtures of REQ-846 to REQ-859 are written by hand from the measured lines of `docs/claude-engine.md`: the transcripts of the probe were deleted with it. N179a has to check them against a real orphan transcript before it is called done.
- RISK-131 Two saves for each permission (REQ-848) write the whole file; the file has no items when the transcript exists (`engine.rs:1497-1506`), so it is small, but a conversation of the old format (`legacy`) keeps its items up to 2 MiB.

## Open questions
- OPEN-28 (N160) The panel that had the focus is not restored: `open` clamps the focus against a workspace document that the client asks for only once the workspace is open, so the recorded index would always clamp to 0. It would need a second pending step.
- OPEN-29 (N160) There is one file per identity, so two terminal clients overwrite each other: the last one to open a workspace wins.
- OPEN-30 (N160) The fallback (no recorded workspace, or it is gone) highlights the first workspace and does not open it, as before. A workspace that is closed with `esc` stays the recorded one: the next start opens it again.
- OPEN-31 (N160) The phone's `ClientState.last` is in memory only, so the phone forgets where it was; not part of N160.
- OPEN-26 (N159b) Two details of the prototype of the terminal client are not drawn: the lines `⠋ quitar <nombre>… 3,4 s` that the open workspace's panel shows for its own jobs, and `esc` stopping the job selected in the tab (it closes the box, as the bar's hint says; `c` stops the job). The row, the bar's mark and the tab say the same things.
- OPEN-27 (N159c) The prototype of the phone has a bar at the bottom (`⠋ 1`, a tap opens the jobs); the app has no such bar, so the mark is on the bell and the spinner of the row, and a removal's notice is a line over Home (the app has no toast). The phone cannot `force` a removal: a failed removal says that the terminal of the PC does it. A notice of a removal that ends is said only while Home is on screen.
- OPEN-32 (N161) A word is what spaces bound (the composer's rule), so `feat/login-form` is one word and erasing it takes the whole branch name. Readline's rule (letters and digits) or a path's (stop at `/`) would suit the branch and folder fields better and the composer worse; left as the composer's, one rule for all. Reconsider if the branch field annoys.
- OPEN-33 (N161) The line of `jw claude` has no test beyond `erase.rs` (it needs a daemon). What Ghostty sends was captured for option-backspace, ctrl-backspace and shift-enter under the flags 1, 3, 5, 8, 9, 13 and 31 (RAT-83); other terminals were not.
- OPEN-34 (N161) With the flag `disambiguate` option-backspace reaches the client as a plain `DEL` in Ghostty with option composing (RAT-83), and nothing in the client can make a word of it. Left to the terminal: `keybind = option+backspace=text:\x1b\x7f` in Ghostty sends `esc DEL` as text whatever the flags are and leaves option composing for every other key. Not done in the client: not asking for the flag (loses shift-enter as a key of its own) and asking for `report all keys` (changes every typed key and the composed characters); the second would need a test of `ñ` and the accents on a device first.
- OPEN-35 (N162) Home, End and ctrl-a, ctrl-e go to the start and the end of the line up to the `\n`, not of the visible row; with a long wrapped line they cross several rows. Left as they were (readline's); the arrows are the ones that follow rows.
- OPEN-36 (N162) The cursor keeps the column of the row it leaves, not the column it had when the person began to move (a short row in the middle loses it). Left as it was; a remembered column is one more field in the editor.
- OPEN-37 (N163) Whether the terminal client keeps which groups are folded between runs of `jw tui` (REQ-696 keeps it in memory: a restart unfolds all); the phone does (REQ-700). A file of the client's own, like `settings.json`, would do it; decide when the restart is missed.
- OPEN-38 (N163) The view of a group as a table of its agents (the prototype's «IDEA»: what each agent does, what it waits for, its PR and CI, how long ago), read-only, from nodes the clients already have. Not in N164; its key is undecided. Decide when there are groups with an agent in each.
- OPEN-39 (N163) The numbers drawn at the left of a workspace row are labels (REQ-693): none jumps by number today and N164 adds none. The prototype's first version had the digits jump; it was taken out.
- OPEN-40 (N163) A repository split between groups (one worktree in a group of its own): the prototype has it as an option, «por workspace»; REQ-685 says no. Reopen if a person needs a worktree apart from its repository.
- OPEN-41 (N165, closed by N165e) The field of a shell pane had the same defect and kept five rows; it has the limit, the window, the bar and the wheel of the composer (REQ-708).
- OPEN-42 (N165) The wheel moves the window one row a notch and the list three (`SCROLL_STEP`); with a window of 8 to 12 rows three would cross most of it. One row is the default; revisit if it feels slow in Ghostty, which sends several events for a gesture.
- OPEN-43 (N166) The prototype's list «Para decidir» was seen by the user, who said «perfecto» without answering point by point (2026-10-22): the defaults are its recommendations (fold at 5 rows or 300 characters, a label with its beginning, ↑ recalls, a file by repository of the last 100 and 20 KB, ^R, the list folded to three rows, the phone as it is). Changed in the spec: the list folds past four rows (RAT-92), `↑ i/n` is a chip and not at the right end (REQ-715), ^R's Enter keeps the draft (RAT-93), ↓ with nothing recalled scrolls the list as it did (REQ-714). A second terminal's messages arrive when the client is reopened (RISK-90); loading them each time ↑ starts a recall is one command more.
- OPEN-44 (N166) `[Imagen#n]` is plain characters and its counter is not reset by a send, while a fold is one character numbered by message. Making the image one too is a change of the same shape (a private-use character and a table), not in N166; decide when an image is erased by mistake.
- OPEN-45 (N166) A message folded in the list cannot be opened there (no `^O`, no click); a drag copies the rows that are drawn, and the item's own copy (`c` on a picked item) takes the whole text. The whole text also comes back with ↑ or ^R. Not in N166; decide when it is missed.
- OPEN-46 (N166) What is edited in a recalled message is dropped when ↑ or ↓ leaves it (the draft is the only text that is kept). Keeping each message's edits until the next send is the other behaviour.
- OPEN-47 (N166) There is no switch to turn off the history file. If one is wanted, it is a key of the personal configuration (`projects/<key>.toml`) that the daemon reads when it appends.
- OPEN-48 (N166) The phone neither folds nor recalls (REQ-718). `conversation_history` is the door; a send from the phone already reaches the daemon's file.
- OPEN-49 (N166) The list of ^R filters the whole text of every message (up to 100 of 20 KB, lower-cased at each frame while it is open), and its filter also matches the `N líneas` at the right of a row. Cutting the text of a row would make the filter search only its beginning; not done. Decide if the list is slow with a pasted log in the history.
- OPEN-22 (N159) A push that `cancel` stops is a job that `failed` with `detail` `cancelado`, so it shows `✗` and counts in the mark of the bar; a state `cancelled` would say it better and would be one more value for every client. Left as `failed`; reconsider if it annoys.
- OPEN-23 (N159) The text of a job that ended well is the client's (`quitado <nombre> · 4,2 s`, `subido <rama>`), not the daemon's: the daemon says kind, name and duration only. A job of a kind a client does not know shows its kind as it is.
- OPEN-24 (N159) A push with nothing to push is no job (REQ-652 begins it at the push): the person is told as before. Whether `check_workspace` and the other reads should show as jobs is not decided (REQ-654 says they do not).
- OPEN-25 (N159) A phone shows the time of a running job from the PC's clock (RISK-76): the `hello` could carry the daemon's time so the phone can correct it; not built.
